# AGENTS.md

_Last updated: 2026-11-09_
_Last updated: 2026-11-10_
_Last updated: 2026-11-11_
_Last updated: 2026-11-12_

SparkyFitness Mobile is a React Native 0.86 + Expo SDK 57 app for syncing Apple Health / Health Connect data with the SparkyFitness backend, tracking nutrition, hydration, fasting, measurements, exercise, saved foods, meal templates, custom exercises, workout presets, iOS / Android widgets, the active workout HUD, and the Sparky AI chat.

This is the package guide for `SparkyFitnessMobile/`. Work from this directory for mobile implementation and validation. If a task crosses into the backend, frontend, or `shared/`, read that package guide too before editing outside mobile.

## Scope And Style

- TypeScript is strict. Keep changes type-safe and compiling cleanly.
- Keep this guide to rules and short pointers. A feature note longer than a few lines goes in `agent-docs/<section>.md` (this package's folder, one file per section of this guide) under its own heading, with a one-line link here.
- Prefer small, direct changes that fit the existing screen, hook, and service boundaries.
- For ambiguous bugs, prove which layer is failing before patching. One narrow diagnostic check beats speculative edits across multiple layers.
- Do not replace a working implementation with a rewrite unless the requester explicitly approves that direction.
- When asked to plan work, confirm scope with clarifying questions before exploring code or drafting the plan.
- Run scripts from `SparkyFitnessMobile/`, except root package operations such as `pnpm install` for patched dependencies.
- Treat `android/` and `ios/` as generated output when possible. Edit `app.config.ts`, `plugins/`, `targets/`, JS/TS sources, or patch files first, then regenerate with prebuild when needed.

## Stack And Imports

- Primary stack: React 19.2, React Native 0.86, Expo SDK 57, TypeScript 6, React Navigation 7, TanStack Query 5, Uniwind / TailwindCSS v4, Reanimated 4, Skia, Victory Native, Expo Background Task / Task Manager / Notifications, Zustand, assistant-ui + AI SDK (chat).
- `@/*` maps to this package and `@workspace/shared` maps to `../shared/src/index.ts`.
- Prefer `@workspace/shared` schemas, constants, date/timezone helpers, and types over local duplicates.
- The app talks to the backend under `/api`; health uploads go to `POST /api/health-data`.
- Global `fetch` is Expo's WinterCG `expo/fetch`, so React Native's `{uri, name, type}` FormData file parts throw "Unsupported FormDataPart implementation". Append an `expo-file-system` `File` (it implements Blob) for multipart uploads; see `pregnancyPhotosApi.ts`.
- Server-stored distance/weight units are metric. UI conversion belongs in mobile helpers such as `unitConversions.ts`.
- Energy is stored and sent in kcal everywhere; the account's `energy_unit` (kcal or kJ, picked on the web or in Calorie Settings) only changes display and entry. Read it with `useEnergyUnit()` (Jest mocks it to `'kcal'` in `jest.setup.js`), show values with `roundedEnergy` / `formatEnergyNumber` / `energyUnitLabel` (`utils/energyUnit.ts`), and enter energy with `EnergyFormInput` (`kcalValue` / `onChangeKcal`), or `useEnergyInputText` for another input element, so form state stays kcal and only an edited value is converted. Never hold energy drafts in the display unit: the unit can arrive after the screen mounts. Widget and watch payloads stay kcal and carry `energyUnit`; the Swift, Kotlin and Wear OS code converts at display. When a translated string gains a `{{unit}}` placeholder, rename its key so existing translations go stale instead of failing the i18n audit.

## Localization contract

English (`en`) is the canonical source locale and the deterministic fallback. Feature developers must add/update the English catalog, use semantic static keys, provide explicit English fallback/defaultValue text, use count-based i18next pluralization, use app-locale date/number/unit formatters, and avoid user-facing hardcoded text. Custom, user, and server content stays literal.

Feature developers do not need to know or translate Polish or any future language, and do not need to wait for Weblate. Translators/Weblate own Polish and future translations and linguistic QA. Missing translation content is non-blocking and falls back to English; existing translated content remains structurally validated.

The shipped locale registry is `src/localization/localeRegistry.json`, read through the typed accessors in `src/localization/localeRegistry.ts`. Adding a catalog to Weblate does not ship it. Shipping requires explicit registry enablement plus native/platform support verification. RN catalogs and native resources are separate surfaces (Expo metadata, Android widget resources, and iOS widget/Live Activity `.lproj` resources), each its own Weblate component and each synced by `.github/workflows/sync-translations.yml`. Only the `en` side of any surface is edited by hand; the i18n audit blocks on registered locales and reports unregistered ones without failing. The workflow pulls the widget resources for registered locales only, because Android and iOS compile whatever resource directories are present and there is no build-time registry check for them.

## Commands

- Timestamped Apple Health and Health Connect glucose readings share the bounded/account-scoped pipeline in `services/shared/heartRateSamplesSync.ts` with heart rate, using an independent cursor. The existing Blood Glucose toggle controls both daily summaries and sample uploads on both platforms; samples are not downsampled.
- Reports → Measurements includes `components/reports/MeasurementTimelineCard.tsx` for timestamped glucose and all numeric custom categories (including non-manual sources), with account-level `default_glucose_unit` (App Settings / web Preferences), native context icons, and independently selectable food, exercise, sleep, medication, stress, caffeine/alcohol and hydration context. Shared context normalization is in `shared/src/glucose/context.ts`; context reads retain each endpoint's permissions.

```bash
pnpm start
pnpm run ios
pnpm run android
pnpm run lint
pnpm run typecheck
pnpm run validate
pnpm exec jest --watchman=false --runInBand
pnpm exec jest --watchman=false --runInBand <test-path>
pnpm run test:coverage -- --watchman=false --runInBand
npx expo prebuild --clean
```

- `pnpm run validate` runs i18n and muscle-figure generation checks, typecheck, lint, i18n audit, watch i18n audit, Knip (`pnpm run knip` for unused files and exports), native locales check, and Prettier check together.
- Use Watchman-disabled Jest commands in agent/sandbox runs; bare Jest often fails on macOS.
- `collectCoverage` is enabled in Jest config, so expect coverage output from normal test runs.
- Run `npx expo prebuild --clean` after native dependency changes, permissions, app group or widget target changes, Expo plugin changes, native config edits, or patching native modules.
- After editing the root `patches/react-native-health-connect@4.1.3.patch`, run `pnpm install` from the repo root, then prebuild from mobile.

## App Shell And Navigation

- `App.tsx` is the root composition point. `App()` wraps `QueryClientProvider`, `KeyboardProvider`, `GestureHandlerRootView`, and `BottomSheetModalProvider`; `AppContent()` owns `NavigationContainer`, `SafeAreaProvider`, navigators, `AddSheet`, auth modals, the embedded/floating active-workout bars, the tab-bar `WhatsNewBanner`, and toasts.
- App-shell logic lives in dedicated hooks that `AppContent` composes: `useAppBootstrap` (`src/hooks/useAppBootstrap.ts`) owns language initialization, initial-route selection, the initial linking-enabled state, and splash hiding; `useAppStartup` (`src/hooks/useAppStartup.ts`) owns the one-time startup services (theme, notifications, notification actions, background sync, HealthKit observers); `useAutoSyncOnOpen` (`src/hooks/useAutoSyncOnOpen.ts`) for cold-start/foreground-return sync and the observer-yield window; `useAddSheetActions` (`src/hooks/useAddSheetActions.ts`) for AddSheet handlers and last-active-tab tracking. Error-boundary-wrapped `Safe*` screen components live in `src/navigation/safeScreens.tsx`.
- The What's New screen (`WhatsNewScreen`) uses coded mockups, not images, and its banner is gated on `WHATS_NEW_CONTENT_VERSION`. Use the `whats-new` skill to update it; the `What's New Drift` workflow keeps an issue open listing features since the last bump.
- Startup initializes theme, haptics, sounds, notification prefs, logs, timezone bootstrap, background sync, pending cache refreshes, fasting/hydration card visibility, and platform health observers.
- Initial route comes from `getActiveServerConfig()` inside `useAppBootstrap`: no active config lands on `Onboarding`; otherwise users enter `Tabs`. A language-initialization failure is logged and never changes the route.
- Deep links are enabled only after startup confirms `Tabs`, so widget links do not bypass first-run onboarding.
- `CompareReport` is a stack route (`SafeCompareReport`, title `screens.compareReport`) opened from the Reports hub.
- Navigation source of truth is `App.tsx` plus `src/types/navigation.ts`; update both and the linking config when routes change.
- Root stack uses `@react-navigation/native-stack`. Tabs use `@react-navigation/bottom-tabs`.
- Tabs are `Dashboard`, `Diary`, `Add`, `Library`, and `Settings`. `Add` is a center action in `CustomTabBar`, not a content screen.
- Native iOS Liquid Glass tabs use `@bottom-tabs/react-navigation` in `src/components/TabsLayout.tsx`; each content tab is wrapped in its own `createNativeStackNavigator` so the tab path still gets native headers. The `Add` tab uses `role: 'search'`, which the iOS 26 SDK draws as the detached round button. The iOS 27 SDK no longer does, so `patches/react-native-bottom-tabs@1.4.0.patch` (a pnpm patch) leaves the role off on iOS 27+ and sets the tab controller's `prominentTabIdentifier` (the iOS 27 API for a separated trailing tab) to that tab instead. Drop the patch once the library does this itself.
- `TabsLayout` switches at runtime via `useNativeIOSTabsActive()` (`services/nativeTabBarPreference.ts`): native tabs require iOS 26+ Liquid Glass support AND the user's `liquidGlassTabBarEnabled` preference (opt-in). Everything else renders `CustomTabBar` (`TAB_BAR_HEIGHT = 56`); `ActiveWorkoutBar` reads the native tab-bar height instead when native tabs are active.
- When adding a root-stack screen, add the route to `RootStackParamList` and register a matching `<Stack.Screen>` in `App.tsx` with `createStackScreenOptions(...)` or equivalent explicit iOS native-stack header options.
- Native header option/button builders live in `utils/nativeHeaderItems.ts` (`createIOSNativeHeaderOptions`, `createIOSSmallNativeHeaderOptions`, text/icon button items); header action colors come from `useHeaderActionColors`, which is Liquid-Glass-aware on iOS.
- Root-stack screens with a screen-owned React header are automatically checked by `__tests__/navigation/nativeHeaderContract.test.ts`; do not add screen-specific native-header allowlists.
- Root-stack screens with a screen-owned React header and a real back button must set `headerBackTitle` or `headerBackButtonDisplayMode: 'minimal'` in `App.tsx` so iOS back labels stay explicit or intentionally hidden; close/cancel modal headers do not need either option.
- Declare screen headers with `useScreenHeader(config)` (`src/hooks/useScreenHeader.tsx`). One declarative descriptor (`title`/`nativeTitle`, `left`/`right` items of kind `back`/`dismiss`/`text`/`icon`/`primary`/`menu`, `busy`/`disabled`, `animateKey` for view↔edit cross-fades) renders both paths: on the native path it mirrors items into `unstable_header{Left,Right}Items` via a layout effect and returns `null`; on the custom path it returns the bar element for the screen to render. A `menu` item is a declarative dropdown (plain actions and/or titled single-select sections, optional accent-dot `showsBadge`): the native path builds a system UIMenu header button, the custom path renders the trigger plus an `AnchoredMenu` under it — one item list drives both, as in `FoodsLibraryScreen`'s ownership filter. Hook screens must not keep hand-rolled header code (no `unstable_header*Items` blocks or custom bars alongside the hook) — the contract test enforces this.
- Path selection is `useNativeIOSHeadersActive()` (`services/nativeTabBarPreference.ts`): always false on Android; on iOS it is true below iOS 26 (classic native headers) and follows the Liquid Glass toggle on iOS 26+, so turning the toggle off swaps in the same screen-owned fallback headers Android renders.
- The custom bar does not reserve the status bar, and Android runs edge to edge: a screen must render the bar the hook returns (never a bare `useScreenHeader({...})` statement) inside a root padded with `insets.top` on every return path that shows it (pushed screens `style={usesNativeHeader ? undefined : { paddingTop: insets.top }}`, as `RoutesScreen`; `presentation: 'modal'` routes on Android only, as `DESIGN.md` explains), or render it through `FormScreenChrome` or `ReportScreenLayout`, which do this. A bare fragment puts the title and buttons under the clock and battery icons. `nativeHeaderContract.test.ts` checks both, per file: it cannot tell which return of a screen is padded, so check each one by hand.
- One-accent rule: exactly one primary header action per screen (`kind: 'primary'` or `role: 'primary'`), enforced with a `__DEV__` throw; primary header save buttons fall back to the localized `t('common.save')` / `t('common.saving')` labels when no `label`/`busyLabel` is supplied. Footer-save forms mark their header Save `placement: 'native-only'` so the custom bar does not duplicate the sticky-footer button. `onPress` handlers dispatch through the hook's internal ref map — do not add per-screen handler-ref effects for native header buttons.
- A **right-slot** `kind: 'primary'` press is wrapped in the shared synchronous duplicate-press guard (`utils/duplicatePress.ts`, same one `FooterSaveBar` uses): presses inside `DUPLICATE_PRESS_WINDOW_MS` collapse to one. `disabled`/`busy` are React state and have not committed when taps queued behind a blocked JS thread replay, so every queued press otherwise ran the handler again (#2191). The guard is deliberately time-based, not a latch on `busy`, because handlers that never report a pending state would leave the button dead. **Left-slot** primaries are exempt — that slot is navigation, and `CycleOnboardingScreen` uses the sugar for a wizard Back where repeated presses are intended. A screen that needs a rapidly repeatable right-slot action must not use `kind: 'primary'`.
- If a root-stack screen is intentionally presented above `Tabs` instead of inside native-tabs mode, document it in `NATIVE_TABS_ROUTE_EXCLUSIONS` in `__tests__/navigation/nativeHeaderContract.test.ts` with a short reason.
- Screens intentionally off the hook (e.g. `FoodSearchScreen`'s bespoke anchored-menu bar) must mirror custom actions with `unstable_header{Left,Right}Items` themselves, hide the screen-owned React header behind `useNativeIOSHeadersActive()` with a guard such as `{!usesNativeHeader && <Header />}`, and gate the `useLayoutEffect` that sets native header items on the same flag; otherwise iOS renders both headers.
- When adding a tab, update `TabParamList`, `NativeTab.Screen`, and `FallbackTab.Screen`; for content tabs also add a tab-local native stack screen using `createIOSNativeHeaderOptions(...)`.
- `__tests__/navigation/nativeHeaderContract.test.ts` enforces this native-header wiring. If it fails, fix the route/type/navigator alignment instead of weakening the test.
- Current stack screens include onboarding/tabs, library/detail/form flows for foods/meals/meal plans/exercises/presets, food entry view/edit, meal type detail and copy, the family diary flows (`FamilyMembers`, `FamilyDiary`, `FamilyMealDetail`, and `FamilyCopyReview`), `EditBarcode`, food search/entry/scan/photo flow, workout/activity add/detail, exercise/preset search, settings subscreens, logs, sync, measurements, the progress photo flow (`ProgressPhotos`, `ProgressPhotoCompare`, and `ProgressPhotoTimelapse`), `ExerciseStatistics` and `CardioSession` (with `ActivityShare`, opened from its Share as image button: a 4:5 `ActivityShareCard` of stats and the GPS route over a picked photo or a transparent background, captured with Skia `makeImageFromView` in `services/activityShareImage.ts` and shared as a PNG; color from `utils/shareColor.ts`), `Shoes`, `ShoeForm` and `PlateInventory`, fasting, mindfulness (`MindfulnessDetail` and `MindfulnessLog`), and `WhatsNew`.
- Current stack screens include onboarding/tabs, library/detail/form flows for foods/meals/meal plans/exercises/presets, food entry view/edit, meal type detail and copy, the family diary flows (`FamilyMembers`, `FamilyDiary`, `FamilyMealDetail`, and `FamilyCopyReview`), `EditBarcode`, food search/entry/scan/photo flow, workout/activity add/detail, exercise/preset search, settings subscreens, logs, sync, measurements, the progress photo flow (`ProgressPhotos`, `ProgressPhotoCompare`, and `ProgressPhotoTimelapse`), `ExerciseStatistics` and `CardioSession` (with `ActivityShare`, opened from its Share as image button: a 4:5 `ActivityShareCard` of stats and the GPS route over a picked photo or a transparent background, captured with Skia `makeImageFromView` in `services/activityShareImage.ts` and shared as a PNG; color from `utils/shareColor.ts`), `Shoes` and `ShoeForm`, fasting, mindfulness (`MindfulnessDetail` and `MindfulnessLog`), `WhatsNew`, and `HeartRateReport` (`HeartRateReportScreen`).
- Quick Actions sheet (AddSheet): see [app-shell-and-navigation.md](agent-docs/app-shell-and-navigation.md#quick-actions-sheet-addsheet).
- Lab results (`LabResults` list, `LabResultDetail` chart + history, `LabResultAdd` modal) are reached from the Library tab and read the `/api/v2/lab-results` tables through `services/api/labResultsApi.ts` (`hooks/useLabResults.ts`: `useLabMarkers`, `useLabHistory`, `useSaveLabResult`, `useDeleteLabResult`; keys `labMarkersQueryKey`, `labLatestQueryKey` and `labHistoryQueryKey`, all invalidated together after a save or delete). A marker is a built-in preset key from `@workspace/shared` (`LAB_MARKER_PRESETS`, shown once it has a result and named through `labs.markers.<key>` by `utils/labMarkerLabels.ts`, never from the stored text) or one of the user's own markers; the screens pass a marker as `builtin:<key>` or `custom:<uuid>` (`LabMarker.id`). Each result keeps its own unit and reference range, so status is judged by the result's range first and the marker's second (`labResultStatus`), several results a day are allowed, and nothing is filtered out of the diary measurements. The add form is prefilled from the marker's latest result, else the preset (the profile's sex picks the range via `labPresetDefaults`). Helpers are in `utils/labResults.ts`. The Dashboard has a toggleable `labResults` card (`components/labs/LabResultsCard.tsx`, registered in `constants/dashboardCards.ts`, `labResultsCardVisible` in the preferences store) that lists the newest results and how many are out of range; it renders nothing until the user has a lab marker.
- `AddSheet` is the Quick Actions sheet: a title with Done, four tinted tiles in one panel (Food, Exercise, Measure, Scan by default; Exercise opens a tinted list of Workout / Activity / Log Workout / Record Activity), a list panel of chevron rows (Daily Check-In, Progress Photos, the cycle row (Wellness, Log Cycle or Log Pregnancy Entry while cycle tracking is on), Symptoms & Health), compact Mood and Mindfulness pairs, and Ask Sparky and Sync Health as buttons at the foot. How each item looks below the tiles is `ADD_MENU_ITEM_ROW_STYLES`, its tile colour `ADD_MENU_ITEM_TINTS` (`constants/addMenuItems.ts`); `AddMenuIconTile` draws the tile for the sheet and its settings. Check-In is the guided check-in: it chains `MoodLog` → `MeasurementsAdd` → `ProgressPhotos` for one day through the optional `guidedCheckIn` route param (`GuidedCheckInParams`; `hooks/useGuidedCheckIn.ts`, `components/GuidedCheckInStepIndicator.tsx`). The handler fixes the day once (the Diary's active date, else the diary store's), and every hop is a `replace` guarded by `isFocused()` and a one-shot latch, so a save that resolves after the user left never moves the stack. Each step shows "Check-in: Step N of 3"; steps 1 and 2 have Skip and "Save & continue", and steps 2 and 3 have a Back in the same bar that replaces the step with the one before (disabled while a save or a photo is pending). Skip and Back ask first when the step has edits: on `MeasurementsAdd` when the form differs from what the day holds (a value typed and cleared again is not an edit), on the mood forms once any field has been touched. Every step saves on its own, so going back drops nothing that was saved, and `MeasurementsAdd` remembers its own writes (`stepSavedRef`) so a retry after a partial failure, Skip or Back still reports the step as saved. "Save & continue" moves on without writing on `MeasurementsAdd` when nothing differs from the day's values and on the one-entry mood form while it is untouched (so an untouched form never saves its default mood); the multi-check-in composer keeps it disabled until the check-in has content, leaving an empty step to Skip. Steps 2 and 3 lock the date (no `CalendarSheet`, so the global diary date is untouched). All three steps are sheets: `ProgressPhotos` is pushed on its own, but its route options in `App.tsx` add `presentation: 'modal'` when `route.params.guidedCheckIn` is set, and then it pads the top inset like a modal (Android only). The steps keep to logging the day: inside the flow `ProgressPhotos` leaves out Compare and Time-lapse (its capped history note no longer points at them) and the Log weight prompts (`PhotoDayWeight` without `onLogWeight` shows only a stored weight), since step 2 logs the weight and those prompts open a `MeasurementsAdd` with a date picker. Step 3 has a Close X and a header Finish, both disabled while a picked photo is processed, uploaded or deleted. Photos save as they are added, so on step 3 the X, Finish, system Back and the iOS swipe down all finish the flow (the last two through a `beforeRemove` listener calling `onNavigatorClose`; while a picked photo is still on its way it waits until the photo has landed or failed): it goes back, shows "Check-in complete" when a step saved something and, after a new mood check-in, makes the one-time reminder offer that `MoodLog` otherwise makes on leaving. Leaving at steps 1-2 (X, system Back, a swipe) keeps what was saved, without the toast or the offer; the step-bar Back never leaves the flow (on step 3 it returns to step 2 carrying whether a photo was saved). A `checkinId` from elsewhere turns the flow off; the flow only edits the check-in its own step 1 created, which it carries as `guidedCheckIn.moodCheckin` (id and the day it was saved on) so going back to step 1 opens it for editing instead of adding another (no Delete there, an unchanged edit moves on without writing, and a check-in deleted meanwhile is replaced by a new one, decided only once the day's list has finished loading or refreshing, since a list cached before step 1 created the check-in lacks it; a failed load shows Retry rather than a Back that would close the flow). On an older one-entry server step 1 saves over the day's mood, as the standalone form does. Each hop also records the check-in in progress (day, step to open, flags) in `stores/guidedCheckInStore.ts`, a device-local persisted store cleared when step 3 finishes and on an account or server switch (`useAuth`'s identity-change reset); Check-In in the Add menu, and the What's New check-in card's "Start a check-in" for the device's today (`getTodayDate`), both go through `startGuidedCheckIn`, which asks "Continue your check-in?" when one was left on the same day, with Continue (opens the recorded step) and Start over (clears it; a new mood check-in is added rather than the earlier one edited). Its Mood row opens `MoodLog`, and the Dashboard has a toggleable `mood` card (today's mood, 7-day trend) that opens it (mood faces, tags, a Journal box, and a mood-over-time chart). `useMoodCheckinsSupport` probes `GET /api/mood/date/<today>` with `X-Mood-Model-Version: 2` the first time the `mood` card, `MoodLog` or `MoodDay` mounts, and the answer is cached for the session: an array back means the server takes several check-ins a day and any other answer keeps the one-entry-per-day screen via `POST /api/mood`. A failed probe with nothing known shows a Retry on `MoodLog` and `MoodDay` (`components/mood/MoodSupportCheckFailed`) rather than either form, because the one-entry POST on a v2 server edits the day's latest check-in instead of adding one. On a v2 server the `mood` card shows one mood per day (the average of your own moods, else the Garmin or imported one), its body always opens `MoodDay` (the day's check-ins in time order with the day's averages; tap to edit in `MoodLog`, swipe or long-press to delete, Add check-in for a new one), which replaces itself with `MoodLog` for the day on an older server, and its "+ Log" (shown when no mood is logged) opens `MoodLog` for a new check-in. `MoodLog` takes an optional `checkinId` to edit; a new check-in for today goes on the profile zone's today even when the dashboard passed the device's day (`utils/moodDay.ts` `moodDayInZone`, which `MoodDay` also uses so its list shows that day; its redirect to the one-entry form keeps the requested day); each face writes the shared `moodFaceValue` (Sad 10 to Excited 90, as on the web); every field is optional but at least one is required, the mood can be left unset (tapping the selected face clears it), the time row opens `TimeSheet` and stays on the current time until set, and edit mode has Delete check-in. The mood report and history chart use one value per day. It also has optional 1-5 Energy and Focus taps (`components/mood/RatingSelector`) that are sent only when changed on the screen (absent means keep, `null` means clear); `useMoodRatingsSupport` applies only to the one-entry-per-day form; it hides them when the server's mood rows lack `energy_value` and shows them when there are no rows to tell, and a save whose response lacks the key shows a one-time "update your server" toast. Its Progress Photos row opens `ProgressPhotos` on the diary's active date. Its Mindfulness row opens `MindfulnessLog` (`MindfulnessLogScreen`), a root-stack modal laid out like `MoodLog` and `MeasurementsAdd` (screen header, scrolling form, sticky `FooterSaveBar`, so the navigator owns Android's Back); `MindfulnessDetail` opens the same screen from the Log Mindful Session row on its Practice & Log tab and, with a `session` param, from Edit on its History tab, so it has no log form of its own. Session type names and icons come from `utils/mindfulnessSessionTypes.ts`, shared by the log screen, the detail screen and `MindfulnessCard`; the list is the shared `mindfulnessSessionTypeSchema`, and a stored type it does not have shows as Other and is kept on save. Paced Breathing and the Meditation Timer keep Finish & Save on screen while the save runs, so they go through `useSaveOncePerRun`: a run is saved once (presses are dropped until the practice is reset, taps replayed before that reset renders included), a failed save can be retried, and a save from a run closed while pending does not reset the next one. `MindfulnessLog` goes back after a save only while it is still focused, because it can be opened from a screen other than the tab host. Keep `AddSheet`'s present/dismiss refs intact to avoid Android re-present loops. Android's hardware Back in `AddSheet` does what the sheet's own Back link does: from the Exercise menu it returns to the main menu, from the main menu it closes the sheet.
- Lab results (`LabResults` list, `LabResultDetail` chart + history, `LabResultAdd` modal) are reached from the Library tab and read the `/api/v2/lab-results` tables through `services/api/labResultsApi.ts` (`hooks/useLabResults.ts`: `useLabMarkers`, `useLabHistory`, `useSaveLabResult`, `useDeleteLabResult`, `useDeleteLabMarker`, `useDeleteLabImports`; keys `labMarkersQueryKey`, `labLatestQueryKey` and `labHistoryQueryKey`, all invalidated together after a save or delete). A marker is a built-in preset key from `@workspace/shared` (`LAB_MARKER_PRESETS`, shown once it has a result and named through `labs.markers.<key>` by `utils/labMarkerLabels.ts`, never from the stored text) or one of the user's own markers; the screens pass a marker as `builtin:<key>` or `custom:<uuid>` (`LabMarker.id`). Each result keeps its own unit and reference range, so status is judged by the result's range first and the marker's second (`labResultStatus`), several results a day are allowed, and nothing is filtered out of the diary measurements. The add form is prefilled from the marker's latest result, else the preset (the profile's sex picks the range via `labPresetDefaults`). Helpers are in `utils/labResults.ts`. Reports are imported from `LabReportImport` (modal, opened from the list's Import button): `useScanLabReport` reads a PDF or photo through `/api/v2/lab-reports/scan`, `planLabImport` (`utils/labReportImport.ts`) matches each row to a preset, one of the user's markers or a new marker and flags a duplicate or a unit that differs from earlier results, and `useImportLabReport` saves the whole report in one `POST /api/v2/lab-results/imports` (all or nothing, each result keeping its printed unit and range). The list header menu deletes imported results (`DELETE /imports`), and the detail header menu deletes a marker with all its results. Helpers are in `utils/labResults.ts`. The Dashboard has a toggleable `labResults` card (`components/labs/LabResultsCard.tsx`, registered in `constants/dashboardCards.ts`, `labResultsCardVisible` in the preferences store) that lists the newest results and how many are out of range; it renders nothing until the user has a lab marker.
- Lab results (`LabResults` list, `LabResultDetail` chart + history, `LabResultAdd` modal) are reached from the Library tab and read the `/api/v2/lab-results` tables through `services/api/labResultsApi.ts` (`hooks/useLabResults.ts`: `useLabMarkers`, `useLabHistory`, `useSaveLabResult`, `useDeleteLabResult`, `useDeleteLabMarker`, `useDeleteLabImports`; keys `labMarkersQueryKey`, `labLatestQueryKey` and `labHistoryQueryKey`, all invalidated together after a save or delete). A marker is a built-in preset key from `@workspace/shared` (`LAB_MARKER_PRESETS`, shown once it has a result and named through `labs.markers.<key>` by `utils/labMarkerLabels.ts`, never from the stored text) or one of the user's own markers; the screens pass a marker as `builtin:<key>` or `custom:<uuid>` (`LabMarker.id`). Each result keeps its own unit and reference range, so status is judged by the result's range first and the marker's second (`labResultStatus`), several results a day are allowed, and nothing is filtered out of the diary measurements. The add form is prefilled from the marker's latest result, else the preset (the profile's sex picks the range via `labPresetDefaults`). Helpers are in `utils/labResults.ts`. The Dashboard has a toggleable `labResults` card (`components/labs/LabResultsCard.tsx`, registered in `constants/dashboardCards.ts`, `labResultsCardVisible` in the preferences store) that lists the newest results and how many are out of range; it renders nothing until the user has a lab marker. Importing a report and deleting imported results or a marker: see [app-shell-and-navigation.md](agent-docs/app-shell-and-navigation.md#lab-results-report-import-and-deleting).
- `useNavigationActionGuard` locks navigation-triggering actions while a native-stack transition is running (idle-callback unlock on re-focus, 5s safety release) so double-taps cannot queue duplicate screens; Library create actions use it.
- `ActiveWorkoutBar` is mounted outside normal screen trees, uses the root navigation ref, and hides itself on modal/editor routes such as food search/forms/scan/photo, exercise search, workout/activity add, measurements, mindfulness log, and barcode edit.
- Most screens are wrapped with `withErrorBoundary(...)`; `SettingsScreen` also uses section-level recovery so settings remain reachable.

## Source Map

- `src/components/` - reusable UI, charts, settings rows, custom tab bar, add sheet, workout HUD, form chrome, library rows, diary rows, serving sheets, food/workout editors, fasting UI, writeback UI, and `ui/` primitives.
- `src/components/auth/` - MFA UI shared by onboarding, setup, and reauth.
- `src/components/compare/` - the Compare report's sentence, span control, start state, answer/checks cards and dots/bars/over-time charts, plus the since-a-habit-started pieces (`CompareSinceRows`, `CompareSinceCard`, `CompareSinceChart`); copy comes from `utils/compareCopy.ts` / `utils/compareSinceCopy.ts`, metric labels from `constants/compareMetrics.ts`; the question model (spans, windows, suggested questions, recents helpers) and chart data (paired dots, trend line, over-time and since chart data) come from `@workspace/shared` (`shared/src/compare/question.ts`, `shared/src/compare/chartData.ts`), the since line steps (`sinceLineOptions`, the phone passes its own label formatter) from `shared/src/compare/sinceLine.ts`, and which since answer to give (`sinceVerdictCase`: pick a start, not enough yet and why, changed or no clear change; `sinceAlreadyChanging`) from `shared/src/compare/sinceVerdict.ts`, which `utils/compareSinceCopy.ts` only words.
- `src/components/glp1/` - GLP-1 tracker cards (injection log, site body map, modeled level, pen/vial inventory, titration, daily check-in) used by `Glp1TrackerScreen`; hooks in `useGlp1.ts` (query keys sit under the `medications` root so `invalidateMedicationEntryCaches` refreshes them). Body-map geometry and the contract types come from `@workspace/shared`.
- `src/components/medications/` - dose rows, the medication list row and the adherence section: `AdherenceOverviewCard` (stat tiles, ring, 14-day bars, streak), `MedicationChecklistCard`, `MedicationDoseListCard` (due and as-needed rows over `DoseRow`), `MedicationLogCalendar` (month grid, medication filter, legend, symptom dots; owns its own entries and symptom queries), `MedicationDayLog` (the selected day's entries with edit and delete) and `useMedicationStatusColors` (theme tokens for all of them). The screens are `MedicationAdherenceScreen` (route `MedicationAdherence`, opened from the "Adherence & log" row on `MedicationsListScreen`) and `MedicationEntryEditScreen` (route `MedicationEntryEdit`, a modal that takes the entry as a param). All adherence, streak, checklist and calendar-status maths comes from `@workspace/shared` (`medications/adherence.ts`, shared with the web Medications page); `hooks/useMedicationAdherence.ts` feeds it one entries window ending on the selected day, and `utils/medicationAdherence.ts` holds the mobile-only helpers (as-needed selection, month shifting, edited-time conversion). The `All | Meds | Supplements` filter helpers also live in shared (`medications/subtype.ts`). Every dose change goes through `invalidateMedicationEntryCaches`, which refreshes the diary card, adherence window, month calendar and daily summary together; injection rows are edited and deleted through the injection endpoints (`useUpdateInjection`, `useDeleteInjection`). The calendar's symptom dots read `useSymptomEntries`, need the separate symptoms permission (without it the dots are simply absent), and never show `source: 'cycle'` symptoms.
- `src/screens/` - top-level route destinations: dashboard, diary, family member/diary/meal/copy-review flows, settings, sync, logs, Whats New, fasting, food search/scan/photo, library CRUD flows, workout/activity flows, and measurement entry.
- `src/screens/` - top-level route destinations: dashboard, diary, family member/diary/meal/copy-review flows, settings (`WorkoutSettingsScreen` links to `TreadmillSettingsScreen`), sync, logs, Whats New, fasting, food search/scan/photo, library CRUD flows, workout/activity flows, and measurement entry.
- `src/navigation/` - navigation-level modules such as `safeScreens.tsx`, the error-boundary-wrapped screen components registered in `App.tsx`. (`FoodPhotoFlow` lives in `src/components/`.)
- `src/hooks/` - TanStack Query hooks, auth/connection hooks, library/search/mutation hooks, measurement/water/check-in hooks, fasting hooks, workout form hooks, widget sync, query client, query keys, and cache helpers.
- `src/services/api/` - backend clients. `apiClient.ts` handles normal API auth/proxy headers; `healthDataApi.ts`, `aiSettingsApi.ts`, food-photo estimate, and other raw fetch paths must keep auth, proxy, timeout, and session-expiry behavior aligned.
- `src/services/healthconnect/` - Android Health Connect reads, native aggregation, transformation, enrichment, preferences, and writeback.
- `src/services/healthkit/` - iOS HealthKit reads, statistics aggregation, transformation, background delivery, preferences, and writeback.
- `src/services/shared/` - platform-agnostic health helpers: the `collectHealthData` / `runForegroundSync` engine both orchestrators share, the per-run workout-telemetry budget and its reuse cache, Health Connect error classification, sample downsampling, day aggregation/transformation, preference factories, and permission migration/sets.
- `src/services/` - platform health orchestration, writeback re-exports, background sync, auto-sync coordination, diagnostics, calculations, logging, storage, theme, haptics, sounds, notifications, food photo intro, meal selection, boolean preferences, card visibility, and workout drafts.
- `src/stores/` - Zustand stores, including the persisted active workout/rest timer store.
- `src/utils/` - treadmill correction maths (`treadmillCorrection.ts`, pure and tested; `treadmillCorrectionFormat.ts` for the signed percent text), date helpers, unit conversion, food details, meal nutrition, nutrient display, workout/session helpers, fasting formatting, numeric input, concurrency, sync utilities, duplicate-press guarding, photo estimate error mapping, and rate limiting.
- `src/stores/` - Zustand stores, including the persisted active workout/rest timer store and `offlineQueueStore` (the in-memory mirror of the offline queue).
- `src/services/offlineQueue/` - offline logging: `queueLogic.ts` (pure: failure classification, backoff, ordering, account reconcile, shape guards), `queueStorage.ts` (the one AsyncStorage key, serialised writes, corrupt-data quarantine), `queueRunner.ts` (`submitOrQueue`, the single-flight `drainQueue`, `createDrain` for tests), `overlay.ts` (queued entries laid over cached data), `foodPreview.ts`, `networkMonitor.ts` (expo-network), `queueActions.ts` (cancel, discard, identity change, purge). `services/queryCachePersistence.ts` keeps a bounded copy of the read cache. Design: `docs/src/developer/offline-logging.md`.
- `src/utils/` - date helpers, unit conversion, food details, meal nutrition, nutrient display, workout/session helpers, fasting formatting, numeric input, concurrency, sync utilities, duplicate-press guarding, photo estimate error mapping, and rate limiting.
- `src/constants/` - meal, exercise, fasting, and nutrient metadata.
- NFC tags (#941): a sticker holds only `https://tag.sparkyfitness.com/t/<code>` (`actionTagUrl` / `tagCodeFromUrl` in `@workspace/shared`); the action it runs lives on the server (`action_tags`, owner-only, `/api/v2/action-tags`), which also runs it and drops a double read. `services/nfc/nfcService.ts` is the only code that touches `react-native-nfc-manager` (`readTag`, `writeTag` — it never overwrites a tag that holds other data unless `overwrite: true` — `generateTagCode`); tests drive it through the mock in `jest.setup.js`. A tap arrives as a link: `App.tsx` linking has the `https://tag.sparkyfitness.com` prefix and `ActionTagScan: 't/:code'`, so `ActionTagScanScreen` waits for the server connection, calls `scanActionTag` once per opening and shows each status (`done` goes back with an Undo toast; `needs_confirmation`, `unknown_tag`, `target_missing`, `too_soon`). `ActionTagsScreen` (Settings > NFC tags, shown only when `useNfcSupport` says the phone has NFC, and a Scan button for when a tap does not open the app) and `ActionTagFormScreen` (one setup form offering Water or drink and Food or meal; params `tagId` to edit, `tagCode` to save a tapped unknown tag, `presetAction` (plus `presetLabel`, the food's name) to pre-fill, as the `AddNfcTagRow` on `WaterContainerEditScreen`, `FoodDetailScreen` and `MealDetailScreen` does; Food or meal opens `FoodSearch` with `pickerMode: 'action-tag'`, so foods and meals come from one search and the amount from the logging `FoodEntryAdd` screen, handed back through `services/actionTagSelection.ts`; the server still stores `food` or `meal`; the meal type is By time of day or a fixed one) manage them; a new tag writes the sticker first and saves to the server only if that worked. `services/tagActions.ts` is the per-type switch (describe, refresh caches, undo; food and meal undo deletes the `logged` row the scan returned) and fails to compile for an unhandled type, but never throws at runtime for a type a newer app saved (it shows "Update the app to use this tag"); a new action type also needs a runner in the server's `actionTagService`. Water caches refresh through `hooks/invalidateWaterIntake.ts`, shared with the dashboard buttons. Config: the `react-native-nfc-manager` plugin asks only for the `TAG` reader format; `ios.associatedDomains` and the Android `VIEW` (autoVerify) filter for the tag host are on for production and for a dev build made with `ENABLE_NFC_LINKS=1` in `SparkyFitnessMobile/.env` (only a paid team can sign Associated Domains and NFC; the tag site lists the production app id and the maintainer's dev id, so other dev builds use Scan). The Android `NDEF_DISCOVERED` filter is added by `plugins/withNfcTagIntent.ts`, not by Expo's `intentFilters`, which prefixes every action with `android.intent.action.`. Dev builds are named `DevSparkyFitness` (`plugins/withDevAppName.ts` for the app, `displayName` in the Apple targets' `expo-target.config.js` for the widget, watch and complication) so they sit next to the production app; the project name, slug and bundle ids are unchanged. `pnpm ios:release`, `ios:release:clean`, `android:release` and `android:release:clean` are shortcuts for Release builds on a connected phone. `plugins/withoutPaidTeamEntitlements.ts` (was `withoutPushNotificationEntitlement`) strips the push and NFC entitlements from dev builds, and `withQuickActionLaunchGuard` also drops the tag link and `NDEF_DISCOVERED` from a restored Android activity so a killed app does not log the drink again. The site itself is `SparkyFitnessTag/` at the repo root. After changing NFC config run `npx expo prebuild --clean` and test on a real phone; the tap flow cannot be checked in Jest.
- JS bridges to native modules live in `src/services/` (`CalorieWidgetBridge.ts`, `ExactAlarmBridge.ts`); there is no `src/native/` directory.
- `plugins/`, `targets/widget/`, `targets/android-widget/`, `targets/android-exact-alarm/` - Expo plugins and widget/native extension sources.

## React Query And Local State

- Query setup lives in `src/hooks/queryClient.ts`; keys live in `src/hooks/queryKeys.ts`.
- Default `staleTime` is `Infinity`, so mutations must explicitly invalidate or update affected caches.
- `refreshHealthSyncCache` invalidates daily summary, measurements, sleep (`sleepDayQueryKey` / `sleepRangeQueryKey`), the heart rate report families (including raw `heartRateSamples` and the day timeline's `heartRateDaySleep`), and exercise history after a health sync. Sleep is in that set because a partial observer upload would otherwise sit in the diary until the app is reloaded.
- `useRefetchOnFocus(refetch, enabled)` is the standard focus-refresh hook.
- `useCompare(question, window)` wraps `fetchCompare` on `compareQueryKey` for a day or week question; it is disabled until the question is askable (and for a since question), refetches on focus, and reports `serverTooOld` for a 404. `useCompareSince(question, habitStart, line)` wraps `fetchCompareSince` on `compareSinceQueryKey` (`habitStart` and `line` are null until picked and then left to the server); its `serverTooOld` is a 404 or a 400 whose JSON body names `details.mode` (an older server rejecting `mode=since`), while any other 400, such as a start out of range, is an ordinary error. `compareRecentQuestions` (appPreferencesStore, newest first, max 5) is written by `CompareReportScreen` once a distinct question, since questions included, has an answer.
- `useFoodsLibrary` is an intentional exception with an infinite query, finite stale window, and `resetQueries(...)` refreshes so focus/pull refresh reloads page 1 instead of every cached page.
- Meal mutations invalidate meals, recent meals, search, and details; food entry creation can affect recent meals.
- Exercise/workout preset list/search/detail invalidation belongs in their mutation hooks.
- `useUpsertCheckIn` updates measurement queries and calls `refreshHealthSyncCache(queryClient)`.
- Custom measurement categories are managed in `CustomCategoriesScreen` / `CustomCategoryEditScreen` (Settings > Custom Measurements) through `useCreateCustomCategory` / `useUpdateCustomCategory` / `useDeleteCustomCategory`, which invalidate the category list and every cached custom-entry day because entry rows embed their category.
- **Offline queue**: food diary entries for foods already in the library (`useAddFoodEntry`), single water presses (`useWaterIntakeMutation`) and check-ins (`useUpsertCheckIn`) go through `submitOrQueue`. The write is tried first (10 s budget, shorter when the last connection check failed) and is parked in the queue only when the device reports no connection or the request got no answer or a 401. A 4xx or 5xx keeps the old behaviour (error toast, nothing queued). The id is minted before the first attempt and sent as `client_id`, so the server dedupes a replay. Queued items are never written into the React Query cache: `useDailySummary` and `useMeasurements` lay them over the cached data at read time (`overlay.ts`, `useOfflineOverlay`), and a just-sent item stays overlaid until its refetch settles. A queued write returns `QUEUED_WRITE` (check `isQueuedWrite`) or a `pending:<uuid>` `FoodEntry`; do not invalidate dailySummary for those, since the refetch cannot succeed offline and would flag the query as errored. Diary rows with a `pending:` id show `PendingSyncBadge`, cannot be edited, and deleting one cancels it (`useDeleteFoodEntry`). Edits and deletes of server entries, water `-1`, meals, new or external foods, barcode, photo and exercise are not queued; they surface `NeedsConnectionError`. Add a queueable write by extending the `QueueItem` union in `offlineQueue/types.ts`, `sendQueueItem`, `overlay.ts` and `invalidateOfflineQueueCaches`, plus a server idempotency key if it is not a natural upsert.
- **Persisted read cache**: `queryCachePersistence.ts` stores a bounded, size-capped whitelist (`PERSISTED_ROOTS`: the newest `dailySummary` and `measurements` days, food variants, preferences, profile, water containers, meal types, recent/favourite foods, custom nutrients) under one key per active server config. It is restored once at startup by `useOfflineQueueSync` (restored queries are marked stale), saved on a debounce and when the app backgrounds, and cleared on every identity change. Diary, Dashboard and FoodSearch show cached data when the server cannot be reached instead of the "No server" state; typed food search still needs a connection.
- `useWaterIntakeMutation` fetches `waterContainersQueryKey`, persists the selected container, and optimistically updates `dailySummaryQueryKey(date)`.
- Active-server switches clear React Query state before refetching connection state.
- Error-boundary retry flows call `queryClient.resetQueries()`.
- App-local toggles (appPreferencesStore): see [react-query-and-local-state.md](agent-docs/react-query-and-local-state.md#app-local-toggles-apppreferencesstore).
- App-local toggles live in `stores/appPreferencesStore.ts` (Zustand `persist`, single AsyncStorage key `@SparkyFitness/app-preferences`): haptics, sounds, notifications, hydration/fasting card visibility, Ask Sparky and progress photos card visibility, the Liquid Glass tab bar opt-in, the active-workout metric column, the default rest period (`defaultRestSec`, edited in `WorkoutSettingsScreen`), hydration reminders (`waterReminderEnabled`, `waterReminderIntervalHours`, `waterReminderWindowStart` / `waterReminderWindowEnd`, edited in `NotificationSettingsScreen`), the Health Trends layout (`healthTrendOrder` + `hiddenHealthTrends`, edited in `HealthTrendsSettingsScreen`), the Reports layout (`reportOrder`, `hiddenReports`, `reportDefaultRange`, `hiddenReportSections`, `reportNutrientOrder`, `shownReportNutrients`, edited in `ReportsSettingsScreen`), and the Apple Watch layout (`watchPageOrder` + `hiddenWatchPages`, the Goals page's `watchNutrientOrder` + `shownWatchNutrients`, and `watchSetInputStyle`, all edited in `WatchSettingsScreen`). The treadmill correction is two more device-only preferences, `treadmillCorrectionEnabled` and `treadmillCorrectionPercent` (clamped to ±25 by `setTreadmillCorrectionPercent`; non-React code reads both with `getTreadmillCorrection()`), edited in `TreadmillSettingsScreen`. The Health Trends pair is written through the single `setHealthTrendLayout(order, hiddenKeys)` setter rather than one per field, because one drag changes order and visibility together; a saved order is reconciled against `constants/healthTrends.ts` by `resolveHealthTrendOrder`, so registering a new graph needs no `STORE_VERSION` bump. Consume via selectors (`useAppPreferencesStore((s) => s.hapticsEnabled)`) plus generated setters; non-React code reads current values through helpers like `getDefaultRestSec()`. A legacy-aware storage adapter migrates the old per-key `@HealthConnect:*` values once. These preferences never sync to the server.
- App-local toggles live in `stores/appPreferencesStore.ts` (Zustand `persist`, single AsyncStorage key `@SparkyFitness/app-preferences`): haptics, sounds, notifications, hydration/fasting card visibility, Ask Sparky and progress photos card visibility, the Liquid Glass tab bar opt-in, the guided check-in opt-in (`guidedCheckInEnabled`, edited in `DiarySettingsScreen`, adds the AddSheet's Check-In row), the active-workout metric column, the default rest period (`defaultRestSec`, edited in `WorkoutSettingsScreen`), mood check-in reminders (`checkinRemindersEnabled`, `checkinReminderTimes`, edited in `NotificationSettingsScreen` and offered once after the first check-in), hydration reminders (`waterReminderEnabled`, `waterReminderIntervalHours`, `waterReminderWindowStart` / `waterReminderWindowEnd`, edited in `NotificationSettingsScreen`), the Health Trends layout (`healthTrendOrder` + `hiddenHealthTrends`, plus `healthTrendsLayout` `pager`|`tiles`, up to `MAX_HEALTH_TREND_TILES` chosen `healthTrendTiles` and a per-graph window `healthTrendRanges`, edited in `HealthTrendsSettingsScreen` and the Dashboard's range control; the Dashboard loads each window with its own `useHealthTrends` call, `HealthTrendTiles` draws the tiles from `utils/healthTrendTiles.ts`, and `HealthTrendChart` is the one place a graph key maps to its chart), the Reports layout (`reportOrder`, `hiddenReports`, `reportDefaultRange`, `hiddenReportSections`, `reportNutrientOrder`, `shownReportNutrients`, edited in `ReportsSettingsScreen`), the Compare report's recent questions (`compareRecentQuestions`, newest first, max 5, written by `CompareReportScreen` and read through `knownCompareQuestions`, which drops any a later build no longer knows), and the Apple Watch layout (`watchPageOrder` + `hiddenWatchPages`, the Goals page's `watchNutrientOrder` + `shownWatchNutrients`, and `watchSetInputStyle`, all edited in `WatchSettingsScreen`). both cards are opt-in, hidden by default), the Reports layout (`reportOrder`, `hiddenReports`, `reportDefaultRange`, `hiddenReportSections`, `reportNutrientOrder`, `shownReportNutrients`, edited in `ReportsSettingsScreen`), the Compare report's recent questions (`compareRecentQuestions`, newest first, max 5, written by `CompareReportScreen` and read through `knownCompareQuestions`, which drops any a later build no longer knows), and the Apple Watch layout (`watchPageOrder` + `hiddenWatchPages`, the Goals page's `watchNutrientOrder` + `shownWatchNutrients`, and `watchSetInputStyle`, all edited in `WatchSettingsScreen`). The Health Trends pair is written through the single `setHealthTrendLayout(order, hiddenKeys)` setter rather than one per field, because one drag changes order and visibility together; a saved order is reconciled against `constants/healthTrends.ts` by `resolveHealthTrendOrder`, so registering a new graph needs no `STORE_VERSION` bump. ## Health Sync

- `src/services/healthConnectService.ts` is Android orchestration; `src/services/healthConnectService.ios.ts` is iOS orchestration. They are substantial platform implementations, not thin wrappers.
- Both orchestrators batch metric reads with `runTasksInBatches`, a concurrency of 3, and per-metric timeouts. Preserve timeout and partial-error handling.
- Bootstrap timezone state before sync. `ensureTimezoneBootstrapped(...)` runs at startup and `healthDataApi.ts` enforces it before upload.
- Preserve `record_timezone` and `record_utc_offset_minutes` when available.
- Manual sync, sync-on-open, foreground-return sync, background sync, and iOS observer-triggered sync share coordination logic. Preserve claim/in-flight guards and cooldown recording.
- All-day heart rate: with the Heart Rate toggle on, `runForegroundSync` starts `syncAllDayHeartRate` (`services/shared/heartRateSamplesSync.ts`) after the main upload without waiting for it; it sends minute readings a week at a time to `POST /api/generic-health/samples`, only ever to the account the sync started for. See [health-sync.md](agent-docs/health-sync.md#all-day-heart-rate).
- Health uploads are chunked. Simple measurements use large chunks; sleep sessions use smaller session chunks; exercise/workout records are grouped by source to match server delete-then-insert behavior.
- Sync result objects include `syncErrors`; callers should surface partial failures and avoid advancing `lastSyncedTime` when any metric read failed.
- `backgroundSyncService.ts` uses overlap windows for sessions and day-aligned rolling windows for cumulative metrics and nutrition. Do not collapse those into one naive window.
- On iOS, cumulative metrics should use HealthKit statistics queries, not raw sample summation. Active energy (Active Calories, and the active part of Total Calories) can instead use the Move ring: with the account setting `use_move_ring_active_calories` on (an iPhone-only switch on `CalorieSettingsScreen`; `healthkit/provider.ts` and the Sync screen preview read it through `fetchMoveRingActiveCaloriesSetting` on every read; a failed fetch fails the calorie read so the sync retries, never a remembered answer that could belong to another account), each day uses the ring's calories (`HKActivitySummary`, `activeEnergyByDay`) so it matches the Fitness app, and a day with no ring keeps the statistics sum, because a denied or unanswered activity summary permission reads as no rings rather than an error. The activity summary read is requested with both calorie metrics whether the setting is on or not, so the switch needs no permission step and a new phone already has it.
- On Android, cumulative metrics (`Steps`, `Distance`, `ActiveCaloriesBurned`, `TotalCaloriesBurned`, `FloorsClimbed`) use Health Connect `aggregateGroupByPeriod` once per range. Native source-priority dedup should match Health Connect UI; do not reintroduce JS `Math.max` or source allowlist dedup.
- Android read helpers return `{ records, error }` via `readHealthRecordsDetailed` and `aggregateCumulativeMetricByDayDetailed`; legacy wrappers unwrap only records.
- Android sleep sessions are uploaded with one per night: `postProcessRaw` passes them through `keepMostDetailedSleepSessions` (`services/healthconnect/index.ts`) before the HRV/RHR enrichment, so when two apps wrote the same night (same wake day, overlapping for at least half of the shorter session) only the more detailed session goes up, by the shared `keepMostDetailedSleep` rule (`shared/src/utils/sleepSameNight.ts`: among sessions at least 80% as long as the longest, more stage kinds, then more stages, then the longer session). Every session is sent as `Health Connect`, so the server would otherwise merge both apps' nights into one entry (#2839). A nap that does not overlap the night still goes up. iOS does not do this yet.
- Android exercise sessions are enriched with `aggregateRecord` for active, total, and basal calories plus distance and steps over the session window. Active/total calories start scoped to `dataOrigin`, while basal energy remains unfiltered; `total - basal` is compared as an active-energy candidate. Incomplete or implausible active/total pairs retry without the origin filter so Health Connect can apply source priority. Distance and steps always stay origin-scoped; never infer workout steps from an unfiltered clock-window query.
- iOS exercise-session steps must come from statistics attached directly to the `HKWorkout`. Do not infer workout steps by querying all step samples in the workout's clock window.
- iOS HealthKit locked-device failures surface as database-inaccessible warnings. Do not treat these as successful empty reads.
- `app.config.ts` grants `android.permission.health.READ_HEALTH_DATA_HISTORY` so Android can read data older than 30 days.
- Health Connect permission migrations belong in `services/shared/healthPermissionMigration.ts`, not UI-only state. iOS does not use that version: `refreshEnabledMetricPermissions` asks HealthKit through `hasUnrequestedHealthPermissions` and requests only when an enabled type was never asked about, so bump the version for Android permission changes only.
- Core check-in measurements use `measurementsApi.ts` and `MeasurementsAddScreen`; preserve `upsertCheckIn` omitted-vs-null semantics. `MeasurementsAddScreen` holds measurements only: mindfulness is logged from the Add menu's Mindfulness row and the Dashboard's `MindfulnessCard`, not from this screen.
- "Import Full History" (`ImportHistoryScreen`, reached from `SyncScreen`) is a one-time resumable backfill: `backfillService.ts` walks 30-day day-aligned windows newest-first from start-of-today down to a probe-derived floor (`readEarliestRecord` on both providers), one upload per window, with a per-server checkpoint in `backfillCheckpoint.ts`. It never advances `lastSyncedTime` and never runs writeback; while it runs, `isBackfillRunning()` (autoSyncCoordinator) makes background sync skip. A window whose read times out (`MetricSyncOutcome.timedOut`) is split 30 → 7 → 1 day instead of retried at the same size, and a single day that still times out is retried once with workout telemetry capped at `BACKFILL_DAY_TELEMETRY_BUDGET` (#3339).
- The backfill's metric set is FROZEN in its checkpoint at first run; resume uses the frozen set verbatim and toggle changes require Start Over. Quota exhaustion, locked device, and app-inactive are expected mid-run stops — the checkpoint keeps them resumable.
- Sleeping wrist temperature (iOS only, `SleepingWristTemperature` -> `HKQuantityTypeIdentifierAppleSleepingWristTemperature`, toggle `syncSleepingWristTemperatureEnabled`) syncs as a plain `skin_temperature` record with `site: 'wrist'`, keyed to the sample's end (the wake day, on the sample's `HKTimeZone` when present, as the sleep session is). The server stores it as a `skin_temperature` sample, not a custom measurement. `SleepDetailScreen` reads it through `useSkinTemperature` (`GET /api/generic-health/samples?metric=skin_temperature`, key under `skinTemperatureQueryKeyRoot`, invalidated by `refreshHealthSyncCache`) and `utils/skinTemperature.ts` `nightSkinTemperature`: a source's own `deviation_celsius` (Polar) wins, else the night against the median of the earlier nights (14 days back) from the same source and `site` (the shared `SKIN_TEMPERATURE_SITES`; wrist and body readings are both `healthkit`) once there are `MIN_BASELINE_NIGHTS`. Values stay unrounded until display, after the unit conversion. Skin temperature and the trend charts belong to the day's main sleep (`useSleepDetail` returns `isMainSleep`, by `selectMainSleep`); a nap shows neither and its rows are not tappable. `SleepBiometrics` shows the temperature row and the signed deviation row in the account's `default_temperature_unit` (`useTemperatureUnit` / `temperatureUnitFor` in `utils/temperatureUnit.ts`, shared with the cardio weather line; a server from before that setting falls back to °F with miles, else °C; the setting is edited on the web's Preferences page, as the other units are; conversions come from `@workspace/shared` `convertTemperature` / `convertTemperatureDelta`); Android has no equivalent. Tapping any biometric row opens its 30-night line chart under the row (`SleepBiometricTrendChart`, data from `useSleepBiometricTrend`: the sleep entries' main sleep per night under `sleepRangeQueryKey`, or the skin temperature rows of the viewed night's source only, which `useSkinTemperatureRows` fetches for the 30 nights plus the 14-day baseline so the row and the chart share one request; points sit at their night's offset in the window, so missing nights show as gaps).
- Overnight body temperature (iOS only, `OvernightBodyTemperature`, toggle `syncOvernightBodyTemperatureEnabled`, needs Body Temperature and Sleep read access) is for a ring or watch that writes plain Body Temperature through the night. Body Temperature is stored as one value per day (a daily custom measurement, last reading wins), so a night cannot be recovered from it: `handleOvernightBodyTemperature` in `healthkit/index.ts` reads the sleep sessions from a day before the sync window (so a night that began before it is whole; only nights that ended inside it are sent), reads the raw Body Temperature samples across them and `overnightBodyTemperatures` (`healthkit/overnightBodyTemperature.ts`) emits one pre-aggregated record per wake day with `site: 'body'`, the mean of one source's readings (the one with the most) inside that day's longest sleep, typed `skin_temperature` like the wrist temperature metric, so the server and the Sleep screen need nothing more. A night with no reading inside its sleep gets no record. Both metrics write to the same `healthkit` day row, told apart by `site`; the Sleep screen takes each site's latest reading of the night and prefers one with a deviation. The Sync preview shows the latest night's value (`RAW_FORMATTERS.OvernightBodyTemperature`).

## Health Writeback

- Writeback sends Sparky diary nutrition and hydration back to Apple Health on iOS and Health Connect on Android.
- Platform split: `services/writeback.ios.ts` re-exports `healthkit/writeback.ts`; `services/writeback.ts` re-exports `healthconnect/writeback.ts`.
- `runWriteback()` runs after inbound sync in its own try/catch. Writeback failures must not block inbound sync results.
- Writeback is opt-in per metric and gated on write permissions. Android production permissions include `WRITE_NUTRITION` and `WRITE_HYDRATION`; other write permissions are dev-only.
- Imported health entries are skipped to avoid echo loops. iOS sets the app bundle id as the own-source guard; Android relies on source metadata.
- Per-day content-signature hashing skips unchanged days. Each run deletes prior tracked UUIDs then saves fresh records; failed deletes are retried next run.
- `HealthDataWriteback` on `SyncScreen` owns the remove flow. `BottomSheetPicker` offers all-time purge or date range through `DateRangeSheet`; both call `removeWrittenData(range)` and clear tracking.
- Inbound iOS nutrition sync reads food correlations with a rolling nutrition lookback and upserts by `(source, source_id)` server-side.

## Native Patches

- `expo-camera@57.0.6` is patched via `../patches/expo-camera@57.0.6.patch` and `patchedDependencies` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` ([#3394](https://github.com/CodeWithCJ/SparkyFitness/pull/3394)). Android pause/cleanup unbind only that view's CameraX use cases; a late old-view teardown otherwise closes the replacement camera and leaves a black preview.
- `package.json` includes `expo-camera` in `expo.autolinking.android.buildFromSource` so the precompiled module cannot bypass the patched Kotlin. A native rebuild is required. Remove the camera patch, its `patchedDependencies` entry, and that module's `buildFromSource` entry once an upgraded release scopes pause/cleanup to its own use cases; verify repeated camera restarts and background return with optical scans.
- `react-native-health-connect` is declared as `^4.1.3`; the installed build is patched from the repo root via `patchedDependencies` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`.
- Patch file: `../patches/react-native-health-connect@4.1.3.patch`, one hunk. `PermissionUtils.mapPermissionResult` maps `ExerciseRoute` and `BackgroundAccessPermission` back to JS but not `ReadHealthDataHistory`, even though `parsePermissions` accepts it on the request path. Since `requestPermission` also resolves through `mapPermissionResult`, a granted history permission can never be observed, so `ensureHistoryReadPermission` always returns false and Import Full History believes it is capped at 30 days. Reported upstream as matinzd/react-native-health-connect#276; drop the patch if that lands.
- The older 3.5.3 patch also forced `getTimeRangeFilterLocal` in `getAggregateGroupByPeriodRequest` (DST / local-day grouping) and made `ExerciseRouteResultType` string-valued. Both are gone: 4.1.3 uses the local filter at all 21 sites, and upstream is fixing the route enum in #274 by typing the read path as a string literal union instead — a better fix than ours, so `routeNeedsConsent` keeps accepting both forms and depends on neither declaration.
- `@bacons/apple-targets@4.0.7` is patched via `../patches/@bacons__apple-targets@4.0.7.patch`, fixing two upstream bugs. First, its xcode pass matched "its" extension target by type with a fall-back to any same-type target, which adopted and corrupted the expo-widgets `ExpoWidgetsTarget` on a clean prebuild; the patch scopes the match to an exact product-name hit. Second, the existing-target update path crashed every non-clean prebuild (EvanBacon/expo-apple-targets#201): removing the old build configuration list's referrers cleared `target.props.buildConfigurationList`, which the next line then dereferenced; the patch holds the list in a local and iterates a copy of its configurations so none are skipped mid-removal.
- `@bacons/apple-targets` is held on the 4.x line on purpose: it has a 5.x major, and moving to it invalidates the patch file. `expo install --fix` does not touch either patched package (neither is an Expo SDK package), so SDK upgrades leave both patches applying cleanly.
- `expo-health-connect` was removed. It only ever supplied an Expo config plugin, and upstream archived it: everything it did ships inside `react-native-health-connect` as of v4, which now writes both the `ACTION_SHOW_PERMISSIONS_RATIONALE` intent filter (Android 13 and below) and the `ViewPermissionUsageActivity` alias (Android 14+). Installing both causes Android build failures from duplicate classes.
- After changing a patch or upgrading a patched package, run `pnpm install` from the repo root and then `npx expo prebuild --clean` from mobile before native validation.

## Food, Meals, Units, And Photo Estimates

- Food search spans local foods, online providers, meals, barcode scan, label scan, and AI photo estimates. Keep `FoodSearchScreen`, `FoodScanScreen`, `FoodEntryAddScreen`, `FoodFormScreen`, `FoodPhotoFlow`, and route params aligned.
- `LibraryScreen` is the hub for saved Foods, Meals, recurring Meal Plans, Exercises, and Workout Presets. Full lists live in `FoodsLibraryScreen`, `MealsLibraryScreen`, `MealPlansScreen`, `ExercisesLibraryScreen`, and `WorkoutPresetsLibraryScreen`.
- Food detail/edit flow: `FoodDetailScreen`, `FoodFormScreen`, `FoodForm`, `FoodUnitSelectorSheet`, `useFoodVariants`, `useFoodsLibrary`, `useDeleteFood`, `foodsApi`, and `utils/foodDetails.ts`.
- `FoodForm` supports equivalent serving sizes grouped by nutrient signature, auto-scale nutrition, compatible unit conversion via `convertServingSizeOnUnitChange`, optional AI cross-category unit conversion, custom nutrients, and caller-provided `headerChildren`.
- `EditBarcodeScreen` lets users add or remove extra barcodes for a saved food. Keep `FoodDetailScreen`, `EditBarcodeScreen`, `foodsApi`, and the `EditBarcode` route params aligned.
- Meal templates use `MealAddScreen`, `MealDetailScreen`, `FoodSearch` / `FoodEntryAdd` with `pickerMode: 'meal-builder'`, and `services/mealBuilderSelection.ts` for pending ingredient handoff.
- Picker modes (`meal-builder`, `meal-plan`, `container-link`, `action-tag`) pick an item for another screen instead of logging it. `FoodSearch`, `FoodScan`, `FoodEntryAdd` and `foodForm/CreateFoodMode` all ask `isFoodSelectionMode` (`utils/foodPickerMode.ts`), so a new mode added there cannot log by mistake from a scan or New Food; each mode's hand-off lives in its own `services/*Selection.ts`.
- Recurring meal plans use `MealPlansScreen`, `MealPlanFormScreen`, `useMealPlans`, `mealPlansApi`, and `utils/mealPlanForm.ts`. Plans can schedule any meal already visible in the user's meal library, including family/public meals, but the plan itself remains private to its owner.
- The + sheet is arranged in Settings → Quick Actions (`AddMenuSettingsScreen`): any item can sit in one of four tile slots (`addMenuCards`) or in the list under them, which are reordered and hidden with `addMenuOrder` and `hiddenAddMenuItems`. Each item below the tiles is shown as a list row, one of a pair, or a footer button: its default is `ADD_MENU_ITEM_ROW_STYLES`, a per-item choice (`addMenuRowStyles`, set from the "Show as" control on its settings row, a choice equal to the default is not stored) overrides it, and `groupRowsByStyle` (`utils/addMenu.ts`) splits the rows for both the sheet and settings, which lists them in those groups; pairs and buttons go two to a line (`chunkIntoLines`) and one on its own takes the whole line; settings shows a small live `AddMenuPreview` of the layout and each pair or button row says which line it is on. All of these live in the app preferences and are reconciled against `ADD_MENU_ITEM_KEYS` (`constants/addMenuItems.ts`, helpers in `utils/addMenu.ts`) like the watch pages, so a new item only needs a key, a label, an icon and an entry in `AddSheet`'s `itemDefs`. An item whose handler or feature is off is absent whatever the arrangement says. Exercise has no action of its own and opens its sub-menu from a card or a row.
- Workout plans (the schedule the watch's "Scheduled today" and the diary banner read) are created and edited from `Library > Workout plans` with `WorkoutPlansScreen`, `WorkoutPlanFormScreen`, `useWorkoutPlans`, `workoutPlansApi.ts` and `utils/workoutPlanForm.ts`, modeled on the meal plan screens. The form supports weekly and sequential plans and assigns saved workouts (presets); a single-exercise assignment made on the web is preserved on save but only editable there. Switching the schedule type clears the assignments, as the server expects new ones with a type change.
- Logged-meal grouped diary entries use `foodEntryMealsApi`, `FoodEntryViewScreen`, and `EditLoggedMealScreen`. Preserve stored component nutrition snapshots when editing.
- `MealTypeDetailScreen` owns single-meal-type day views and copy-to-another-day via `useCopyFoodEntries`; be careful with custom meal types and synthetic buckets.
- External food providers use provider-agnostic v2 endpoints where possible. Provider categories and barcode support come from server config; do not hardcode provider type allowlists unless preserving an explicit fallback.
- Barcode lookup keeps its source context on `FoodEntryAdd`: `BarcodeSources` loads alternatives only when opened through `barcodeLookupApi`, with account guards around loading and selection. Source switching replaces the add screen at the same stack depth, preserving diary/meal/basket context and resetting item-specific import and serving state. Comparisons use shared `barcodeNutritionPreview` with an explicit serving basis. The selected alternative applies only to the current add flow; the existing default provider and fallback settings govern later scans. Keep capture-only barcode/label modes separate from lookup.
- "All Providers" aggregated search (`useAllProvidersSearch`) fans one debounced term out across every active provider in parallel — one `useQueries` entry per provider, results projected in the `combine` callback for structural sharing. Providers stream in independently; a slow or failing provider must not block the others. Open Food Facts calls go through the shared rate limiter.
- Label scan tries Apple's on-device model first: `modules/on-device-nutrition` (Swift, `FoundationModels` with the label photo as an `Attachment` plus its text from a Vision `VNRecognizeTextRequest` run in the module (greedy sampling), iOS 27+ with Apple Intelligence; compiled out under `compiler(<6.4)`) is wrapped by `services/onDeviceLabelScan.ts`, which validates the extraction (calories and macros must appear as numbers in the returned `ocr_text`, ranges, sugars/fiber <= carbs, energy vs macros) and returns null on any failure or when the device-local `onDeviceLabelScanEnabled` preference (Food Settings, shown only when the model is available) is off, so `FoodScanScreen.handleUsePhoto` falls back to the server `scanNutritionLabel`. The Swift side has not been compiled against the iOS 27 SDK from CI-less environments; verify on an iOS 27 device after `npx expo prebuild --clean`.
- Visual Intelligence (#115): SparkyFitness is a result in iOS 26+ Visual Intelligence. `plugins/ios/VisualIntelligence.swift` (added to the app target by `plugins/withVisualIntelligence.ts`, because intents are only discovered from the app target; guarded by `canImport(VisualIntelligence)` and `@available(iOS 26)`) holds a `MealPhotoValueQuery` (an `IntentValueQuery` over `SemanticContentDescriptor`) that saves the captured image as a JPEG under Caches/visual-intelligence and returns a `MealPhotoEntity`, and a `LogMealPhotoIntent` (`OpenIntent`) that leaves a note in UserDefaults when the result is tapped. `modules/visual-intelligence` (`consumePendingImage`) returns that photo's path once (nothing if over 10 minutes old); `useVisualIntelligenceHandoff` (mounted as `VisualIntelligenceGate` in `App.tsx`) checks on mount and whenever the app becomes active, waits up to 15s for the root navigator, and opens `FoodPhotoFlow` → `Improve` with the photo, so the usual on-device or server estimate and review follow. Apple passes only the image, not a nutrition estimate. The Swift cannot be exercised in Jest or the simulator; it needs the iOS 27 SDK build and a Visual Intelligence capable iPhone. The key and folder names in the module and the Swift file must match.
- Single-photo meal estimates can also run on device (off by default): `OnDeviceNutritionModule.estimateMeal` (same module, same `compiler(>=6.4)` gate) is wrapped by `services/onDeviceFoodPhoto.ts`, which checks the numbers (item count, ranges, sugars/fiber <= carbs, energy vs macros per item) and maps them to the server's `FoodPhotoEstimateResponse` shape with no library matches. `useEstimateFoodPhoto` tries it only for exactly one image and when the device-local `onDeviceFoodPhotoEnabled` preference (Food Settings, shown only when the model is available) is on; otherwise, or on any failure, it calls the server estimate. A toast says which path produced the estimate. Not yet compiled against the iOS 27 SDK; verify with the CI compile job and on a device.
- Photo mode is hidden in meal-builder mode because photo estimates log to the diary.
- `FoodPhotoFlow` is a modal native stack and wraps itself in a local `KeyboardProvider`.
- Photo availability fetches `GET /api/chat/ai-service-settings/active` through `aiSettingsApi.ts`; food photo is attempt-all, so `isFoodPhotoAvailable` gates only on a configured provider, not a specific provider type.
- Estimation posts to `POST /api/foods/estimate-food-photo` through `estimateFoodPhoto(...)` in `externalFoodSearchApi.ts` and uses typed `FoodPhotoEstimateError` codes from `@workspace/shared`.
- Food-photo request/response changes cross package boundaries: update shared schema and server route/service with mobile.
- Keep `auto_scale_online_imports` separate from Open Food Facts-specific scaling preferences in `FoodSettingsScreen`.
- Nutrient management lives under Food Settings: `CustomNutrientsScreen` / `CustomNutrientFormScreen` (create, edit, delete; renames and deletes invalidate display, goals and daily-summary caches since the nutrient name is the key), and `NutrientDisplaySettingsScreen` (`mobile` platform only, and only the view groups a mobile screen reads: `quick_info`, which `FoodEntryViewScreen` applies to a diary entry's nutrient list, `food_database`, which `FoodNutrientBreakdown` applies to the food and meal nutrient lists, and `report_tabular`, which `DailyNutritionDetailsScreen` applies; `summary` and `diary` keep their Dashboard/Diary screens, and `goal` and `report_chart` are not offered until a mobile screen reads them). A nutrient's goal direction is edited on the Goals screen.

## Exercise, Workouts, And Fasting

- Exercise and workout preset flows use `ExerciseSearch`, `PresetSearch`, detail/form screens, paginated/search hooks, mutation hooks, and shared workout payload helpers in `utils/workoutSession.ts`.
- Session responses are discriminated unions from `@workspace/shared`: preset workouts and individual activity sessions have different shapes. Keep detail/edit screens type-safe.
- Workout/activity drafts are persisted by `workoutDraftService`; `useWorkoutForm`, `useActivityForm`, and `useDraftPersistence` own form state.
- Exercise selection returns via `CommonActions.setParams` and a nonce pattern through `useSelectedExercise`.
- Adaptive coaching (#1560): every Replace action passes `replaceFor` (built by `utils/exerciseReplace.ts`) so `ExerciseSearch` opens on a ranked **Suggested** tab (`components/ExerciseAlternativesList.tsx`, `GET /api/v2/exercises/:id/alternatives`); catalog picks import through `importExercise` first. End-of-workout feedback is `components/WorkoutFeedbackCard.tsx` (on `WorkoutCompleteScreen` and `WorkoutDetailScreen`) backed by `useWorkoutSessionFeedback`, which autosaves serially so the last answer wins. `useLiveCoachingSignals` (mounted in `ActiveWorkoutScreen`) captures signals into `activeWorkoutStore.coachingSignals` (`null` = asked, no history); `resolveLiveAssumedSetValues` applies the shared `decideAdaptiveAdjustment` rules unless the entry is in `declinedAdaptive`, and `AdaptiveSuggestionBanner` always explains the change with a "Use my usual" undo. The `adaptive_workout_suggestions` switch in `WorkoutSettingsScreen` is an account setting; `duckMusicDuringCues` (Lower music during cues) is device-local and drives `beginCueDucking`/`endCueDucking` in `services/sounds.ts` — with it off, the audio session is never touched.
