- Bodyweight exercises (#56) use the `bodyweight_reps` modality: a set's weight is signed (+ added, − assistance, blank = body weight alone). Weight inputs parse it with `parseSignedDecimalInput` (`utils/numericInput.ts`; the base `parseDecimalInput` still rejects a sign) only when the modality is `bodyweight_reps`, including the save builders, and switch to a keyboard with a minus key. Volume and the e1RM/10RM columns go through `setVolumeKg` / `setLoadKg` in `utils/workoutSession.ts`, which delegate to the shared `effectiveLoadKg` rule, with body weight from `hooks/useBodyWeightKg.ts` (the latest check-in on or before the day, fetched only when an exercise on screen is bodyweight). Live PRs stay on the added weight, an unweighted set ranking as +0.
- Weighted carries and loaded holds (#127) use the `weight_distance` / `weight_duration` modalities. Carry distance is typed in metres (yards when the app distance unit is miles) and converted with `carryDistanceFromKm` / `carryDistanceToKm` from `@workspace/shared` (`utils/carryDistance.ts`); always go through `setDistanceFromKm` / `setDistanceToKm` / `modalityStoresDuration` / `modalityStoresDistance` in `utils/workoutSession.ts` rather than branching on modality. Carry distance is excluded from the workout's cardio distance total. The activity edit flow (`EditableSetRow` / `buildActivitySetsPayload`, which takes the distance unit) edits weight plus seconds or distance for these types.
- Watch support for them: a loaded hold is armed as `timed` + `weighted` (weight box beside the countdown/stopwatch), a carry as `carry` with `targetDistanceKm`; the watch shows and edits the distance in the phone's unit (metres, or yards when the distance unit is miles; the context carries `distanceUnit`, read as `effectiveCarryUnit`) while `SetValues.distanceKm` / `CompletedSet.distanceKm` stay km on the wire, and `useWatchWorkoutBridge` writes it back as `distance`. The distance keypad accepts a tenth. A pending distance edit is dropped if `distanceUnit` changes before that edit commits, and an open distance keypad is dismissed, same as a weight-unit change drops a pending weight edit. `resolveWatchSetTargets` is the single place those targets are built.
- Rest timer state lives in `stores/activeWorkoutStore.ts`; notifications are scheduled through `services/notifications.ts`. The rest-complete ping carries a background "Complete Set" action (`rest-complete` category): responses are routed to `completeActiveSetIfReady` by `initWorkoutNotificationActions` (exported from the store, wired in App startup — the response listener cannot live in `notifications.ts` without a store↔service import cycle), and stale delivered pings are swept when the next rest is scheduled. Exactly one cue fires per rest: the in-app chime (`services/sounds.ts`) while the app is active, otherwise the ping's own sound. Anything that stands down in favour of the chime must test `willPlayRestCompleteSound()` (preference **and** `AppState`), never the preference alone — the notification handler also runs for frontmost-but-inactive states (screen locking, app switcher), and `markRestReady` cancels the due ping only while the app is active so the deadline flip cannot race the OS delivery. The ping ships at the `timeSensitive` interruption level so a Focus mode cannot silence it.
- Set IDs are preserved server-side across workout edits so the active workout cursor stays attached to the right row.
- With Workout Settings → Now card on (`activeWorkoutNowCard`, off by default), the active workout pins `ActiveWorkoutNowCard` under the exercise rail (standard format, guided mode off, hidden while a set value is being typed): "Now" or, during a rest, "Up next", "Set 2 of 3" with one dot per set, the exercise and its photos or GIF (cycled with `useImageSlideshow` when there are several), its target in large type (assumed-aware, as the workout bar), and the next exercise from `upcomingExercises` (`utils/workoutSession.ts`). Its Swap button opens the exercise overflow sheet in `swap` mode: the upcoming exercises, each done now through `activeWorkoutStore.doExerciseNow` (moves it, or its whole superset, in front of the current exercise and puts the cursor on its first set still to do, ending a running rest), then "Replace with another exercise…". The main mode of the current exercise's sheet has "Do another exercise first…" for the same list. The watch sends the same move as the `doExerciseNow` workout edit. A weight, reps or carry distance typed on the watch's keypad is sent as the `setValues` workout edit once the wearer has stopped adjusting for 1.5 s (`WorkoutSessionStore.onSetValueEditedHere`, held and merged per set by `WatchSessionManager.queueValueEdit`, because each message makes the phone echo its whole target list back, which made the crown jumpy when it was sent on every crown commit; only the fields edited, kg/km, stamped `at` so a late older copy is dropped per field, and `armedAt` so an edit queued from an earlier arm of the same saved workout is ignored), and `applyWatchWorkoutEdit` writes it to the phone's set unless that set is already logged, so both show the value before the set is logged (a focused live `ActiveWorkoutSetRow` keeps its drafts under the keyboard but never writes an unedited one back: a per-field `edited` flag, set when the user types and cleared on commit, re-seed or when focus returns, decides what is written back and what a store change may re-seed, so a watch edit made meanwhile shows in every field not typed in and is not reverted on blur); it reuses the `workoutEdit` message, so there is no new type to keep in step.
- With Workout Settings → Now card on (`activeWorkoutNowCard`, off by default), the active workout pins `ActiveWorkoutNowCard` under the exercise rail (standard format, guided mode off, hidden while a set value is being typed): "Now" or, during a rest, "Up next", "Set 2 of 3" with one dot per set, the exercise and its photos or GIF (cycled with `useImageSlideshow` when there are several), its target in large type (assumed-aware, as the workout bar), and the next exercise from `upcomingExercises` (`utils/workoutSession.ts`). Its Swap button opens the exercise overflow sheet in `swap` mode: the upcoming exercises, each done now through `activeWorkoutStore.doExerciseNow` (moves it, or its whole superset, in front of the current exercise and puts the cursor on its first set still to do, ending a running rest), then "Replace with another exercise…". The main mode of the current exercise's sheet has "Do another exercise first…" for the same list. The watch sends the same move as the `doExerciseNow` workout edit.
- Focus view: with `activeWorkoutView` set to `focus` (`appPreferencesStore`, "View" in Workout Settings; `list` is the default) a standard-format workout renders `ActiveWorkoutFocusView` instead of the rail, Now / guided cards and `ActiveWorkoutExerciseList`, keeping the header, rest bar, keyboard accessory bar and the Add exercise / End workout buttons (interval, tabata, EMOM and WOD formats ignore it). It shows one exercise at a time: `ActiveWorkoutFocusChips` (numbered pager, superset bars from `useSupersetBorders`), `ActiveWorkoutFocusMedia` (large zoomable card or thumbnail beside the title block, opening per `focusMediaDefault` and toggled per session), name with the ⋯ button (`onPressOverflow`), muscle / equipment / Best chips, "Plan: 3 × 12" and "Last time" lines (`utils/focusView.ts`), the same adaptive and progression banners as the card, `ActiveWorkoutFocusSets` (steppers; long-press deletes through the screen's `handleDeleteSet`) and Previous / Next. The coaching state is shared with the list card through `hooks/useLiveExerciseCoaching.ts` (`useLiveAssumedSetValues`, `useLiveAdaptive`, `useLiveProgression`, `useExerciseBest`, `useCaptureLiveHistory`) and `ProgressionHintBanner`, so a hidden exercise still captures its PR baseline and last-session sets (`HistoryCapture`). A horizontal pan (`swipePageDelta`: 64 px or a fast flick; `activeOffsetX` ±20, `failOffsetY` ±12) changes the page with a short slide/fade (instant under Reduce Motion, no layout transitions) and a selection haptic; a pager is not used because the page is one tall keyboard-aware column. The view follows `activeExerciseId` (the exercise holding `activeSetId`, so a watch completion moves it too) unless the lifter moved by hand within `FOCUS_FOLLOW_GRACE_MS` (30 s); then a "Back to current" pill returns to it.
- Focus view: with `activeWorkoutView` set to `focus` (`appPreferencesStore`, "View" in Workout Settings; `list` is the default) a standard-format workout renders `ActiveWorkoutFocusView` instead of the rail, Now / guided cards and `ActiveWorkoutExerciseList`, keeping the header, rest bar, keyboard accessory bar and the Add exercise / End workout buttons (interval, tabata, EMOM and WOD formats ignore it). It shows one exercise at a time: `ActiveWorkoutFocusChips` (numbered pager, superset bars from `useSupersetBorders`), `ActiveWorkoutFocusMedia` (large zoomable card or thumbnail beside the title block, opening per `focusMediaDefault` and toggled per session), name with the ⋯ button (`onPressOverflow`), muscle / equipment / Best chips, "Plan: 3 × 12" and "Last time" lines (`utils/focusView.ts`), the same adaptive and progression banners as the card, `ActiveWorkoutFocusSets` and Previous / Next. The view is wired to the screen's own handlers and sheets, so it matches the list's controls: the rest chip (`RestPeriodChip` -> `handlePressRestChip`, the per-set rest drawer), the exercise note editor (the ⋯ menu's Notes item sets `noteEditorEntryId`; `WorkoutNotesField` renders under the chips when open or a note exists), a plate graphic for barbell exercises (`plateTargetKg`, shared with the card) and the keyboard bar's Calculator button, the set-number tap opening `SetTypeMenu` (type, drop sets, Plate calculator; `FocusSetRow` and the `ActiveWorkoutSetRow` fallback), the effort header opening `MetricColumnMenu` (RPE / RIR editable; Vol / 1RM / 10RM read-only via `weightMetricText`, clamped to RPE by `isMetricClampedToRpe` as on the card), and long-press on a set row toggling `ActiveWorkoutSetDetail` (per-set note plus a Delete set button, since stepper rows have no swipe: the page pan would fight it; without `onToggleSetDetail`, long-press deletes). `FocusSetRow` commits a typed draft on unmount as well as on blur; the `ActiveWorkoutSetRow` fallback (timed rows) still commits only on blur or deactivation, so a draft typed there is lost if the lifter pages away with the keyboard up. Not carried over from the list: the Previous column and tap-to-fill, the cardio effort form, the bodyweight explainer banner, and the card's live heart-rate and calories readouts. The coaching state is shared with the list card through `hooks/useLiveExerciseCoaching.ts` (`useLiveAssumedSetValues`, `useLiveAdaptive`, `useLiveProgression`, `useExerciseBest`, `useCaptureLiveHistory`) and `ProgressionHintBanner`, so a hidden exercise still captures its PR baseline and last-session sets (`HistoryCapture`). A horizontal pan (`swipePageDelta`: 64 px or a fast flick; `activeOffsetX` ±20, `failOffsetY` ±12) changes the page with a short slide/fade (instant under Reduce Motion, no layout transitions) and a selection haptic; a pager is not used because the page is one tall keyboard-aware column. The view follows `activeExerciseId` (the exercise holding `activeSetId`, so a watch completion moves it too) unless the lifter moved by hand within `FOCUS_FOLLOW_GRACE_MS` (30 s); then a "Back to current" pill returns to it.
- Focus view: with `activeWorkoutView` set to `focus` (`appPreferencesStore`, "View" in Workout Settings; `list` is the default) a standard-format workout renders `ActiveWorkoutFocusView` instead of the rail, Now / guided cards and `ActiveWorkoutExerciseList`, keeping the header, rest bar, keyboard accessory bar and the Add exercise / End workout buttons (interval, tabata, EMOM and WOD formats ignore it). It shows one exercise at a time: `ActiveWorkoutFocusChips` (numbered pager, superset bars from `useSupersetBorders`), `ActiveWorkoutFocusMedia` (large zoomable card or thumbnail beside the title block, opening per `focusMediaDefault` and toggled per session), name with the ⋯ button (`onPressOverflow`), muscle / equipment / Best chips, "Plan: 3 × 12" and "Last time" lines (`utils/focusView.ts`), the same adaptive and progression banners as the card, `ActiveWorkoutFocusSets` and Previous / Next. The view is wired to the screen's own handlers and sheets, so it matches the list's controls: the rest chip (`RestPeriodChip` -> `handlePressRestChip`, the per-set rest drawer), the exercise note editor (the ⋯ menu's Notes item sets `noteEditorEntryId`; `WorkoutNotesField` renders under the chips when open or a note exists), a plate graphic for barbell exercises (`plateTargetKg`, shared with the card) and the keyboard bar's Calculator button, the set-number tap opening `SetTypeMenu` (type, drop sets, Plate calculator; `FocusSetRow` and the `ActiveWorkoutSetRow` fallback), the effort header opening `MetricColumnMenu` (RPE / RIR editable; Vol / 1RM / 10RM read-only via `weightMetricText`, clamped to RPE by `isMetricClampedToRpe` as on the card), and long-press on a set row toggling `ActiveWorkoutSetDetail` (per-set note plus a Delete set button, since stepper rows have no swipe: the page pan would fight it; without `onToggleSetDetail`, long-press deletes). `FocusSetRow` and the live focused `ActiveWorkoutSetRow` fallback (timed rows) both commit a typed draft on unmount as well as on blur (the fallback goes through its usual edited / unchanged guards, so an untouched draft never overwrites a watch edit), and the fallback tables get their own header row with the same metric trigger. The screen's scroll ref is passed to the view so logging the last set scrolls to the End workout button. Not carried over from the list: the Previous column and tap-to-fill, the cardio effort form, the bodyweight explainer banner, and the card's live heart-rate and calories readouts. The coaching state is shared with the list card through `hooks/useLiveExerciseCoaching.ts` (`useLiveAssumedSetValues`, `useLiveAdaptive`, `useLiveProgression`, `useExerciseBest`, `useCaptureLiveHistory`) and `ProgressionHintBanner`, so a hidden exercise still captures its PR baseline and last-session sets (`HistoryCapture`). A horizontal pan (`swipePageDelta`: 64 px or a fast flick; `activeOffsetX` ±20, `failOffsetY` ±12) changes the page with a short slide/fade (instant under Reduce Motion, no layout transitions) and a selection haptic; a pager is not used because the page is one tall keyboard-aware column. The view follows `activeExerciseId` (the exercise holding `activeSetId`, so a watch completion moves it too) unless the lifter moved by hand within `FOCUS_FOLLOW_GRACE_MS` (30 s); then a "Back to current" pill returns to it.
- Focus view: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#focus-view).
- "Add warm-ups" (#55) is an item in the active workout's exercise overflow sheet and in the ⋮ menu of `WorkoutFormExerciseList` (preset form, new workout, edit workout; prop `addWarmupSets`, backed by the `ADD_WARMUP_SETS` action in `hooks/draftExercisesSlice.ts`). `calculateWarmupSets` (`shared/src/utils/warmupSets.ts`) builds the ramp from the first working set's weight (typed or assumed) following the lifter's method (`warmupMethod`: percent × reps steps, default 40% × 5, 60% × 5, 80% × 3, kept ascending by `normalizeWarmupMethod`) and rounds to `warmupPlateRounding`, or `warmupDumbbellRounding` for an exercise whose equipment includes dumbbells (`usesDumbbells`), per display unit. `resolveWarmupOptions` (`utils/warmupSettings.ts`) turns the preferences into those options and `buildWarmupDrafts` (`utils/warmupDrafts.ts`) into draft rows. All four preferences and the on/off switch (`warmupCalculatorEnabled`) are device-local and edited on `WarmupSettingsScreen` (Workout Settings → Warm-up calculator). A step that would not climb or would reach the working weight is dropped. The sets are `warmup`-typed with `WARMUP_REST_SEC` rest; unlogged warm-ups are replaced, and once one is logged the item is hidden. The PREV column and the placeholder values pair warm-ups with last time's warm-ups and working sets with its working sets (`alignPreviousSets`), so a ramp added ahead of the working sets does not take their history.
- Left/right sets: `exercise_entry_sets.side` (`'left'` | `'right'`, null = both sides). In the live workout the set-number menu (`SetTypeMenu`) has Both / Left / Right and **Add other side** (`activeWorkoutStore.addOtherSideSet`: a copy of the set right after it with the opposite side, keeping weight/reps/rest/type but none of the outcomes; a set with no side becomes left and the copy right), and the set row appends an L or R to its number. The watch does the same from Set options (`setSetSide`, `addOtherSide` `workoutEdit` actions, applied by `useWatchWorkoutEdit`; `side` rides on each planned set and in `useWatchPlanSync`'s structure key, so a change shows on the wrist after the next plan sync). Presets and plans do not store a side, and the preset-update check ignores it. The Wear OS target does not send or show `side` yet. The watch Swift has not been compiled outside Xcode; verify on a real watch after `npx expo prebuild --clean`.
- Volume counting (#3157): `load_convention` (`total`, `per_hand`, `per_side`) on the exercise and its snapshot says how logged weight and reps count toward VOLUME only (the weight, e1RM, PRs and rep counts stay as logged). It is edited in `ExerciseFormScreen` (a Volume counting picker, shown for weight-and-reps and bodyweight exercises; per hand only for weight and reps) and sent through `CreateExercisePayload` / `UpdateExercisePayload`; the server also updates the exercise's logged entries. Volume goes through `setVolumeKg(set, modality, bodyWeightKg, loadConvention)` and `getExerciseVolumeKg` in `utils/workoutSession.ts`, which call the shared `volumeMultiplier` with the set's `side`; the live workout reads it from `exercise_snapshot.load_convention`, which `activeWorkoutStore.addExercise` / `replaceExercise` copy from the library exercise. The draft forms (`WorkoutDraftExercise`) do not carry it because they show no volume.
- Rest duration is configurable per exercise via `RestPeriodChip` / `RestPeriodSheet` and is forwarded through `buildExercisesPayload`. New exercises/sets and null `rest_time` fallbacks seed from the `defaultRestSec` app preference via `getDefaultRestSec()` (Settings → Workout Settings), not a hardcoded constant.
- `ExerciseStatisticsScreen` is the mobile side of the web's Reports → Exercise Progress. It reads `GET /api/reports/exercise-dashboard` through `useExerciseDashboard` (typed by the shared `ExerciseDashboardSummary`), and the 7d/30d/90d control (`utils/trendRange.ts`, shared with `NutrientTrendsScreen`) is the only thing that sets its range. It is reached from the Dashboard's `ExerciseProgressCard` header and from a Library row, the latter because the card hides on days with nothing logged. Set counts come first, then the body figure, then the extra analysis in `CollapsibleSection`s that open one at a time.
- `StrengthVolumeCard` (section `exercise.volume`, toggle in Reports settings) follows the consistency card: `useStrengthVolume` reads `GET /api/reports/strength-volume` (weekly hard sets per muscle against 10-20 and the deload suggestion from shared `buildMuscleVolume` / `suggestDeload`, refreshed on focus). The suggestion is advice only; reasons arrive as codes and the card translates them (`exerciseStatistics.volume.*`, English only).
- `TrainingConsistencyCard` (#59) sits between the heat map and "More Analysis" and, like the cardio list, ignores the range control: `useTrainingConsistency` reads `GET /api/reports/training-consistency` (a fixed 26 weeks from the shared `buildTrainingConsistency`, refreshed on focus). It shows the weekly streak, a week-column calendar (weekday labels down the side, month labels above, a legend below; weeks start on the account's `first_day_of_week`, which the server returns as `firstDayOfWeek`) and this-week-vs-last-week working sets per muscle; the grid and table rows come from `utils/trainingConsistency.ts`.
- Training load card: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#training-load-card).
- `RunEstimatesCard` (section key `exercise.load`, under the load card): `useRunEstimates` reads `GET /api/reports/run-estimates` (fixed window ending today, refreshed on focus) and shows the estimated VO2 max (median of qualifying runs in 6 weeks, needs 2) and the average running power (last 4 weeks, with W/kg). Both come from the shared `computeRunningEstimates` on the server and are labelled as estimates on the card (`≈`, and a subtitle saying they are not a measurement); it renders nothing until at least one exists. The estimates are separate from the device-synced VO2 max in Health Trends (`Vo2MaxLineChart`), which is a measurement; it only uses the estimate when nothing has synced.
- `FitnessFatigueCard` (section key `exercise.load`, under `TrainingLoadCard`): `useFitnessFatigue` reads `GET /api/reports/fitness-fatigue` (fixed 90 days ending today, refreshed on focus) and shows today's 0 to 100 readiness with its factors (training form, sleep, resting heart rate, HRV; only the ones with data; HRV and resting heart rate also show "41 ms against your usual 68 ms (median of 9 nights)" from the optional `readiness.vitals`) over fitness, fatigue and form numbers and the last 28 days of form. The score and the curve are worked out on the server by the shared `computeReadiness` and `computeFitnessFatigue` (`shared/src/utils/readiness.ts`, `fitnessFatigue.ts`), so the card only draws them.
- Strength / Cardio switch and cardio sessions: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#strength--cardio-switch-and-cardio-sessions).
- `TrainingLoadCard` (#250) sits above the consistency card (section key `exercise.load`): `useTrainingLoad` reads `GET /api/reports/training-load` (fixed 28 days ending today, refreshed on focus). The server (`reportService.getTrainingLoad`) takes every logged workout, cardio and strength alike, and the shared pure `computeTrainingLoad` (`shared/src/utils/trainingLoad.ts`) turns them into four 7-day loads, acute (last 7 days) against chronic (4-week average), a status (`noData`, `learning` under 21 days of history, `low` under 0.8, `steady` to 1.3, `high` to 1.5, `spike` above) and a rest nudge (`takeRest` after a high/spike week with a session today or yesterday, `ready` after a low week with 2+ rest days). A session's load is minutes times an Edwards zone weight (1 to 5) from average heart rate against the person's heart rate zones (the server resolves them from their own max heart rate, else Nes from their date of birth, else 190; see Heart rate zones below); a session with no heart rate counts at weight 2, so a strength workout with no watch still counts by its time. `RunProgramBanner` reads the same hook: after a `spike` it offers the same one-tap ease as the break offer (`suggestFromLoad` in `shared/src/utils/runProgramLoad.ts`: -15% on the next two workouts, not offered within 72 h of a plan change, and the break offer wins when both apply). The Dashboard has a short version, `TrainingLoadDashboardCard` (card key `trainingLoad`, preference `trainingLoadCardVisible`, shown after Exercise Progress by default and switchable in Dashboard Settings): this week's load against the usual, a one-line verdict, any rest nudge and the four weekly bars, from the same `useTrainingLoad` (its `enabled` argument keeps a hidden card from requesting). It renders nothing until a workout has been logged, and a tap opens Exercise Statistics.
- Its Strength / Cardio switch leaves the range alone. Cardio lists sessions from `GET /api/exercise-stats/query` (`useCardioSessions`, an infinite query; the server leaves strength out when no category is sent), grouped by month and led by `cardioSessionHeadline` from shared, the rule the web list uses. A tap opens `CardioSessionScreen`: the route from `/api/generic-health/workout-gps/:id` (`RouteMap`: over Apple Maps on iOS, over Google Maps on Android only when the build set `GOOGLE_MAPS_ANDROID_API_KEY`, otherwise the plain SVG `RouteLine`), a Pace card (`PaceChart`, fed by `paceSeriesFromTrack` in `utils/cardioSession.ts`: pace over each stretch of at least 30 s between trackpoints against distance in the display unit, using the points' own `dist` when present, dropping standing-still stretches slower than an hour per unit, drawn on negated pace so faster is higher; hidden when fewer than two readings), heart rate from the trackpoints or else the day's stored samples through shared `buildWorkoutHeartRateSeries` (indoor sessions still get a graph), and time in each zone from `/api/generic-health/workout-hr-zones/:id`. The stat tiles come from the `/api/exercise-stats/query` item, which also carries `floorsClimbed` and `elevationGainMeters` (null, never 0, when the source did not measure them) so a stair stepper shows floors and elevation (feet with the miles setting); the HealthKit reader takes floors from the workout's `FlightsClimbed` statistic when the sample's own `totalFlightsClimbed` is empty, as it is for a GymKit workout. Garmin's raw provider blob, which the web still falls back to for old activities, is not read. The Route card has an **Export GPX** button (`shareRouteAsGpx` in `services/gpxExport.ts`: `buildGpx` in `utils/gpx.ts` writes a GPX 1.1 track with elevation, time and Garmin heart-rate/cadence extensions, points in time order, unusable points dropped; the file goes to the cache folder and the system share sheet, so it can reach Files, Strava or Garmin Connect; a cancelled sheet is not an error). From the diary, a synced cardio workout opens it, and so does a run, walk or ride logged in the app (a GPS recording or watch run is an in-app entry) when it has a distance or heart rate; for those it gets a `diaryEntry` param and an Edit button that replaces it with `ActivityDetail`, the in-app editor. `useTrendRangeBounds` keeps both queries' ranges current across midnight.
- Best efforts: the Cardio view shows `BestEffortsCard` (all-time board from `GET /api/exercise-stats/best-efforts`, `useBestEfforts`, key `['exerciseStats', 'bestEfforts']` so `invalidateExerciseCache` reloads it) and the run detail (`CardioSessionScreen`) shows `RunBestEfforts`, the fastest stretches inside that one run computed on the device by `utils/bestEfforts.ts` (`runBestEfforts`) from the track or distance series it already loads, marking any that is the board's best. The short distance follows the person's unit (`bestEffortIdsForUnit`). The algorithm is shared: `shared/src/utils/bestEfforts.ts`.
- Running power and dynamics: `CardioSessionScreen` renders `RunningPowerCard` and `RunningDynamicsCard` (`components/exerciseStats/`, chart `RunSeriesChart`) from the stored trackpoints (`detail.route`, per-point `power`, `gct` ms, `vo` mm, `stride` cm). All maths is the shared `runningPowerDynamics.ts` (normalized power, variability index, power-to-heart-rate decoupling, critical power as 95% of the best 20 minutes and the five power zones from it, ground contact drift, vertical ratio); the web activity report uses the same functions (`ActivityPowerDynamicsCards`, `ActivitySensorChart`). HealthKit merges `gct`, `vo` and `stride` onto the trackpoints with the other series (`workoutTelemetry.ts`; queried in cm and m, stored in mm and cm). Critical power is only estimated from the run itself; there is no setting for it yet.
- Running power and dynamics: `CardioSessionScreen` renders `RunningPowerCard` and `RunningDynamicsCard` (`components/exerciseStats/`, chart `RunSeriesChart`) from the stored trackpoints (`detail.route`, per-point `power`, `gct` ms, `vo` mm, `stride` cm). All maths is the shared `runningPowerDynamics.ts` (normalized power, variability index, power-to-heart-rate decoupling, critical power as 95% of the best 20 minutes and the five power zones from it, ground contact drift, vertical ratio); the web activity report uses the same functions (`ActivityPowerDynamicsCards`, `ActivitySensorChart`). HealthKit merges `gct`, `vo` and `stride` onto the trackpoints with the other series (`workoutTelemetry.ts`; queried in cm and m, stored in mm and cm). Zones use the account's `running_critical_power_watts` (50 to 600 W, edited on the power card itself through `updatePreferences`; clearing sends null) via shared `resolveCriticalPower`, and otherwise an estimate from the run.
- Running trends: the Cardio view opens with `RunningTrendsCard` when the last twelve weeks hold a run. `useRunningTrends` reads those weeks from the same `/api/exercise-stats/query` endpoint as the session list (all pages, metric units) and the running personal records from `GET /api/exercise-stats/prs` (`fetchPersonalRecords`); both keys sit in the `cardioSessions` family (`['cardioSessions', 'runningTrends', start, end]` and `['cardioSessions', 'runningRecords']`) so the Cardio refresh and exercise cache invalidation reload them. Weeks begin on the account's `first_day_of_week` preference (`toWeekStart`, Monday if unset). The maths is in `@workspace/shared`: `buildRunningTrends` (`utils/runningTrends.ts`: runs only via `classifyActivitySport`, weekly distance and longest run, this week against the average of the previous four, and efficiency, metres per minute per bpm on runs of 20+ minutes with a plausible average heart rate, compared between the earlier and later half of the window) and `predictRaceTimes` (`utils/raceTimePredictions.ts`: Riegel from the nearest-distance running record, no extrapolation beyond 8x, a record at the exact distance shown as such, `runningEfforts` picks running records out of the PR matrix). The web Reports → Exercise → Cardio view shows the same card from the same util (`SparkyFitnessFrontend/src/components/ExerciseCharts/RunningTrendsCard.tsx`).
- The route map (`RouteTileMap`, `components/maps/MapSurface.tsx` / `MapSurface.android.tsx`) renders cardio routes across platforms, providing keyless OpenStreetMap vector tile rendering via MapLibre on Android when no Google Maps API key is configured. On iOS, `MapSurface` mounts `ReactNativeMapSurface` (`react-native-maps`) backed by native Apple Maps, avoiding MapLibre CocoaPods or runtime overhead (`react-native.config.js` excludes `@maplibre/maplibre-react-native` from iOS autolinking). On Android, `usesGoogleMapsOnAndroid()` (`utils/routeMapSupport.ts`) checks `extra.androidGoogleMapsEnabled` (set in `app.config.ts` from `GOOGLE_MAPS_ANDROID_API_KEY`): keyed Android builds use `ReactNativeMapSurface` (Google Maps, rendered in lite mode for still previews inside scroll views), while keyless Android builds mount `MapLibreMapSurface` (`@maplibre/maplibre-react-native`) rendering OpenStreetMap vector tiles via OpenFreeMap public endpoints (`liberty` and `dark`). `canShowRouteMap()` returns true on iOS and Android (non-mobile platforms fall back to `RouteLine`). `canShowSatelliteMap()` allows satellite/hybrid imagery on iOS Apple Maps or keyed Google Maps, but hides the toggle where OpenStreetMap vector tiles have no imagery layer. Never set `googleMapId`: a map ID bills every load as Dynamic Maps, while plain Maps SDK loads are free. Dark mode is `userInterfaceStyle` on iOS, `DARK_MAP_STYLE` on Android Google Maps, and OpenFreeMap dark style JSON in MapLibre. `jest.setup.js` mocks `react-native-maps`, hidden from screen readers, so query them with `includeHiddenElements`; the MapLibre surface is mocked in its own test.
- The body figure (`components/exerciseStats/MuscleFigure.tsx`) draws one `<Path>` per region so a tap knows which muscle it hit; `SvgXml` cannot. Its paths are generated from the web's `SparkyFitnessFrontend/public/images/muscle-{male,female}.svg` into `muscleFigurePaths.ts` (`MUSCLE_FIGURES`) by `pnpm run muscle-figure:generate`, and `validate` fails if they drift. Both figures are 462 units tall so one set of stroke widths fits both. A Male / Female control on the card starts from the profile's BMR gender (shared `defaultBodyFigure`) and never writes the profile. The female figure is converted from react-native-body-highlighter (MIT, notice kept in the SVG); its head, hands and feet are the untinted grey the male figure uses, and its adductors and tibialis stay untinted because the male figure does not draw them. Region classes map through the shared `svgClassToSchemaName` / `figureKeyForMuscle` (`shared/src/utils/muscleBodyMap.ts`), the same map the web heat map uses; muscles the figure does not draw are listed under "Not on the figure".
- GPS activity recording: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#gps-activity-recording).
- GPS activity recording (#125): `RecordActivityScreen` (root-stack `RecordActivity`, opened from `AddSheet`'s Record Activity row) records a walk, run or ride. `services/gpsRecordingService.ts` owns the session: an `expo-location` task registered at module scope (`index.js` imports it, so a location fix can wake a killed app) feeds fixes through `acceptFix` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts` (accuracy, impossible-speed, out-of-order and standing-still filtering) and writes accepted points to AsyncStorage in 100-point chunks as they arrive, so a killed app or a dropped connection loses nothing; `hydrate()` restores them, and the screen reads live state through `useGpsRecording()`. The screen offers Discard (with a confirmation) while recording or paused as well as when finished. Leaving the screen does not hide a recording: while no strength workout is active, `ActiveWorkoutBar` shows it above the dock (`useRecordingBarContent`: clock, distance and pace, discard on the left, pause/resume on the right, a check that opens the screen when finished; the bar is hidden on `RecordActivity` itself and `useActiveWorkoutBarPadding` reserves room through the cheap `useHasGpsRecording`). Indoor sessions (`session.indoor`, chosen with the Outdoor/Indoor control before Start) skip the location permission and the task entirely: `startRecording({ indoor: true })` never touches `expo-location`, pause and resume only flip the status, the Auto-pause switch is hidden and sent off (there are no fixes for it to judge by), and the screen shows the clock (plus watch heart rate) with no map or pace. On Finish the screen asks for an optional distance typed in the user's distance unit, and `saveRecordedActivity(session, points, unit, indoorDistanceKm)` logs it with no track upload under `Indoor Walking`/`Indoor Running`/`Indoor Cycling` (never `Treadmill Walk`: shared `activitySport` reads `treadmill` as running, so a walk would match two sports and fall back to `other`). On iPhone and Android the distance field is prefilled from the step counter: `useIndoorDistanceEstimate` calls `services/stepDistance.estimateIndoorDistance`, which reads the steps over the session window from the system pedometer (`services/stepCounter`; iOS queries the window after Finish and asks for Motion & Fitness the first time; Android stores the hardware counter at Start in `stepCounterAtStart` via `modules/step-counter` and subtracts it at Finish, so the app can be killed in between, and asks for Activity recognition at the start of an indoor walk or run only), scales them by the moving share of the window because pauses are only a total, and multiplies by the learned stride (`services/strideCalibration`, AsyncStorage on this device only, walk and run kept apart, blended with `utils/stride.blendStride`) or a typical default. The screen says whether the stride is learned or typical. A distance the person changes teaches the stride (`learnFromIndoorEntry`; an accepted estimate teaches nothing); a typed figure is a treadmill reading, so the treadmill correction (below) is taken off it first and the stride learns from the corrected distance, while the prefilled step estimate is never corrected, and a saved outdoor walk or run teaches it from its GPS distance when Motion & Fitness is already allowed (`learnFromOutdoorRecording`, which never prompts). Both run once, with the entry's first save, and never fail it. The prompt text is `motionUsagePermission` in `app.config.ts`, so changing it needs a prebuild. Whether the distance was entered or estimated is not stored on the entry (that needs a server field), and indoor sessions are not yet kept out of personal records. The watch still shows distance 0 and pace `—` for an indoor session and starts its HealthKit session as outdoor; nothing in the recording payload says indoor yet. `RecordActivityScreen` shows a spinner until that `hydrate()` resolves (so Start never flashes before a restored recording appears) and, for a live recording the screen did not start itself, a notice with its start time and a Discard button. Pause only flips the session status: the task keeps running and `ingestFixes` drops its fixes, because starting a location task again cannot be done from the background (iOS ignores a while-using start made out of sight, Android 12+ refuses the foreground service), which a resume sent from the watch with the phone locked would need. Resume restarts the task only if the OS dropped it. Auto-pause (`utils/autoPause.ts`, pure `stepAutoPause`) runs inside `ingestFixes` on the raw fixes before `acceptFix`, because standing-still jitter is filtered out of the track: stopped below a per-activity speed for 6 to 10 s pauses the session with `pausedAt` set to when the person stopped and `autoPaused: true`; moving above a higher speed for 2 to 3 s resumes it with a new `seg`, the pause counted up to when movement began. The location task drops the distance filter (`distanceInterval: 0`) only while auto-pause is on, because iOS delivers no fixes to a person standing still when one is set and the detector, which only advances on arriving fixes, would never reach its stop time; with auto-pause off it keeps the 2 m filter. A fix with no Doppler speed (iOS reports -1, common while standing still) falls back to speed from position, measured from the oldest fix in a 5 to 15 s trail and less the larger accuracy radius, so a phone drifting a few metres while its owner stands still reads as stopped; at Finish the app log gets one line counting the fixes auto-pause saw with a speed, without one and too inaccurate. Fixes with accuracy worse than 30 m are ignored, a manual pause is never auto-resumed (only `autoPaused` sessions are watched), and the toggle is `session.autoPause` set from the Record screen's switch (preference in AsyncStorage, on by default). While a recording runs, the screen shows compact Auto-pause and Voice cues switches for that session only (the preferences stay as they are): `setRecordingAutoPause(enabled, notification)` resets the detector, starts the running location task again with the matching distance filter (0 on, 2 m off; a failure is logged and the setting kept) and, when turned off while it holds the session auto-paused, resumes it (an indoor session stays off), and `setRecordingAudioCues(unit | undefined)` counts the splits already completed as said, so none is announced late. Start countdown: `RecordActivityScreen` holds `countdownLeft` (preference `countdownSeconds` from `COUNTDOWN_CHOICES`, off by default, stored beside the other recording preferences); Start with a countdown arms it, each second fires a selection haptic and (voice cues on) speaks the number, and at zero it calls `startRecording`. Nothing is recorded or requested until then, so the first-run location prompt follows the countdown, and Cancel leaves no session. Laps: the Lap button calls `markLap()` (recording only; presses within 3 s of the last are ignored) which stores epoch-ms marks in `session.laps`; `computeLaps(points, marks)` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts` turns them into splits (interpolating distance and active time at each mark, a mark inside a pause taking the fix before it, the stretch after the last mark `partial`), the finished view lists them above the splits, and `saveRecordedActivity` uploads them as the entry's lap windows instead of the automatic splits when any exist. `splitBars` draws the per-split pace bars and picks the fastest (never a partial). Intervals: `utils/intervals.ts` is the pure side (`IntervalPlan` = a style, `runWalk` or `fastEasy`, and timed `warmup`/`work`/`recovery`/`cooldown` steps; `buildIntervalPlan` clamps and builds one from options, `INTERVAL_PRESETS` are the built-in plans, `intervalPosition(plan, activeSeconds)` says where a plan stands). `startRecording({ intervals })` stores it as `session.intervals = { plan, cued }` (`cued` is the last step started, so a restored session never repeats one); `advanceIntervals` runs after each batch of fixes in `ingestFixes` and from `tickIntervals()` (the screen calls it every second while open), against `elapsedSeconds`, so paused time never counts. A new step buzzes (`fireImpactHaptic`), is spoken when voice cues are on (`intervalCue`; the first step replaces the "Recording started" cue) and is marked as a lap at its exact start (`session.laps`, deduped against the 3 s gap), so the saved activity is cut into the work and recovery stretches by the existing lap path; steps passed in one go are all marked but only the latest is spoken. UI: `components/recording/IntervalSetup.tsx` (picker plus custom steppers; the screen owns the choice) and `IntervalCard.tsx` (live step). On a phone recording the watch is told the step (`intervalIndex/Label/Remaining/Next` in `WatchRecordingStatePayload`; `useWatchRecordingBridge` re-sends durably when the step changes, `RecordingStore` buzzes via `Haptics.step()`); watch-only recordings follow no plan. Programs: `utils/runPrograms.ts` is a list of workouts, each an `IntervalPlan` (`RUN_PROGRAMS`: `walkFit` (8 weeks of brisk walking, `activity: 'walk'` so Record starts a walk and "Count it" matches a walk; `programActivity(id)` gives it), `walk10k` (10 weeks of brisk walking to a 10K walk, also `activity: 'walk'`), `returnToRunning` (4), `gentle5k` (12), `beginner5k` (9 weeks of run/walk), `fiveToTenK` (8), `faster5k` (6, speed work in `fastEasy` style), `beginner10k` (12), `faster10k` (8, speed work), `tenKToHalf` (10), `halfMarathon` (12), `fasterHalf` (10, speed work), `beginnerMarathon` (18), `marathon` (16) and `advancedMarathon` (16, five runs a week, speed work). `tenKToHalf`, `halfMarathon`, `fasterHalf` and `beginnerMarathon` also have a four-runs-a-week twin (`<id>4`, `variantOf` set); `PICKER_PROGRAMS` is the list the picker shows, and `RunProgramsScreen` puts a `SegmentedControl` on those cards (`programVersions(id)`) to choose 3 or 4 runs, starting the chosen id. A variant needs its own name, description and structure strings; the faster plans use `stridesRun`, `hills`, `tempo` and `marathonPaceLong` in `runPrograms.ts` for strides, hill repeats, tempo runs and marathon-pace blocks, built from run/walk pieces between a warm-up and cool-down; the longer ones are continuous runs timed in minutes, so a plan step may be up to 4 hours (`MAX_PROGRAM_STEP_SECONDS`); `RunProgramCard` shows a picker, and switching a running program to another one asks first and restarts at week 1), with `programStatus`/`advanceProgress` over a `ProgramProgress` (`next` = index of the workout due). `services/runProgramService.ts` is the server-backed side (`services/api/runProgramApi.ts` over `/api/v2/run-program`, a `run_programs` row per user holding the person's own copy of the workouts, the place `next_index`, the `enabled` flag and a change log; the React Query copy under `runProgramQueryKey` is set from each response, and `useRunProgram` re-reads on every mount because the AI assistant may have changed it): `setProgramEnabled`, `restartProgram`, `skipProgramWorkout`, `setProgramPosition`, `completeProgramWorkout`. The status is built with shared `storedProgramStatus`, so adjusted workouts reach the card, the picker and the recording. `RunProgramsScreen` wraps each change in `change`, which toasts a failure (offline); a failed tick-off after a save is logged, not shown, since the activity itself is saved. Programs are opt-in: `ProgramProgress.enabled` is the Record card's switch (absent in older data means on), the card always shows the stored place, but `getProgramStatus()` and the screen's `programStatus` are null while it is off, so nothing is preselected, nothing is ticked off and the Diary shows nothing; turning it off keeps the place. Setup and management live on `RunProgramsScreen` (root-stack `RunPrograms`, from a Library row; with no program it lists the fifteen `PICKER_PROGRAMS` with Start program (which asks Start today / Pick a date, then calls `startProgram(id, startDay)`; null = today), with one it shows `RunProgramCard`: switch, today's workout, Skip, Start over, workout picker, run days and start day); a collapsible `RunProgramGuide` ("How the plans work": what a workout is made of, effort by the talk test and a 1-10 scale rather than pace, and a brisk walking pace of about 5 to 6 km/h; `programStructure` describes each program's weeks and also shows on each program card; `programRest` adds a per-program "Rest days" section, e.g. no back-to-back workouts for the beginner plans) sits on that screen in both states; the Record screen only has the slim `RunProgramSelect` (today's workout, with a Manage link), and the Diary shows `RunProgramBanner` through `ExerciseSummary`'s `programBanner` prop, styled like a scheduled workout plan: `programRunForDate` (shared `utils/runProgramSchedule.ts`) decides what a Diary day shows: with run days chosen it follows the calendar `projectRuns` lays out from the next workout due (one workout per chosen weekday starting today, the first day or tomorrow once today's is done, so a missed run moves everything after it along and nothing is ever skipped), so today shows the workout due ("Scheduled Today") and later days show what is coming ("Scheduled", no Start button); with none chosen it offers the next workout today only ("Up next"); nothing before the first day, after the goal day, once a workout was done today (`doneDay`), or when the program is off or finished. Saving a recording replaces `RecordActivity` with `RunSummary` (`RunSummaryScreen`): `handleSave` builds `RunSummaryParams` with `buildRunSummary` (`utils/runSummary.ts`: figures, a route thinned to 250 points, per-unit splits, the person's laps, average heart rate from `getHeartRateSamples`; plain data, since the recording is cleared right after) and, for a program workout, `nextProgramLine` (`utils/runProgramSummary.ts`: the next workout and the day `projectRuns` puts it on, read from the program the server now holds) with the place in the program. The screen shows the stat cards, the route (`RouteMap`), the program progress with a "How hard was that?" row (1 to 10, shown when `programSummary` returned `rate`, i.e. the workout was ticked off: `rateProgramWorkout` saves it to the server's `effort_log` through `POST /api/v2/run-program/effort`; 8 or more offers "Repeat this workout" = `setProgramPosition(index)`, 3 or less offers "Skip the next workout" = `index + 2` unless that is the last, and shared `suggestFromEffort` adds a note after two hard or two easy in a row; nothing changes unless a button is tapped) and a View program link, and the splits or laps through the shared `SplitRow`/`Stat` (`components/recording/SplitRow.tsx`, also used by the Record screen); Done goes back. A wrist-only run has no summary screen. `RunProgramBanner` also offers "Count it" when `findLoggedRun` (`utils/loggedRun.ts`: a non-preset entry named like a run, at least half the planned length or 10 minutes) is among today's entries, completing the due workout on the person's say-so (a run recorded some other way, such as the watch or an import). Every tick-off passes the diary entry it was done as (`completeProgramWorkout`'s `entryId`: the saved recording, or the counted run), which the server keeps in `completion_log`; `ProgramWorkoutBadge` (`useProgramWorkoutForEntry`, shared lookup `programWorkoutForEntry` in `utils/runProgramSummary.ts`) then shows "Part of your training program" with the workout label on `CardioSessionScreen` and `ActivityDetailScreen`. `RunProgramCalendar` on the screen lists the dates, the finish day (`projectedFinishDay`) and whether it fits before the goal day. The Record screen never chooses the workout for the person: it is offered on `RunProgramSelect` and in the `IntervalSetup` picker (`programLabel`), and chosen as the interval choice `'program'` only when the screen is opened with the `programWorkout` route param (the Diary card's Start and the program screen's Start today's workout), so a plain walk or run starts with no plan. For a walk or ride, `IntervalSetup` (given `activity`) leaves out the run/walk presets and style, the Record screen drops a run/walk preset when the activity changes, and `intervalPlan` turns a `runWalk` plan into `fastEasy`, so the card, spoken cues and watch steps say Fast and Easy rather than Run. While a recording exists the screen's header names it (`recordingTitle`: "Outdoor run", "Indoor walk", "Outdoor ride"), and outdoors `GpsSignalBadge` shows bars from the newest fix's accuracy (`useLatestGpsFix`, its own listener set in `gpsRecordingService` so the bars do not re-render the screen; `gpsSignalLevel` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts`, the watch's thresholds). The watch-run live screen is titled "Run on your watch" or "Walk on your watch". `startRecording({ program })` stores `session.program = { id, index }`, and `handleSave` calls `completeProgramWorkout` only when `planCompleted(session)`, so a run cut short is offered again. `setProgramPosition(index)` lets the person jump to any workout (the card's "Choose a different workout" picker lists the weeks, finished runs ticked), clamped to the program. Schedule: the weekdays the workout shows in the Diary (`run_days`, 0 = Sunday, empty = every day), the first day it shows (`start_date`, null = from today), the goal day (`end_date`, null = none; nothing is scheduled after it and the screen says whether the program fits before it) and the last local day a workout was completed (`last_done_date`, sent as `day` by `completeProgramWorkout`) are columns on the same `run_programs` row, like a workout plan's schedule, so they follow the person between devices; `useRunProgram` returns them as `schedule` and `doneDay`, `setProgramSchedule` writes them, and `RunProgramCard` edits them (day chips; a start date picked from a calendar, today unless one is set). Switching programs keeps the schedule. There are no run notifications; `cancelLegacyRunReminders` (`services/legacyRunReminders.ts`, run at startup) cancels weekly reminders an earlier build scheduled and deletes their stored key. An indoor session has no location task, so the plan moves on while the record screen is open and catches up when it is opened again. Voice cues (`session.audioCues` = `'km'`/`'miles'`, off by default, set from the Record screen's switch via `getRecordingPreferences`/`setRecordingPreference`): `ingestFixes` calls `cueSplit` after new points, which runs `computeSplits` and speaks only the latest newly completed full split (`session.cuedSplits` is persisted so a restore never repeats one); start, manual pause/resume, auto-pause/resume and finish speak through `services/recordingCues.ts` (`Speech.speak` with `useApplicationAudioSession`, each cue replacing the one before; it reuses `beginCueDucking`/`endCueDucking`, so ducking follows `duckMusicDuringCues`). The voice is `resolveSpeechVoice` (`services/speechVoice.ts`), shared with guided workouts: the installed voices are read ahead (`loadSpeechVoices`, called by `beginRecordingCues` and `speakGuided`), then it returns the person's pick (`guidedVoiceId`, one device-local setting for both) while still installed, otherwise the best Premium/Enhanced voice for the app language (`bestVoiceId` in `utils/speechVoices.ts`), otherwise undefined (system default, and also until the voices have loaded, so a stale id never reaches `expo-speech`, which throws on an unknown iOS identifier). `expo-speech` reports an iOS Premium voice as "Default" (it only maps `.enhanced`), so `voiceTier` also reads `.premium.` / `.enhanced.` from Apple's identifier and drops `com.apple.speech.synthesis.voice.*` novelty voices; Siri voices are not exposed to apps. The picker is `useSpeechVoiceChoices`, rendered by `RecordingVoiceCard` (Record screen, while voice cues are on) and `GuidedWorkoutSettingsSection`. The words come from `utils/recordingCues.ts` with an injected `t` (durations are spelled out, since a speech engine reads "5:24" as a time of day). The audio session is `startRecordingCueSession()` in `sounds.ts` (silent-switch override plus iOS `shouldPlayInBackground`): the location task already keeps the app running on iOS, so no keep-alive track is used. Each resume starts a new `seg`, so the jump across a pause is never distance or time. The first fix of a stretch (the start, or after a resume) must be within `STRETCH_START_MAX_ACCURACY_M` (25 m), tighter than the 50 m cutoff for the rest, so a cold-start fix does not put a spike at the head of the route. Coaching audio (#250): on top of splits and plan steps, `advanceCoaching` (next to `advanceIntervals`, from each fix batch and `tickIntervals`) speaks the cues `nextCoachingCues` (`utils/coachingCues.ts`, pure) says are due, only with voice cues on and at most one a tick (the rest are marked said): "three, two, one" in the last three seconds of such a step (one utterance, `stepCountdownCue`; it makes the ten-second warning of that step stale, so both are marked said together) after a ten-second warning before a step of 30 s or more that names the next step, a halfway call and a last-minute call for plan workouts of 5 and 3 minutes or more, a milestone word when the recorded distance passes 5K, 10K, a half marathon or a marathon (`RACE_MILESTONES_METERS`, outdoors only, whatever the unit; the furthest one after a gap), and a spoken check-in (time, distance and average pace; time only indoors) every `session.checkInMinutes` (the `CHECK_IN_CHOICES` 0, 5, 10 or 15, set in `CheckInCard` before Start, changed mid-run with `setRecordingCheckIn`, remembered as a preference). `session.coaching` holds what was said so a restore never repeats it (a state saved before the countdown and milestones existed is read with their defaults). A split announced at the same fix is spoken first, in the same breath as the coaching cue (`advanceCoaching(now, splitText)`), because a second utterance replaces the first; the countdown alone skips that, as it is for this second only. A program workout adds two sentences (`programSpokenCues` in `utils/programCues.ts`, already in the app language): `start` ("Week 2, run 3. 25 minutes.", plus "Eased for today." when its running time is 10% or more under the original) spoken in the same breath as the first step, and `done` ("Workout 4 of 27 done. Next up, Week 2, run 4.", or "Program complete. Well done.") added to the finish cue once every step of the plan has run for its full unpaused duration (a run stopped early gets none). The phone service reads them from `session.program`/`session.intervals`; the watch gets them as `startText`/`doneText` on the plan (`buildWatchRunPlans`, `buildWatchStartPlan`) and speaks them as they are. Splits also say how much faster or slower they were than the split before when it differs by 5 s or more (`splitTrendNote`). A watch run speaks the same cues: `Domain/WatchCoaching.swift` is the Swift port of `nextCoachingCues` (constants and cue order must stay in step), `WatchRunStore.cueCoachingIfDue` runs on its 1 s timer (which now also runs for a run with a check-in interval but no plan, and for any outdoor run, for the milestones) and speaks through `WatchRunCues` (`stepCountdownText` (spoken with `interrupt`), `milestoneText`, `stepWarningText`, `halfwayText`, `lastMinuteText`, `checkInText`, English like the other watch cues; check-ins give only the time indoors). The interval comes from `runStart` (`checkInMinutes`, sent with voice cues on) or, for a run begun on the wrist, the context's `runCheckInMinutes`; it lives in `WatchRunSettings.checkInMinutes` (optional, so older snapshots decode), and after a relaunch `WatchCoachingState.resumed` counts what has passed as said. Only while-using permission is requested: iOS keeps recording through `UIBackgroundModes: location` set in `app.config.ts`, Android through the plugin's foreground service, so `ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` stays off (Google Play reviews it closely). `utils/gpsRecording.ts` is the pure maths (distance, pace, smoothed elevation gain/loss, per-km/mile splits); `services/gpsRecordingSave.ts` finds or creates the Walking/Running/Cycling cardio exercise, creates a normal entry with `createExerciseEntry` (no `calories_burned`, so the server estimates it), then posts the route and split windows to `POST /api/exercise-entries/:id/gps-track` (`attachExerciseEntryGpsTrack`). It does NOT go through `/api/health-data`, whose per-source delete-then-insert would wipe other recorded activities on the same day. The entry id is kept on the session, so a failed track upload retries without logging the activity twice, and the server replaces an existing track on re-post. The live map reuses `RouteMap`, so Android shows a plain line without a Google Maps key. Changing the recorder's permissions, background modes or foreground-service options needs `npx expo prebuild --clean` and an outdoor check on a real iPhone and Android phone. Apple Watch tie-in (phone GPS + watch): the phone still records; `hooks/useWatchRecordingBridge.ts` (mounted in `WatchWorkoutGate`, gated on `WatchConnectivity.isSupported()`) sends the session to the watch as `recordingState` through `updateRecordingState` (`sendMessage` when reachable, plus `transferUserInfo` for status changes only, never application context, which belongs to the check-in data; `sentAt` lets the watch drop an out-of-order queued copy; a `status: 'ended'` message tells the watch the recording was saved or discarded, with `discarded` true when the session left the store without a `savedEntryId`), refreshes distance and pace every 3s while recording, and applies the watch's `recordingControl` (`pause`/`resume`/`finish`/`lap`) to `gpsRecordingService` for the live session id only. A `lap` carries `at`, when the wearer pressed it (controls ride the queued transport and can arrive late); `markLap(at)` stores that time (a future or pre-recording time counts as now, a press within 3 s of an existing mark is dropped as a re-delivery, marks are kept sorted), and the state sent to the watch carries `lapCount` for the page's "Lap N" line. Heart-rate batches arrive as `recordingHeartRate` (`onRecordingHeartRate`, deduped by `clientId`), go to `addHeartRateSamples` (persisted beside the session, still accepted after Finish because the last batch is queued and lands late, cleared by `discardRecording`), and `gpsRecordingSave` posts them to the existing `watch-telemetry` route after the route upload (best effort: a failure is logged and never blocks the save; fewer than two readings are skipped). The current reading is also shown live: the watch puts the newest one beside the status on `RecordingView` (`RecordingStore.liveHeartRate`, cleared after 15 s without a new reading or when the recording stops being live) and sends it to the phone as the same live-only `liveHeartRate` message the strength workout uses, with the recording's id as `sessionId` and an empty `exerciseEntryId` (`WatchSessionManager.sendRecordingLiveHeartRate`, at most every 3 s and only while the phone is reachable; never queued). `useWatchRecordingBridge` takes it for the live session into `liveHeartRateStore`, and `RecordActivityScreen` shows a Heart rate tile through `useRecordingHeartRate` (hidden once the reading is 15 s old, so a watch that goes quiet does not leave a stale number). No new message type, so nothing to rename in four places. On the watch, `RecordingStore` mirrors the state and `RecordingView` shows it on the Workout page (`WorkoutView`, after a strength workout and before the idle picker; `ContentView` keeps the Workout page showing and lands on it while `RecordingStore.isActive`, as for a strength workout; the old `WatchPage.recording` page is no longer put in the list); `RecordingHealthKitController` runs an `HKWorkoutSession` for heart-rate sampling only (skipped while a strength workout owns the session) and stamps `SparkyFitnessSessionId` so the saved HKWorkout is not imported back. It writes nothing to Apple Health until the phone decides: on Finish the collection stops but the workout is held (`Outcome.hold`, `heldFor`), and the phone's `ended` message then finishes it (`resolveHeld(save: true)`) or discards it, so a discarded recording never reaches Health (a workout already written cannot be reliably removed: deleting the `HKWorkout` leaves attached samples). An older phone build that sends no `discarded` is treated as saved, and a watch app that is relaunched before the phone decides loses the held workout (the diary entry is unaffected). Message type and key strings (`recordingState`, `recordingControl`, `recordingHeartRate`) are duplicated in `index.ts`, `WatchConnectivityModule.swift`, `ContextPayloadMapper`/`OutboundPayloads` and `WatchSessionManager.route`; rename all or none. The watch Swift has not been compiled outside Xcode; verify on a real watch after `npx expo prebuild --clean`.
- GPS activity recording (#125): `RecordActivityScreen` (root-stack `RecordActivity`, opened from `AddSheet`'s Record Activity row) records a walk, run or ride. `services/gpsRecordingService.ts` owns the session: an `expo-location` task registered at module scope (`index.js` imports it, so a location fix can wake a killed app) feeds fixes through `acceptFix` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts` (accuracy, impossible-speed, out-of-order and standing-still filtering) and writes accepted points to AsyncStorage in 100-point chunks as they arrive, so a killed app or a dropped connection loses nothing; `hydrate()` restores them, and the screen reads live state through `useGpsRecording()`. The screen offers Discard (with a confirmation) while recording or paused as well as when finished. Leaving the screen does not hide a recording: while no strength workout is active, `ActiveWorkoutBar` shows it above the dock (`useRecordingBarContent`: clock, distance and pace, discard on the left, pause/resume on the right, a check that opens the screen when finished; the bar is hidden on `RecordActivity` itself and `useActiveWorkoutBarPadding` reserves room through the cheap `useHasGpsRecording`). Indoor sessions (`session.indoor`, chosen with the Outdoor/Indoor control before Start) skip the location permission and the task entirely: `startRecording({ indoor: true })` never touches `expo-location`, pause and resume only flip the status, the Auto-pause switch is hidden and sent off (there are no fixes for it to judge by), and the screen shows the clock (plus watch heart rate) with no map or pace. On Finish the screen asks for an optional distance typed in the user's distance unit, and `saveRecordedActivity(session, points, unit, indoorDistanceKm)` logs it with no track upload under `Indoor Walking`/`Indoor Running`/`Indoor Cycling` (never `Treadmill Walk`: shared `activitySport` reads `treadmill` as running, so a walk would match two sports and fall back to `other`). On iPhone and Android the distance field is prefilled from the step counter: `useIndoorDistanceEstimate` calls `services/stepDistance.estimateIndoorDistance`, which reads the steps over the session window from the system pedometer (`services/stepCounter`; iOS queries the window after Finish and asks for Motion & Fitness the first time; Android stores the hardware counter at Start in `stepCounterAtStart` via `modules/step-counter` and subtracts it at Finish, so the app can be killed in between, and asks for Activity recognition at the start of an indoor walk or run only), scales them by the moving share of the window because pauses are only a total, and multiplies by the learned stride (`services/strideCalibration`, AsyncStorage on this device only, walk and run kept apart, blended with `utils/stride.blendStride`) or a typical default. The screen says whether the stride is learned or typical. A distance the person changes teaches the stride (`learnFromIndoorEntry`; an accepted estimate teaches nothing); a typed figure is a treadmill reading, so the treadmill correction (below) is taken off it first and the stride learns from the corrected distance, while the prefilled step estimate is never corrected, and a watch treadmill run teaches it when the distance is corrected on the run summary (`learnFromWatchRun` reads the phone's steps over the run's window from `watchIndoor.startedAt`/`endedAt`, so it only counts when the phone was carried), and a saved outdoor walk or run teaches it from its GPS distance when Motion & Fitness is already allowed (`learnFromOutdoorRecording`, which never prompts). Both run once, with the entry's first save, and never fail it. The prompt text is `motionUsagePermission` in `app.config.ts`, so changing it needs a prebuild. Whether the distance was entered or estimated is not stored on the entry (that needs a server field), and indoor sessions are not yet kept out of personal records. The watch still shows distance 0 and pace `—` for an indoor session and starts its HealthKit session as outdoor; nothing in the recording payload says indoor yet. `RecordActivityScreen` shows a spinner until that `hydrate()` resolves (so Start never flashes before a restored recording appears) and, for a live recording the screen did not start itself, a notice with its start time and a Discard button. Pause only flips the session status: the task keeps running and `ingestFixes` drops its fixes, because starting a location task again cannot be done from the background (iOS ignores a while-using start made out of sight, Android 12+ refuses the foreground service), which a resume sent from the watch with the phone locked would need. Resume restarts the task only if the OS dropped it. Auto-pause (`utils/autoPause.ts`, pure `stepAutoPause`) runs inside `ingestFixes` on the raw fixes before `acceptFix`, because standing-still jitter is filtered out of the track: stopped below a per-activity speed for 6 to 10 s pauses the session with `pausedAt` set to when the person stopped and `autoPaused: true`; moving above a higher speed for 2 to 3 s resumes it with a new `seg`, the pause counted up to when movement began. The location task drops the distance filter (`distanceInterval: 0`) only while auto-pause is on, because iOS delivers no fixes to a person standing still when one is set and the detector, which only advances on arriving fixes, would never reach its stop time; with auto-pause off it keeps the 2 m filter. A fix with no Doppler speed (iOS reports -1, common while standing still) falls back to speed from position, measured from the oldest fix in a 5 to 15 s trail and less the larger accuracy radius, so a phone drifting a few metres while its owner stands still reads as stopped; at Finish the app log gets one line counting the fixes auto-pause saw with a speed, without one and too inaccurate. Fixes with accuracy worse than 30 m are ignored, a manual pause is never auto-resumed (only `autoPaused` sessions are watched), and the toggle is `session.autoPause` set from the Record screen's switch (preference in AsyncStorage, on by default). While a recording runs, the screen shows compact Auto-pause and Voice cues switches for that session only (the preferences stay as they are): `setRecordingAutoPause(enabled, notification)` resets the detector, starts the running location task again with the matching distance filter (0 on, 2 m off; a failure is logged and the setting kept) and, when turned off while it holds the session auto-paused, resumes it (an indoor session stays off), and `setRecordingAudioCues(unit | undefined)` counts the splits already completed as said, so none is announced late. Start countdown: `RecordActivityScreen` holds `countdownLeft` (preference `countdownSeconds` from `COUNTDOWN_CHOICES`, off by default, stored beside the other recording preferences); Start with a countdown arms it, each second fires a selection haptic and (voice cues on) speaks the number, and at zero it calls `startRecording`. Nothing is recorded or requested until then, so the first-run location prompt follows the countdown, and Cancel leaves no session. Laps: the Lap button calls `markLap()` (recording only; presses within 3 s of the last are ignored) which stores epoch-ms marks in `session.laps`; `computeLaps(points, marks)` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts` turns them into splits (interpolating distance and active time at each mark, a mark inside a pause taking the fix before it, the stretch after the last mark `partial`), the finished view lists them above the splits, and `saveRecordedActivity` uploads them as the entry's lap windows instead of the automatic splits when any exist. `splitBars` draws the per-split pace bars and picks the fastest (never a partial). Intervals: `utils/intervals.ts` is the pure side (`IntervalPlan` = a style, `runWalk` or `fastEasy`, and timed `warmup`/`work`/`recovery`/`cooldown` steps; `buildIntervalPlan` clamps and builds one from options, `INTERVAL_PRESETS` are the built-in plans, `intervalPosition(plan, activeSeconds)` says where a plan stands). `startRecording({ intervals })` stores it as `session.intervals = { plan, cued }` (`cued` is the last step started, so a restored session never repeats one); `advanceIntervals` runs after each batch of fixes in `ingestFixes` and from `tickIntervals()` (the screen calls it every second while open), against `elapsedSeconds`, so paused time never counts. A new step buzzes (`fireImpactHaptic`), is spoken when voice cues are on (`intervalCue`; the first step replaces the "Recording started" cue) and is marked as a lap at its exact start (`session.laps`, deduped against the 3 s gap), so the saved activity is cut into the work and recovery stretches by the existing lap path; steps passed in one go are all marked but only the latest is spoken. UI: `components/recording/IntervalSetup.tsx` (picker plus custom steppers; the screen owns the choice) and `IntervalCard.tsx` (live step). On a phone recording the watch is told the step (`intervalIndex/Label/Remaining/Next` in `WatchRecordingStatePayload`; `useWatchRecordingBridge` re-sends durably when the step changes, `RecordingStore` buzzes via `Haptics.step()`); watch-only recordings follow no plan. Programs: `utils/runPrograms.ts` is a list of workouts, each an `IntervalPlan` (`RUN_PROGRAMS`: `walkFit` (8 weeks of brisk walking, `activity: 'walk'` so Record starts a walk and "Count it" matches a walk; `programActivity(id)` gives it), `walk10k` (10 weeks of brisk walking to a 10K walk, also `activity: 'walk'`), `returnToRunning` (4), `gentle5k` (12), `beginner5k` (9 weeks of run/walk), `fiveToTenK` (8), `faster5k` (6, speed work in `fastEasy` style), `beginner10k` (12), `faster10k` (8, speed work), `tenKToHalf` (10), `halfMarathon` (12), `fasterHalf` (10, speed work), `beginnerMarathon` (18), `marathon` (16) and `advancedMarathon` (16, five runs a week, speed work). `tenKToHalf`, `halfMarathon`, `fasterHalf` and `beginnerMarathon` also have a four-runs-a-week twin (`<id>4`, `variantOf` set); `PICKER_PROGRAMS` is the list the picker shows, and `RunProgramsScreen` puts a `SegmentedControl` on those cards (`programVersions(id)`) to choose 3 or 4 runs, starting the chosen id. A variant needs its own name, description and structure strings; the faster plans use `stridesRun`, `hills`, `tempo` and `marathonPaceLong` in `runPrograms.ts` for strides, hill repeats, tempo runs and marathon-pace blocks, built from run/walk pieces between a warm-up and cool-down; the longer ones are continuous runs timed in minutes, so a plan step may be up to 4 hours (`MAX_PROGRAM_STEP_SECONDS`); `RunProgramCard` shows a picker, and switching a running program to another one asks first and restarts at week 1), with `programStatus`/`advanceProgress` over a `ProgramProgress` (`next` = index of the workout due). `services/runProgramService.ts` is the server-backed side (`services/api/runProgramApi.ts` over `/api/v2/run-program`, a `run_programs` row per user holding the person's own copy of the workouts, the place `next_index`, the `enabled` flag and a change log; the React Query copy under `runProgramQueryKey` is set from each response, and `useRunProgram` re-reads on every mount because the AI assistant may have changed it): `setProgramEnabled`, `restartProgram`, `skipProgramWorkout`, `setProgramPosition`, `completeProgramWorkout`. The status is built with shared `storedProgramStatus`, so adjusted workouts reach the card, the picker and the recording. `RunProgramsScreen` wraps each change in `change`, which toasts a failure (offline); a failed tick-off after a save is logged, not shown, since the activity itself is saved. Programs are opt-in: `ProgramProgress.enabled` is the Record card's switch (absent in older data means on), the card always shows the stored place, but `getProgramStatus()` and the screen's `programStatus` are null while it is off, so nothing is preselected, nothing is ticked off and the Diary shows nothing; turning it off keeps the place. Setup and management live on `RunProgramsScreen` (root-stack `RunPrograms`, from a Library row; with no program it lists the fifteen `PICKER_PROGRAMS` with Start program (which asks Start today / Pick a date, then calls `startProgram(id, startDay)`; null = today), with one it shows `RunProgramCard`: switch, today's workout, Skip, Start over, workout picker, run days and start day); a collapsible `RunProgramGuide` ("How the plans work": what a workout is made of, effort by the talk test and a 1-10 scale rather than pace, and a brisk walking pace of about 5 to 6 km/h; `programStructure` describes each program's weeks and also shows on each program card; `programRest` adds a per-program "Rest days" section, e.g. no back-to-back workouts for the beginner plans) sits on that screen in both states; the Record screen only has the slim `RunProgramSelect` (today's workout, with a Manage link), and the Diary shows `RunProgramBanner` through `ExerciseSummary`'s `programBanner` prop, styled like a scheduled workout plan: `programRunForDate` (shared `utils/runProgramSchedule.ts`) decides what a Diary day shows: with run days chosen it follows the calendar `projectRuns` lays out from the next workout due (one workout per chosen weekday starting today, the first day or tomorrow once today's is done, so a missed run moves everything after it along and nothing is ever skipped), so today shows the workout due ("Scheduled Today") and later days show what is coming ("Scheduled", no Start button); with none chosen it offers the next workout today only ("Up next"); nothing before the first day, after the goal day, once a workout was done today (`doneDay`), or when the program is off or finished. Saving a recording replaces `RecordActivity` with `RunSummary` (`RunSummaryScreen`): `handleSave` builds `RunSummaryParams` with `buildRunSummary` (`utils/runSummary.ts`: figures, a route thinned to 250 points, per-unit splits, the person's laps, average heart rate from `getHeartRateSamples`; plain data, since the recording is cleared right after) and, for a program workout, `nextProgramLine` (`utils/runProgramSummary.ts`: the next workout and the day `projectRuns` puts it on, read from the program the server now holds) with the place in the program. The screen shows the stat cards, the route (`RouteMap`), the program progress with a "How hard was that?" row (1 to 10, shown when `programSummary` returned `rate`, i.e. the workout was ticked off: `rateProgramWorkout` saves it to the server's `effort_log` through `POST /api/v2/run-program/effort`; 8 or more offers "Repeat this workout" = `setProgramPosition(index)`, 3 or less offers "Skip the next workout" = `index + 2` unless that is the last, and shared `suggestFromEffort` adds a note after two hard or two easy in a row; nothing changes unless a button is tapped) and a View program link, and the splits or laps through the shared `SplitRow`/`Stat` (`components/recording/SplitRow.tsx`, also used by the Record screen); Done goes back. A wrist-only run has no summary screen. `RunProgramBanner` also offers "Count it" when `findLoggedRun` (`utils/loggedRun.ts`: a non-preset entry named like a run, at least half the planned length or 10 minutes) is among today's entries, completing the due workout on the person's say-so (a run recorded some other way, such as the watch or an import). Every tick-off passes the diary entry it was done as (`completeProgramWorkout`'s `entryId`: the saved recording, or the counted run), which the server keeps in `completion_log`; `ProgramWorkoutBadge` (`useProgramWorkoutForEntry`, shared lookup `programWorkoutForEntry` in `utils/runProgramSummary.ts`) then shows "Part of your training program" with the workout label on `CardioSessionScreen` and `ActivityDetailScreen`. `RunProgramCalendar` on the screen lists the dates, the finish day (`projectedFinishDay`) and whether it fits before the goal day. The Record screen never chooses the workout for the person: it is offered on `RunProgramSelect` and in the `IntervalSetup` picker (`programLabel`), and chosen as the interval choice `'program'` only when the screen is opened with the `programWorkout` route param (the Diary card's Start and the program screen's Start today's workout), so a plain walk or run starts with no plan. For a walk or ride, `IntervalSetup` (given `activity`) leaves out the run/walk presets and style, the Record screen drops a run/walk preset when the activity changes, and `intervalPlan` turns a `runWalk` plan into `fastEasy`, so the card, spoken cues and watch steps say Fast and Easy rather than Run. While a recording exists the screen's header names it (`recordingTitle`: "Outdoor run", "Indoor walk", "Outdoor ride"), and outdoors `GpsSignalBadge` shows bars from the newest fix's accuracy (`useLatestGpsFix`, its own listener set in `gpsRecordingService` so the bars do not re-render the screen; `gpsSignalLevel` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts`, the watch's thresholds). The watch-run live screen is titled "Run on your watch" or "Walk on your watch". `startRecording({ program })` stores `session.program = { id, index }`, and `handleSave` calls `completeProgramWorkout` only when `planCompleted(session)`, so a run cut short is offered again. `setProgramPosition(index)` lets the person jump to any workout (the card's "Choose a different workout" picker lists the weeks, finished runs ticked), clamped to the program.
