Schedule: the weekdays the workout shows in the Diary (`run_days`, 0 = Sunday, empty = every day), the first day it shows (`start_date`, null = from today), the goal day (`end_date`, null = none; nothing is scheduled after it and the screen says whether the program fits before it) and the last local day a workout was completed (`last_done_date`, sent as `day` by `completeProgramWorkout`) are columns on the same `run_programs` row, like a workout plan's schedule, so they follow the person between devices; `useRunProgram` returns them as `schedule` and `doneDay`, `setProgramSchedule` writes them, and `RunProgramCard` edits them (day chips; a start date picked from a calendar, today unless one is set). Switching programs keeps the schedule. There are no run notifications; `cancelLegacyRunReminders` (`services/legacyRunReminders.ts`, run at startup) cancels weekly reminders an earlier build scheduled and deletes their stored key. An indoor session has no location task, so the plan moves on while the record screen is open and catches up when it is opened again. Voice cues (`session.audioCues` = `'km'`/`'miles'`, off by default, set from the Record screen's switch via `getRecordingPreferences`/`setRecordingPreference`): `ingestFixes` calls `cueSplit` after new points, which runs `computeSplits` and speaks only the latest newly completed full split (`session.cuedSplits` is persisted so a restore never repeats one); start, manual pause/resume, auto-pause/resume and finish speak through `services/recordingCues.ts` (`Speech.speak` with `useApplicationAudioSession`, each cue replacing the one before; it reuses `beginCueDucking`/`endCueDucking`, so ducking follows `duckMusicDuringCues`). The voice is `resolveSpeechVoice` (`services/speechVoice.ts`), shared with guided workouts: the installed voices are read ahead (`loadSpeechVoices`, called by `beginRecordingCues` and `speakGuided`), then it returns the person's pick (`guidedVoiceId`, one device-local setting for both) while still installed, otherwise the best Premium/Enhanced voice for the app language (`bestVoiceId` in `utils/speechVoices.ts`), otherwise undefined (system default, and also until the voices have loaded, so a stale id never reaches `expo-speech`, which throws on an unknown iOS identifier). `expo-speech` reports an iOS Premium voice as "Default" (it only maps `.enhanced`), so `voiceTier` also reads `.premium.` / `.enhanced.` from Apple's identifier and drops `com.apple.speech.synthesis.voice.*` novelty voices; Siri voices are not exposed to apps. The picker is `useSpeechVoiceChoices`, rendered by `RecordingVoiceCard` (Record screen, while voice cues are on) and `GuidedWorkoutSettingsSection`. The words come from `utils/recordingCues.ts` with an injected `t` (durations are spelled out, since a speech engine reads "5:24" as a time of day). The audio session is `startRecordingCueSession()` in `sounds.ts` (silent-switch override plus iOS `shouldPlayInBackground`): the location task already keeps the app running on iOS, so no keep-alive track is used. Each resume starts a new `seg`, so the jump across a pause is never distance or time. The first fix of a stretch (the start, or after a resume) must be within `STRETCH_START_MAX_ACCURACY_M` (25 m), tighter than the 50 m cutoff for the rest, so a cold-start fix does not put a spike at the head of the route. Coaching audio (#250): on top of splits and plan steps, `advanceCoaching` (next to `advanceIntervals`, from each fix batch and `tickIntervals`) speaks the cues `nextCoachingCues` (`utils/coachingCues.ts`, pure) says are due, only with voice cues on and at most one a tick (the rest are marked said): "three, two, one" in the last three seconds of such a step (one utterance, `stepCountdownCue`; it makes the ten-second warning of that step stale, so both are marked said together) after a ten-second warning before a step of 30 s or more that names the next step, a halfway call and a last-minute call for plan workouts of 5 and 3 minutes or more, a milestone word when the recorded distance passes 5K, 10K, a half marathon or a marathon (`RACE_MILESTONES_METERS`, outdoors only, whatever the unit; the furthest one after a gap), and a spoken check-in (time, distance and average pace; time only indoors) every `session.checkInMinutes` (the `CHECK_IN_CHOICES` 0, 5, 10 or 15, set in `CheckInCard` before Start, changed mid-run with `setRecordingCheckIn`, remembered as a preference). `session.coaching` holds what was said so a restore never repeats it (a state saved before the countdown and milestones existed is read with their defaults). A split announced at the same fix is spoken first, in the same breath as the coaching cue (`advanceCoaching(now, splitText)`), because a second utterance replaces the first; the countdown alone skips that, as it is for this second only. A program workout adds two sentences (`programSpokenCues` in `utils/programCues.ts`, already in the app language): `start` ("Week 2, run 3. 25 minutes.", plus "Eased for today." when its running time is 10% or more under the original) spoken in the same breath as the first step, and `done` ("Workout 4 of 27 done. Next up, Week 2, run 4.", or "Program complete. Well done.") added to the finish cue once every step of the plan has run for its full unpaused duration (a run stopped early gets none). The phone service reads them from `session.program`/`session.intervals`; the watch gets them as `startText`/`doneText` on the plan (`buildWatchRunPlans`, `buildWatchStartPlan`) and speaks them as they are. Splits also say how much faster or slower they were than the split before when it differs by 5 s or more (`splitTrendNote`). A watch run speaks the same cues: `Domain/WatchCoaching.swift` is the Swift port of `nextCoachingCues` (constants and cue order must stay in step), `WatchRunStore.cueCoachingIfDue` runs on its 1 s timer (which now also runs for a run with a check-in interval but no plan, and for any outdoor run, for the milestones) and speaks through `WatchRunCues` (`stepCountdownText` (spoken with `interrupt`), `milestoneText`, `stepWarningText`, `halfwayText`, `lastMinuteText`, `checkInText`, English like the other watch cues; check-ins give only the time indoors). The interval comes from `runStart` (`checkInMinutes`, sent with voice cues on) or, for a run begun on the wrist, the context's `runCheckInMinutes`; it lives in `WatchRunSettings.checkInMinutes` (optional, so older snapshots decode), and after a relaunch `WatchCoachingState.resumed` counts what has passed as said. Only while-using permission is requested: iOS keeps recording through `UIBackgroundModes: location` set in `app.config.ts`, Android through the plugin's foreground service, so `ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` stays off (Google Play reviews it closely). `utils/gpsRecording.ts` is the pure maths (distance, pace, smoothed elevation gain/loss, per-km/mile splits); `services/gpsRecordingSave.ts` finds or creates the Walking/Running/Cycling cardio exercise, creates a normal entry with `createExerciseEntry` (no `calories_burned`, so the server estimates it), then posts the route and split windows to `POST /api/exercise-entries/:id/gps-track` (`attachExerciseEntryGpsTrack`). It does NOT go through `/api/health-data`, whose per-source delete-then-insert would wipe other recorded activities on the same day. The entry id is kept on the session, so a failed track upload retries without logging the activity twice, and the server replaces an existing track on re-post. The live map reuses `RouteMap`, so Android shows a plain line without a Google Maps key. Changing the recorder's permissions, background modes or foreground-service options needs `npx expo prebuild --clean` and an outdoor check on a real iPhone and Android phone. Apple Watch tie-in (phone GPS + watch): the phone still records; `hooks/useWatchRecordingBridge.ts` (mounted in `WatchWorkoutGate`, gated on `WatchConnectivity.isSupported()`) sends the session to the watch as `recordingState` through `updateRecordingState` (`sendMessage` when reachable, plus `transferUserInfo` for status changes only, never application context, which belongs to the check-in data; `sentAt` lets the watch drop an out-of-order queued copy; a `status: 'ended'` message tells the watch the recording was saved or discarded, with `discarded` true when the session left the store without a `savedEntryId`), refreshes distance and pace every 3s while recording, and applies the watch's `recordingControl` (`pause`/`resume`/`finish`/`lap`) to `gpsRecordingService` for the live session id only. A `lap` carries `at`, when the wearer pressed it (controls ride the queued transport and can arrive late); `markLap(at)` stores that time (a future or pre-recording time counts as now, a press within 3 s of an existing mark is dropped as a re-delivery, marks are kept sorted), and the state sent to the watch carries `lapCount` for the page's "Lap N" line. Heart-rate batches arrive as `recordingHeartRate` (`onRecordingHeartRate`, deduped by `clientId`), go to `addHeartRateSamples` (persisted beside the session, still accepted after Finish because the last batch is queued and lands late, cleared by `discardRecording`), and `gpsRecordingSave` posts them to the existing `watch-telemetry` route after the route upload (best effort: a failure is logged and never blocks the save; fewer than two readings are skipped). The current reading is also shown live: the watch puts the newest one beside the status on `RecordingView` (`RecordingStore.liveHeartRate`, cleared after 15 s without a new reading or when the recording stops being live) and sends it to the phone as the same live-only `liveHeartRate` message the strength workout uses, with the recording's id as `sessionId` and an empty `exerciseEntryId` (`WatchSessionManager.sendRecordingLiveHeartRate`, at most every 3 s and only while the phone is reachable; never queued). `useWatchRecordingBridge` takes it for the live session into `liveHeartRateStore`, and `RecordActivityScreen` shows a Heart rate tile through `useRecordingHeartRate` (hidden once the reading is 15 s old, so a watch that goes quiet does not leave a stale number). No new message type, so nothing to rename in four places. On the watch, `RecordingStore` mirrors the state and `RecordingView` shows it on the Workout page (`WorkoutView`, after a strength workout and before the idle picker; `ContentView` keeps the Workout page showing and lands on it while `RecordingStore.isActive`, as for a strength workout; the old `WatchPage.recording` page is no longer put in the list); `RecordingHealthKitController` runs an `HKWorkoutSession` for heart-rate sampling only (skipped while a strength workout owns the session) and stamps `SparkyFitnessSessionId` so the saved HKWorkout is not imported back. It writes nothing to Apple Health until the phone decides: on Finish the collection stops but the workout is held (`Outcome.hold`, `heldFor`), and the phone's `ended` message then finishes it (`resolveHeld(save: true)`) or discards it, so a discarded recording never reaches Health (a workout already written cannot be reliably removed: deleting the `HKWorkout` leaves attached samples). An older phone build that sends no `discarded` is treated as saved, and a watch app that is relaunched before the phone decides loses the held workout (the diary entry is unaffected). Message type and key strings (`recordingState`, `recordingControl`, `recordingHeartRate`) are duplicated in `index.ts`, `WatchConnectivityModule.swift`, `ContextPayloadMapper`/`OutboundPayloads` and `WatchSessionManager.route`; rename all or none. The watch Swift has not been compiled outside Xcode; verify on a real watch after `npx expo prebuild --clean`.
- GPS activity recording (#125): `RecordActivityScreen` (root-stack `RecordActivity`, opened from `AddSheet`'s Record Activity row) records a walk, run or ride. `services/gpsRecordingService.ts` owns the session: an `expo-location` task registered at module scope (`index.js` imports it, so a location fix can wake a killed app) feeds fixes through `acceptFix` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts` (accuracy, impossible-speed, out-of-order and standing-still filtering) and writes accepted points to AsyncStorage in 100-point chunks as they arrive, so a killed app or a dropped connection loses nothing; `hydrate()` restores them, and the screen reads live state through `useGpsRecording()`. The screen offers Discard (with a confirmation) while recording or paused as well as when finished. Leaving the screen does not hide a recording: while no strength workout is active, `ActiveWorkoutBar` shows it above the dock (`useRecordingBarContent`: clock, distance and pace, discard on the left, pause/resume on the right, a check that opens the screen when finished; the bar is hidden on `RecordActivity` itself and `useActiveWorkoutBarPadding` reserves room through the cheap `useHasGpsRecording`). Indoor sessions (`session.indoor`, chosen with the Outdoor/Indoor control before Start) skip the location permission and the task entirely: `startRecording({ indoor: true })` never touches `expo-location`, pause and resume only flip the status, the Auto-pause switch is hidden and sent off (there are no fixes for it to judge by), and the screen shows the clock (plus watch heart rate) with no map or pace. On Finish the screen asks for an optional distance typed in the user's distance unit, and `saveRecordedActivity(session, points, unit, indoorDistanceKm)` logs it with no track upload under `Indoor Walking`/`Indoor Running`/`Indoor Cycling` (never `Treadmill Walk`: shared `activitySport` reads `treadmill` as running, so a walk would match two sports and fall back to `other`). On iPhone and Android the distance field is prefilled from the step counter: `useIndoorDistanceEstimate` calls `services/stepDistance.estimateIndoorDistance`, which reads the steps over the session window from the system pedometer (`services/stepCounter`; iOS queries the window after Finish and asks for Motion & Fitness the first time; Android stores the hardware counter at Start in `stepCounterAtStart` via `modules/step-counter` and subtracts it at Finish, so the app can be killed in between, and asks for Activity recognition at the start of an indoor walk or run only), scales them by the moving share of the window because pauses are only a total, and multiplies by the learned stride (`services/strideCalibration`, AsyncStorage on this device only, walk and run kept apart, blended with `utils/stride.blendStride`) or a typical default. The screen says whether the stride is learned or typical. A distance the person changes teaches the stride (`learnFromIndoorEntry`; an accepted estimate teaches nothing); a typed figure is a treadmill reading, so the treadmill correction (below) is taken off it first and the stride learns from the corrected distance, while the prefilled step estimate is never corrected, and a watch treadmill run teaches it when the distance is corrected on the run summary (`learnFromWatchRun` reads the steps Apple Health holds over the run's window from `watchIndoor.startedAt`/`endedAt` (`getHealthStepsBetween`: the watch writes them there, so the phone can stay on the treadmill; Health access for steps is needed, never prompted) and falls back to the phone's pedometer), and a saved outdoor walk or run teaches it from its GPS distance when Motion & Fitness is already allowed (`learnFromOutdoorRecording`, which never prompts). Both run once, with the entry's first save, and never fail it. The prompt text is `motionUsagePermission` in `app.config.ts`, so changing it needs a prebuild. Whether the distance was entered or estimated is not stored on the entry (that needs a server field), and indoor sessions are not yet kept out of personal records. The watch still shows distance 0 and pace `—` for an indoor session and starts its HealthKit session as outdoor; nothing in the recording payload says indoor yet. `RecordActivityScreen` shows a spinner until that `hydrate()` resolves (so Start never flashes before a restored recording appears) and, for a live recording the screen did not start itself, a notice with its start time and a Discard button. Pause only flips the session status: the task keeps running and `ingestFixes` drops its fixes, because starting a location task again cannot be done from the background (iOS ignores a while-using start made out of sight, Android 12+ refuses the foreground service), which a resume sent from the watch with the phone locked would need. Resume restarts the task only if the OS dropped it. Auto-pause (`utils/autoPause.ts`, pure `stepAutoPause`) runs inside `ingestFixes` on the raw fixes before `acceptFix`, because standing-still jitter is filtered out of the track: stopped below a per-activity speed for 6 to 10 s pauses the session with `pausedAt` set to when the person stopped and `autoPaused: true`; moving above a higher speed for 2 to 3 s resumes it with a new `seg`, the pause counted up to when movement began. The location task drops the distance filter (`distanceInterval: 0`) only while auto-pause is on, because iOS delivers no fixes to a person standing still when one is set and the detector, which only advances on arriving fixes, would never reach its stop time; with auto-pause off it keeps the 2 m filter. A fix with no Doppler speed (iOS reports -1, common while standing still) falls back to speed from position, measured from the oldest fix in a 5 to 15 s trail and less the larger accuracy radius, so a phone drifting a few metres while its owner stands still reads as stopped; at Finish the app log gets one line counting the fixes auto-pause saw with a speed, without one and too inaccurate. Fixes with accuracy worse than 30 m are ignored, a manual pause is never auto-resumed (only `autoPaused` sessions are watched), and the toggle is `session.autoPause` set from the Record screen's switch (preference in AsyncStorage, on by default). While a recording runs, the screen shows compact Auto-pause and Voice cues switches for that session only (the preferences stay as they are): `setRecordingAutoPause(enabled, notification)` resets the detector, starts the running location task again with the matching distance filter (0 on, 2 m off; a failure is logged and the setting kept) and, when turned off while it holds the session auto-paused, resumes it (an indoor session stays off), and `setRecordingAudioCues(unit | undefined)` counts the splits already completed as said, so none is announced late. Start countdown: `RecordActivityScreen` holds `countdownLeft` (preference `countdownSeconds` from `COUNTDOWN_CHOICES`, off by default, stored beside the other recording preferences); Start with a countdown arms it, each second fires a selection haptic and (voice cues on) speaks the number, and at zero it calls `startRecording`. Nothing is recorded or requested until then, so the first-run location prompt follows the countdown, and Cancel leaves no session. Laps: the Lap button calls `markLap()` (recording only; presses within 3 s of the last are ignored) which stores epoch-ms marks in `session.laps`; `computeLaps(points, marks)` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts` turns them into splits (interpolating distance and active time at each mark, a mark inside a pause taking the fix before it, the stretch after the last mark `partial`), the finished view lists them above the splits, and `saveRecordedActivity` uploads them as the entry's lap windows instead of the automatic splits when any exist. `splitBars` draws the per-split pace bars and picks the fastest (never a partial). Intervals: `utils/intervals.ts` is the pure side (`IntervalPlan` = a style, `runWalk` or `fastEasy`, and timed `warmup`/`work`/`recovery`/`cooldown` steps; `buildIntervalPlan` clamps and builds one from options, `INTERVAL_PRESETS` are the built-in plans, `intervalPosition(plan, activeSeconds)` says where a plan stands). `startRecording({ intervals })` stores it as `session.intervals = { plan, cued }` (`cued` is the last step started, so a restored session never repeats one); `advanceIntervals` runs after each batch of fixes in `ingestFixes` and from `tickIntervals()` (the screen calls it every second while open), against `elapsedSeconds`, so paused time never counts. A new step buzzes (`fireImpactHaptic`), is spoken when voice cues are on (`intervalCue`; the first step replaces the "Recording started" cue) and is marked as a lap at its exact start (`session.laps`, deduped against the 3 s gap), so the saved activity is cut into the work and recovery stretches by the existing lap path; steps passed in one go are all marked but only the latest is spoken. UI: `components/recording/IntervalSetup.tsx` (picker plus custom steppers; the screen owns the choice) and `IntervalCard.tsx` (live step). On a phone recording the watch is told the step (`intervalIndex/Label/Remaining/Next` in `WatchRecordingStatePayload`; `useWatchRecordingBridge` re-sends durably when the step changes, `RecordingStore` buzzes via `Haptics.step()`); watch-only recordings follow no plan. Programs: `utils/runPrograms.ts` is a list of workouts, each an `IntervalPlan` (`RUN_PROGRAMS`: `walkFit` (8 weeks of brisk walking, `activity: 'walk'` so Record starts a walk and "Count it" matches a walk; `programActivity(id)` gives it), `walk10k` (10 weeks of brisk walking to a 10K walk, also `activity: 'walk'`), `returnToRunning` (4), `gentle5k` (12), `beginner5k` (9 weeks of run/walk), `fiveToTenK` (8), `faster5k` (6, speed work in `fastEasy` style), `beginner10k` (12), `faster10k` (8, speed work), `tenKToHalf` (10), `halfMarathon` (12), `fasterHalf` (10, speed work), `beginnerMarathon` (18), `marathon` (16) and `advancedMarathon` (16, five runs a week, speed work). `tenKToHalf`, `halfMarathon`, `fasterHalf` and `beginnerMarathon` also have a four-runs-a-week twin (`<id>4`, `variantOf` set); `PICKER_PROGRAMS` is the list the picker shows, and `RunProgramsScreen` puts a `SegmentedControl` on those cards (`programVersions(id)`) to choose 3 or 4 runs, starting the chosen id. A variant needs its own name, description and structure strings; the faster plans use `stridesRun`, `hills`, `tempo` and `marathonPaceLong` in `runPrograms.ts` for strides, hill repeats, tempo runs and marathon-pace blocks, built from run/walk pieces between a warm-up and cool-down; the longer ones are continuous runs timed in minutes, so a plan step may be up to 4 hours (`MAX_PROGRAM_STEP_SECONDS`); `RunProgramCard` shows a picker, and switching a running program to another one asks first and restarts at week 1), with `programStatus`/`advanceProgress` over a `ProgramProgress` (`next` = index of the workout due). `services/runProgramService.ts` is the server-backed side (`services/api/runProgramApi.ts` over `/api/v2/run-program`, a `run_programs` row per user holding the person's own copy of the workouts, the place `next_index`, the `enabled` flag and a change log; the React Query copy under `runProgramQueryKey` is set from each response, and `useRunProgram` re-reads on every mount because the AI assistant may have changed it): `setProgramEnabled`, `restartProgram`, `skipProgramWorkout`, `setProgramPosition`, `completeProgramWorkout`. The status is built with shared `storedProgramStatus`, so adjusted workouts reach the card, the picker and the recording. `RunProgramsScreen` wraps each change in `change`, which toasts a failure (offline); a failed tick-off after a save is logged, not shown, since the activity itself is saved. Programs are opt-in: `ProgramProgress.enabled` is the Record card's switch (absent in older data means on), the card always shows the stored place, but `getProgramStatus()` and the screen's `programStatus` are null while it is off, so nothing is preselected, nothing is ticked off and the Diary shows nothing; turning it off keeps the place. Setup and management live on `RunProgramsScreen` (root-stack `RunPrograms`, from a Library row; with no program it lists the fifteen `PICKER_PROGRAMS` with Start program (which asks Start today / Pick a date, then calls `startProgram(id, startDay)`; null = today), with one it shows `RunProgramCard`: switch, today's workout, Skip, Start over, workout picker, run days and start day); a collapsible `RunProgramGuide` ("How the plans work": what a workout is made of, effort by the talk test and a 1-10 scale rather than pace, and a brisk walking pace of about 5 to 6 km/h; `programStructure` describes each program's weeks and also shows on each program card; `programRest` adds a per-program "Rest days" section, e.g. no back-to-back workouts for the beginner plans) sits on that screen in both states; the Record screen only has the slim `RunProgramSelect` (today's workout, with a Manage link), and the Diary shows `RunProgramBanner` through `ExerciseSummary`'s `programBanner` prop, styled like a scheduled workout plan: `programRunForDate` (shared `utils/runProgramSchedule.ts`) decides what a Diary day shows: with run days chosen it follows the calendar `projectRuns` lays out from the next workout due (one workout per chosen weekday starting today, the first day or tomorrow once today's is done, so a missed run moves everything after it along and nothing is ever skipped), so today shows the workout due ("Scheduled Today") and later days show what is coming ("Scheduled", no Start button); with none chosen it offers the next workout today only ("Up next"); nothing before the first day, after the goal day, once a workout was done today (`doneDay`), or when the program is off or finished. Saving a recording replaces `RecordActivity` with `RunSummary` (`RunSummaryScreen`): `handleSave` builds `RunSummaryParams` with `buildRunSummary` (`utils/runSummary.ts`: figures, a route thinned to 250 points, per-unit splits, the person's laps, average heart rate from `getHeartRateSamples`; plain data, since the recording is cleared right after) and, for a program workout, `nextProgramLine` (`utils/runProgramSummary.ts`: the next workout and the day `projectRuns` puts it on, read from the program the server now holds) with the place in the program. The screen shows the stat cards, the route (`RouteMap`), the program progress with a "How hard was that?" row (1 to 10, shown when `programSummary` returned `rate`, i.e. the workout was ticked off: `rateProgramWorkout` saves it to the server's `effort_log` through `POST /api/v2/run-program/effort`; 8 or more offers "Repeat this workout" = `setProgramPosition(index)`, 3 or less offers "Skip the next workout" = `index + 2` unless that is the last, and shared `suggestFromEffort` adds a note after two hard or two easy in a row; nothing changes unless a button is tapped) and a View program link, and the splits or laps through the shared `SplitRow`/`Stat` (`components/recording/SplitRow.tsx`, also used by the Record screen); Done goes back. A wrist-only run has no summary screen. `RunProgramBanner` also offers "Count it" when `findLoggedRun` (`utils/loggedRun.ts`: a non-preset entry named like a run, at least half the planned length or 10 minutes) is among today's entries, completing the due workout on the person's say-so (a run recorded some other way, such as the watch or an import). Every tick-off passes the diary entry it was done as (`completeProgramWorkout`'s `entryId`: the saved recording, or the counted run), which the server keeps in `completion_log`; `ProgramWorkoutBadge` (`useProgramWorkoutForEntry`, shared lookup `programWorkoutForEntry` in `utils/runProgramSummary.ts`) then shows "Part of your training program" with the workout label on `CardioSessionScreen` and `ActivityDetailScreen`. `RunProgramCalendar` on the screen lists the dates, the finish day (`projectedFinishDay`) and whether it fits before the goal day. The Record screen never chooses the workout for the person: it is offered on `RunProgramSelect` and in the `IntervalSetup` picker (`programLabel`), and chosen as the interval choice `'program'` only when the screen is opened with the `programWorkout` route param (the Diary card's Start and the program screen's Start today's workout), so a plain walk or run starts with no plan. For a walk or ride, `IntervalSetup` (given `activity`) leaves out the run/walk presets and style, the Record screen drops a run/walk preset when the activity changes, and `intervalPlan` turns a `runWalk` plan into `fastEasy`, so the card, spoken cues and watch steps say Fast and Easy rather than Run. While a recording exists the screen's header names it (`recordingTitle`: "Outdoor run", "Indoor walk", "Outdoor ride"), and outdoors `GpsSignalBadge` shows bars from the newest fix's accuracy (`useLatestGpsFix`, its own listener set in `gpsRecordingService` so the bars do not re-render the screen; `gpsSignalLevel` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts`, the watch's thresholds). The watch-run live screen is titled "Run on your watch" or "Walk on your watch". `startRecording({ program })` stores `session.program = { id, index }`, and `handleSave` calls `completeProgramWorkout` only when `planCompleted(session)`, so a run cut short is offered again. `setProgramPosition(index)` lets the person jump to any workout (the card's "Choose a different workout" picker lists the weeks, finished runs ticked), clamped to the program. Schedule: the weekdays the workout shows in the Diary (`run_days`, 0 = Sunday, empty = every day), the first day it shows (`start_date`, null = from today), the goal day (`end_date`, null = none; nothing is scheduled after it and the screen says whether the program fits before it) and the last local day a workout was completed (`last_done_date`, sent as `day` by `completeProgramWorkout`) are columns on the same `run_programs` row, like a workout plan's schedule, so they follow the person between devices; `useRunProgram` returns them as `schedule` and `doneDay`, `setProgramSchedule` writes them, and `RunProgramCard` edits them (day chips; a start date picked from a calendar, today unless one is set). Switching programs keeps the schedule. There are no run notifications; `cancelLegacyRunReminders` (`services/legacyRunReminders.ts`, run at startup) cancels weekly reminders an earlier build scheduled and deletes their stored key. An indoor session has no location task, so the plan moves on while the record screen is open and catches up when it is opened again. Voice cues (`session.audioCues` = `'km'`/`'miles'`, off by default, set from the Record screen's switch via `getRecordingPreferences`/`setRecordingPreference`): `ingestFixes` calls `cueSplit` after new points, which runs `computeSplits` and speaks only the latest newly completed full split (`session.cuedSplits` is persisted so a restore never repeats one); start, manual pause/resume, auto-pause/resume and finish speak through `services/recordingCues.ts` (`Speech.speak` with `useApplicationAudioSession`, each cue replacing the one before; it reuses `beginCueDucking`/`endCueDucking`, so ducking follows `duckMusicDuringCues`). The voice is `resolveSpeechVoice` (`services/speechVoice.ts`), shared with guided workouts: the installed voices are read ahead (`loadSpeechVoices`, called by `beginRecordingCues` and `speakGuided`), then it returns the person's pick (`guidedVoiceId`, one device-local setting for both) while still installed, otherwise the best Premium/Enhanced voice for the app language (`bestVoiceId` in `utils/speechVoices.ts`), otherwise undefined (system default, and also until the voices have loaded, so a stale id never reaches `expo-speech`, which throws on an unknown iOS identifier). `expo-speech` reports an iOS Premium voice as "Default" (it only maps `.enhanced`), so `voiceTier` also reads `.premium.` / `.enhanced.` from Apple's identifier and drops `com.apple.speech.synthesis.voice.*` novelty voices; Siri voices are not exposed to apps. The picker is `useSpeechVoiceChoices`, rendered by `RecordingVoiceCard` (Record screen, while voice cues are on) and `GuidedWorkoutSettingsSection`. The words come from `utils/recordingCues.ts` with an injected `t` (durations are spelled out, since a speech engine reads "5:24" as a time of day). The audio session is `startRecordingCueSession()` in `sounds.ts` (silent-switch override plus iOS `shouldPlayInBackground`): the location task already keeps the app running on iOS, so no keep-alive track is used. Each resume starts a new `seg`, so the jump across a pause is never distance or time. The first fix of a stretch (the start, or after a resume) must be within `STRETCH_START_MAX_ACCURACY_M` (25 m), tighter than the 50 m cutoff for the rest, so a cold-start fix does not put a spike at the head of the route. Coaching audio (#250): on top of splits and plan steps, `advanceCoaching` (next to `advanceIntervals`, from each fix batch and `tickIntervals`) speaks the cues `nextCoachingCues` (`utils/coachingCues.ts`, pure) says are due, only with voice cues on and at most one a tick (the rest are marked said): "three, two, one" in the last three seconds of such a step (one utterance, `stepCountdownCue`; it makes the ten-second warning of that step stale, so both are marked said together) after a ten-second warning before a step of 30 s or more that names the next step, a halfway call and a last-minute call for plan workouts of 5 and 3 minutes or more, a milestone word when the recorded distance passes 5K, 10K, a half marathon or a marathon (`RACE_MILESTONES_METERS`, outdoors only, whatever the unit; the furthest one after a gap), and a spoken check-in (time, distance and average pace; time only indoors) every `session.checkInMinutes` (the `CHECK_IN_CHOICES` 0, 5, 10 or 15, set in `CheckInCard` before Start, changed mid-run with `setRecordingCheckIn`, remembered as a preference). `session.coaching` holds what was said so a restore never repeats it (a state saved before the countdown and milestones existed is read with their defaults). A split announced at the same fix is spoken first, in the same breath as the coaching cue (`advanceCoaching(now, splitText)`), because a second utterance replaces the first; the countdown alone skips that, as it is for this second only. A program workout adds two sentences (`programSpokenCues` in `utils/programCues.ts`, already in the app language): `start` ("Week 2, run 3. 25 minutes.", plus "Eased for today." when its running time is 10% or more under the original) spoken in the same breath as the first step, and `done` ("Workout 4 of 27 done. Next up, Week 2, run 4.", or "Program complete. Well done.") added to the finish cue once every step of the plan has run for its full unpaused duration (a run stopped early gets none). The phone service reads them from `session.program`/`session.intervals`; the watch gets them as `startText`/`doneText` on the plan (`buildWatchRunPlans`, `buildWatchStartPlan`) and speaks them as they are. Splits also say how much faster or slower they were than the split before when it differs by 5 s or more (`splitTrendNote`). A watch run speaks the same cues: `Domain/WatchCoaching.swift` is the Swift port of `nextCoachingCues` (constants and cue order must stay in step), `WatchRunStore.cueCoachingIfDue` runs on its 1 s timer (which now also runs for a run with a check-in interval but no plan, and for any outdoor run, for the milestones) and speaks through `WatchRunCues` (`stepCountdownText` (spoken with `interrupt`), `milestoneText`, `stepWarningText`, `halfwayText`, `lastMinuteText`, `checkInText`, English like the other watch cues; check-ins give only the time indoors). The interval comes from `runStart` (`checkInMinutes`, sent with voice cues on) or, for a run begun on the wrist, the context's `runCheckInMinutes`; it lives in `WatchRunSettings.checkInMinutes` (optional, so older snapshots decode), and after a relaunch `WatchCoachingState.resumed` counts what has passed as said. Only while-using permission is requested: iOS keeps recording through `UIBackgroundModes: location` set in `app.config.ts`, Android through the plugin's foreground service, so `ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` stays off (Google Play reviews it closely). `utils/gpsRecording.ts` is the pure maths (distance, pace, smoothed elevation gain/loss, per-km/mile splits); `services/gpsRecordingSave.ts` finds or creates the Walking/Running/Cycling cardio exercise, creates a normal entry with `createExerciseEntry` (no `calories_burned`, so the server estimates it), then posts the route and split windows to `POST /api/exercise-entries/:id/gps-track` (`attachExerciseEntryGpsTrack`). It does NOT go through `/api/health-data`, whose per-source delete-then-insert would wipe other recorded activities on the same day. The entry id is kept on the session, so a failed track upload retries without logging the activity twice, and the server replaces an existing track on re-post. The live map reuses `RouteMap`, so Android shows a plain line without a Google Maps key. Changing the recorder's permissions, background modes or foreground-service options needs `npx expo prebuild --clean` and an outdoor check on a real iPhone and Android phone. Apple Watch tie-in (phone GPS + watch): the phone still records; `hooks/useWatchRecordingBridge.ts` (mounted in `WatchWorkoutGate`, gated on `WatchConnectivity.isSupported()`) sends the session to the watch as `recordingState` through `updateRecordingState` (`sendMessage` when reachable, plus `transferUserInfo` for status changes only, never application context, which belongs to the check-in data; `sentAt` lets the watch drop an out-of-order queued copy; a `status: 'ended'` message tells the watch the recording was saved or discarded, with `discarded` true when the session left the store without a `savedEntryId`), refreshes distance and pace every 3s while recording, and applies the watch's `recordingControl` (`pause`/`resume`/`finish`/`lap`) to `gpsRecordingService` for the live session id only. A `lap` carries `at`, when the wearer pressed it (controls ride the queued transport and can arrive late); `markLap(at)` stores that time (a future or pre-recording time counts as now, a press within 3 s of an existing mark is dropped as a re-delivery, marks are kept sorted), and the state sent to the watch carries `lapCount` for the page's "Lap N" line. Heart-rate batches arrive as `recordingHeartRate` (`onRecordingHeartRate`, deduped by `clientId`), go to `addHeartRateSamples` (persisted beside the session, still accepted after Finish because the last batch is queued and lands late, cleared by `discardRecording`), and `gpsRecordingSave` posts them to the existing `watch-telemetry` route after the route upload (best effort: a failure is logged and never blocks the save; fewer than two readings are skipped). The current reading is also shown live: the watch puts the newest one beside the status on `RecordingView` (`RecordingStore.liveHeartRate`, cleared after 15 s without a new reading or when the recording stops being live) and sends it to the phone as the same live-only `liveHeartRate` message the strength workout uses, with the recording's id as `sessionId` and an empty `exerciseEntryId` (`WatchSessionManager.sendRecordingLiveHeartRate`, at most every 3 s and only while the phone is reachable; never queued). `useWatchRecordingBridge` takes it for the live session into `liveHeartRateStore`, and `RecordActivityScreen` shows a Heart rate tile through `useRecordingHeartRate` (hidden once the reading is 15 s old, so a watch that goes quiet does not leave a stale number). No new message type, so nothing to rename in four places. On the watch, `RecordingStore` mirrors the state and `RecordingView` shows it on the Workout page (`WorkoutView`, after a strength workout and before the idle picker; `ContentView` keeps the Workout page showing and lands on it while `RecordingStore.isActive`, as for a strength workout; the old `WatchPage.recording` page is no longer put in the list); `RecordingHealthKitController` runs an `HKWorkoutSession` for heart-rate sampling only (skipped while a strength workout owns the session) and stamps `SparkyFitnessSessionId` so the saved HKWorkout is not imported back. It writes nothing to Apple Health until the phone decides: on Finish the collection stops but the workout is held (`Outcome.hold`, `heldFor`), and the phone's `ended` message then finishes it (`resolveHeld(save: true)`) or discards it, so a discarded recording never reaches Health (a workout already written cannot be reliably removed: deleting the `HKWorkout` leaves attached samples). An older phone build that sends no `discarded` is treated as saved, and a watch app that is relaunched before the phone decides loses the held workout (the diary entry is unaffected). Message type and key strings (`recordingState`, `recordingControl`, `recordingHeartRate`) are duplicated in `index.ts`, `WatchConnectivityModule.swift`, `ContextPayloadMapper`/`OutboundPayloads` and `WatchSessionManager.route`; rename all or none. The watch Swift has not been compiled outside Xcode; verify on a real watch after `npx expo prebuild --clean`.
- GPS activity recording (#125): `RecordActivityScreen` (root-stack `RecordActivity`, opened from `AddSheet`'s Record Activity row) records a walk, run or ride. `services/gpsRecordingService.ts` owns the session: an `expo-location` task registered at module scope (`index.js` imports it, so a location fix can wake a killed app) feeds fixes through `acceptFix` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts` (accuracy, impossible-speed, out-of-order and standing-still filtering) and writes accepted points to AsyncStorage in 100-point chunks as they arrive, so a killed app or a dropped connection loses nothing; `hydrate()` restores them, and the screen reads live state through `useGpsRecording()`. The screen offers Discard (with a confirmation) while recording or paused as well as when finished. Leaving the screen does not hide a recording: while no strength workout is active, `ActiveWorkoutBar` shows it above the dock (`useRecordingBarContent`: clock, distance and pace, discard on the left, pause/resume on the right, a check that opens the screen when finished; the bar is hidden on `RecordActivity` itself and `useActiveWorkoutBarPadding` reserves room through the cheap `useHasGpsRecording`). Indoor sessions (`session.indoor`, chosen with the Outdoor/Indoor control before Start) skip the location permission and the task entirely: `startRecording({ indoor: true })` never touches `expo-location`, pause and resume only flip the status, the Auto-pause switch is hidden and sent off (there are no fixes for it to judge by), and the screen shows the clock (plus watch heart rate) with no map or pace. On Finish the screen asks for an optional distance typed in the user's distance unit, and `saveRecordedActivity(session, points, unit, indoorDistanceKm)` logs it with no track upload under `Indoor Walking`/`Indoor Running`/`Indoor Cycling` (never `Treadmill Walk`: shared `activitySport` reads `treadmill` as running, so a walk would match two sports and fall back to `other`). On iPhone and Android the distance field is prefilled from the step counter: `useIndoorDistanceEstimate` calls `services/stepDistance.estimateIndoorDistance`, which reads the steps over the session window from the system pedometer (`services/stepCounter`; iOS queries the window after Finish and asks for Motion & Fitness the first time; Android stores the hardware counter at Start in `stepCounterAtStart` via `modules/step-counter` and subtracts it at Finish, so the app can be killed in between, and asks for Activity recognition at the start of an indoor walk or run only), scales them by the moving share of the window because pauses are only a total, and multiplies by the learned stride (`services/strideCalibration`, AsyncStorage on this device only, walk and run kept apart, blended with `utils/stride.blendStride`) or a typical default. The screen says whether the stride is learned or typical. A distance the person changes teaches the stride (`learnFromIndoorEntry`; an accepted estimate teaches nothing), and a saved outdoor walk or run teaches it from its GPS distance when Motion & Fitness is already allowed (`learnFromOutdoorRecording`, which never prompts). Both run once, with the entry's first save, and never fail it. The same step count gives running form: `saveRecordedActivity` calls `measureForm` before creating the entry (`measureOutdoorForm` for a GPS recording, which never prompts; the indoor estimate's steps for an indoor session) and sends `avg_cadence` (steps per minute over moving time) and `stride_length_cm` (distance over steps; left off for implausible values) with `createExerciseEntry`; it is best effort and a failure just leaves the fields off, and a recording that paused gets neither, since the step window has no pause boundaries to isolate moving-time steps. Power is not recorded. `/api/exercise-stats/query` returns them as `avgCadence` and `strideLengthCm`, and `CardioSessionScreen` shows them as Avg cadence (spm) and Stride length (metres, or feet with the miles setting). The prompt text is `motionUsagePermission` in `app.config.ts`, so changing it needs a prebuild. Whether the distance was entered or estimated is not stored on the entry (that needs a server field), and indoor sessions are not yet kept out of personal records. The watch still shows distance 0 and pace `—` for an indoor session and starts its HealthKit session as outdoor; nothing in the recording payload says indoor yet. The recording screen shows a live Calories (est.) tile from `estimateRecordingKcal` (`utils/recordingCalories.ts`: 1.0 kcal/kg/km for an outdoor run, 0.5 for a walk, otherwise a MET-style rate over active time; body weight from `useBodyWeightKg`, hidden without one). It is only a guide: the saved entry's calories come from the server. `RecordActivityScreen` shows a spinner until that `hydrate()` resolves (so Start never flashes before a restored recording appears) and, for a live recording the screen did not start itself, a notice with its start time and a Discard button. Pause only flips the session status: the task keeps running and `ingestFixes` drops its fixes, because starting a location task again cannot be done from the background (iOS ignores a while-using start made out of sight, Android 12+ refuses the foreground service), which a resume sent from the watch with the phone locked would need. Resume restarts the task only if the OS dropped it. Auto-pause (`utils/autoPause.ts`, pure `stepAutoPause`) runs inside `ingestFixes` on the raw fixes before `acceptFix`, because standing-still jitter is filtered out of the track: stopped below a per-activity speed for 6 to 10 s pauses the session with `pausedAt` set to when the person stopped and `autoPaused: true`; moving above a higher speed for 2 to 3 s resumes it with a new `seg`, the pause counted up to when movement began. The location task drops the distance filter (`distanceInterval: 0`) only while auto-pause is on, because iOS delivers no fixes to a person standing still when one is set and the detector, which only advances on arriving fixes, would never reach its stop time; with auto-pause off it keeps the 2 m filter. A fix with no Doppler speed (iOS reports -1, common while standing still) falls back to speed from position, measured from the oldest fix in a 5 to 15 s trail and less the larger accuracy radius, so a phone drifting a few metres while its owner stands still reads as stopped; at Finish the app log gets one line counting the fixes auto-pause saw with a speed, without one and too inaccurate. Fixes with accuracy worse than 30 m are ignored, a manual pause is never auto-resumed (only `autoPaused` sessions are watched), and the toggle is `session.autoPause` set from the Record screen's switch (preference in AsyncStorage, on by default). While a recording runs, the screen shows compact Auto-pause and Voice cues switches for that session only (the preferences stay as they are): `setRecordingAutoPause(enabled, notification)` resets the detector, starts the running location task again with the matching distance filter (0 on, 2 m off; a failure is logged and the setting kept) and, when turned off while it holds the session auto-paused, resumes it (an indoor session stays off), and `setRecordingAudioCues(unit | undefined)` counts the splits already completed as said, so none is announced late. Start countdown: `RecordActivityScreen` holds `countdownLeft` (preference `countdownSeconds` from `COUNTDOWN_CHOICES`, off by default, stored beside the other recording preferences); Start with a countdown arms it, each second fires a selection haptic and (voice cues on) speaks the number, and at zero it calls `startRecording`. Nothing is recorded or requested until then, so the first-run location prompt follows the countdown, and Cancel leaves no session. Laps: the Lap button calls `markLap()` (recording only; presses within 3 s of the last are ignored) which stores epoch-ms marks in `session.laps`; `computeLaps(points, marks)` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts` turns them into splits (interpolating distance and active time at each mark, a mark inside a pause taking the fix before it, the stretch after the last mark `partial`), the finished view lists them above the splits, and `saveRecordedActivity` uploads them as the entry's lap windows instead of the automatic splits when any exist. `splitBars` draws the per-split pace bars and picks the fastest (never a partial). Intervals: `utils/intervals.ts` is the pure side (`IntervalPlan` = a style, `runWalk` or `fastEasy`, and timed `warmup`/`work`/`recovery`/`cooldown` steps; `buildIntervalPlan` clamps and builds one from options, `INTERVAL_PRESETS` are the built-in plans, `intervalPosition(plan, activeSeconds)` says where a plan stands). `startRecording({ intervals })` stores it as `session.intervals = { plan, cued }` (`cued` is the last step started, so a restored session never repeats one); `advanceIntervals` runs after each batch of fixes in `ingestFixes` and from `tickIntervals()` (the screen calls it every second while open), against `elapsedSeconds`, so paused time never counts. A new step buzzes (`fireImpactHaptic`), is spoken when voice cues are on (`intervalCue`; the first step replaces the "Recording started" cue) and is marked as a lap at its exact start (`session.laps`, deduped against the 3 s gap), so the saved activity is cut into the work and recovery stretches by the existing lap path; steps passed in one go are all marked but only the latest is spoken. UI: `components/recording/IntervalSetup.tsx` (picker plus custom steppers; the screen owns the choice) and `IntervalCard.tsx` (live step). On a phone recording the watch is told the step (`intervalIndex/Label/Remaining/Next` in `WatchRecordingStatePayload`; `useWatchRecordingBridge` re-sends durably when the step changes, `RecordingStore` buzzes via `Haptics.step()`); watch-only recordings follow no plan. Programs: `utils/runPrograms.ts` is a list of workouts, each an `IntervalPlan` (`RUN_PROGRAMS`: `walkFit` (8 weeks of brisk walking, `activity: 'walk'` so Record starts a walk and "Count it" matches a walk; `programActivity(id)` gives it), `walk10k` (10 weeks of brisk walking to a 10K walk, also `activity: 'walk'`), `returnToRunning` (4), `gentle5k` (12), `beginner5k` (9 weeks of run/walk), `fiveToTenK` (8), `zone2Base` (10 weeks of easy runs held to the person's Zone 2 heart rate: a `runWalk` plan of `zone2`-effort steps, a long run up to 70 minutes, a lighter fourth and eighth week, four strides after the first run in weeks 6, 7 and 9, and a zone check, the middle run of weeks 4, 8 and 10, flagged by `isZoneCheckWorkout` and noted by `ZoneCheckNote` on the program card and the Diary card), `faster5k` (6, speed work in `fastEasy` style), `beginner10k` (12), `faster10k` (8, speed work), `tenKToHalf` (10), `halfMarathon` (12), `fasterHalf` (10, speed work), `beginnerMarathon` (18), `marathon` (16) and `advancedMarathon` (16, five runs a week, speed work). `zone2Base`, `tenKToHalf`, `halfMarathon`, `fasterHalf` and `beginnerMarathon` also have a four-runs-a-week twin (`<id>4`, `variantOf` set); `PICKER_PROGRAMS` is the list the picker shows, and `RunProgramsScreen` puts a `SegmentedControl` on those cards (`programVersions(id)`) to choose 3 or 4 runs, starting the chosen id. A variant needs its own name, description and structure strings; the faster plans use `stridesRun`, `hills`, `tempo` and `marathonPaceLong` in `runPrograms.ts` for strides, hill repeats, tempo runs and marathon-pace blocks, built from run/walk pieces between a warm-up and cool-down; the longer ones are continuous runs timed in minutes, so a plan step may be up to 4 hours (`MAX_PROGRAM_STEP_SECONDS`); `RunProgramCard` shows a picker, and switching a running program to another one asks first and restarts at week 1), with `programStatus`/`advanceProgress` over a `ProgramProgress` (`next` = index of the workout due). `services/runProgramService.ts` is the server-backed side (`services/api/runProgramApi.ts` over `/api/v2/run-program`, a `run_programs` row per user holding the person's own copy of the workouts, the place `next_index`, the `enabled` flag and a change log; the React Query copy under `runProgramQueryKey` is set from each response, and `useRunProgram` re-reads on every mount because the AI assistant may have changed it): `setProgramEnabled`, `restartProgram`, `skipProgramWorkout`, `setProgramPosition`, `completeProgramWorkout`. The status is built with shared `storedProgramStatus`, so adjusted workouts reach the card, the picker and the recording. `RunProgramsScreen` wraps each change in `change`, which toasts a failure (offline); a failed tick-off after a save is logged, not shown, since the activity itself is saved. Programs are opt-in: `ProgramProgress.enabled` is the Record card's switch (absent in older data means on), the card always shows the stored place, but `getProgramStatus()` and the screen's `programStatus` are null while it is off, so nothing is preselected, nothing is ticked off and the Diary shows nothing; turning it off keeps the place. Setup and management live on `RunProgramsScreen` (root-stack `RunPrograms`, from a Library row; with no program it lists the sixteen `PICKER_PROGRAMS` with Start program (which asks Start today / Pick a date, then calls `startProgram(id, startDay)`; null = today), with one it shows `RunProgramCard`: switch, today's workout, Skip, Start over, workout picker, run days and start day); a collapsible `RunProgramGuide` ("How the plans work": what a workout is made of, effort by the talk test and a 1-10 scale rather than pace, and a brisk walking pace of about 5 to 6 km/h; `programStructure` describes each program's weeks and also shows on each program card; `programRest` adds a per-program "Rest days" section, e.g. no back-to-back workouts for the beginner plans) sits on that screen in both states; the Record screen only has the slim `RunProgramSelect` (today's workout, with a Manage link), and the Diary shows `RunProgramBanner` through `ExerciseSummary`'s `programBanner` prop, styled like a scheduled workout plan: `programRunForDate` (shared `utils/runProgramSchedule.ts`) decides what a Diary day shows: with run days chosen it follows the calendar `projectRuns` lays out from the next workout due (one workout per chosen weekday starting today, the first day or tomorrow once today's is done, so a missed run moves everything after it along and nothing is ever skipped), so today shows the workout due ("Scheduled Today") and later days show what is coming ("Scheduled", no Start button); with none chosen it offers the next workout today only ("Up next"); nothing before the first day, after the goal day, once a workout was done today (`doneDay`), or when the program is off or finished. Saving a recording replaces `RecordActivity` with `RunSummary` (`RunSummaryScreen`): `handleSave` builds `RunSummaryParams` with `buildRunSummary` (`utils/runSummary.ts`: figures, a route thinned to 250 points, per-unit splits, the person's laps, average heart rate from `getHeartRateSamples`; plain data, since the recording is cleared right after) and, for a program workout, `nextProgramLine` (`utils/runProgramSummary.ts`: the next workout and the day `projectRuns` puts it on, read from the program the server now holds) with the place in the program. The screen shows the stat cards, the route (`RouteMap`), the program progress with a "How hard was that?" row (1 to 10, shown when `programSummary` returned `rate`, i.e. the workout was ticked off: `rateProgramWorkout` saves it to the server's `effort_log` through `POST /api/v2/run-program/effort`; 8 or more offers "Repeat this workout" = `setProgramPosition(index)`, 3 or less offers "Skip the next workout" = `index + 2` unless that is the last, and shared `suggestFromEffort` adds a note after two hard or two easy in a row; nothing changes unless a button is tapped) and a View program link, and the splits or laps through the shared `SplitRow`/`Stat` (`components/recording/SplitRow.tsx`, also used by the Record screen); Done goes back. A wrist-only run has no summary screen. `RunProgramBanner` also offers "Count it" when `findLoggedRun` (`utils/loggedRun.ts`: a non-preset entry named like a run, at least half the planned length or 10 minutes) is among today's entries, completing the due workout on the person's say-so (a run recorded some other way, such as the watch or an import). Every tick-off passes the diary entry it was done as (`completeProgramWorkout`'s `entryId`: the saved recording, or the counted run), which the server keeps in `completion_log`; `ProgramWorkoutBadge` (`useProgramWorkoutForEntry`, shared lookup `programWorkoutForEntry` in `utils/runProgramSummary.ts`) then shows "Part of your training program" with the workout label on `CardioSessionScreen` and `ActivityDetailScreen`. `RunGoalsCard` tops that screen with the running goals (`useRunGoals`, `services/api/runGoalsApi.ts` over `/api/v2/run-goals`, query key `runGoalsQueryKey`, re-read on focus because a run saved elsewhere changes the week): a weekly distance target with this week's progress and a race with a day countdown, both optional and independent of the program; the card edits in place and converts between the display unit and the km the server stores, and Remove race clears the name, distance and day together (the server refuses a name or distance without a day). `RunProgramCalendar` on the screen lists the dates, the finish day (`projectedFinishDay`) and whether it fits before the goal day. The Record screen never chooses the workout for the person: it is offered on `RunProgramSelect` and in the `IntervalSetup` picker (`programLabel`), and chosen as the interval choice `'program'` only when the screen is opened with the `programWorkout` route param (the Diary card's Start and the program screen's Start today's workout), so a plain walk or run starts with no plan. For a walk or ride, `IntervalSetup` (given `activity`) leaves out the run/walk presets and style, the Record screen drops a run/walk preset when the activity changes, and `intervalPlan` turns a `runWalk` plan into `fastEasy`, so the card, spoken cues and watch steps say Fast and Easy rather than Run. While a recording exists the screen's header names it (`recordingTitle`: "Outdoor run", "Indoor walk", "Outdoor ride"), and outdoors `GpsSignalBadge` shows bars from the newest fix's accuracy (`useLatestGpsFix`, its own listener set in `gpsRecordingService` so the bars do not re-render the screen; `gpsSignalLevel` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts`, the watch's thresholds). The watch-run live screen is titled "Run on your watch" or "Walk on your watch". `startRecording({ program })` stores `session.program = { id, index }`, and `handleSave` calls `completeProgramWorkout` only when `planCompleted(session)`, so a run cut short is offered again. `setProgramPosition(index)` lets the person jump to any workout (the card's "Choose a different workout" picker lists the weeks, finished runs ticked), clamped to the program. Schedule: the weekdays the workout shows in the Diary (`run_days`, 0 = Sunday, empty = every day), the first day it shows (`start_date`, null = from today), the goal day (`end_date`, null = none; nothing is scheduled after it and the screen says whether the program fits before it) and the last local day a workout was completed (`last_done_date`, sent as `day` by `completeProgramWorkout`) are columns on the same `run_programs` row, like a workout plan's schedule, so they follow the person between devices; `useRunProgram` returns them as `schedule` and `doneDay`, `setProgramSchedule` writes them, and `RunProgramCard` edits them (day chips; a start date picked from a calendar, today unless one is set). Switching programs keeps the schedule. There are no run notifications; `cancelLegacyRunReminders` (`services/legacyRunReminders.ts`, run at startup) cancels weekly reminders an earlier build scheduled and deletes their stored key. An indoor session has no location task, so the plan moves on while the record screen is open and catches up when it is opened again. Voice cues (`session.audioCues` = `'km'`/`'miles'`, off by default, set from the Record screen's switch via `getRecordingPreferences`/`setRecordingPreference`): `ingestFixes` calls `cueSplit` after new points, which runs `computeSplits` and speaks only the latest newly completed full split (`session.cuedSplits` is persisted so a restore never repeats one); start, manual pause/resume, auto-pause/resume and finish speak through `services/recordingCues.ts` (`Speech.speak` with `useApplicationAudioSession`, each cue replacing the one before; it reuses `beginCueDucking`/`endCueDucking`, so ducking follows `duckMusicDuringCues`). The voice is `resolveSpeechVoice` (`services/speechVoice.ts`), shared with guided workouts: the installed voices are read ahead (`loadSpeechVoices`, called by `beginRecordingCues` and `speakGuided`), then it returns the person's pick (`guidedVoiceId`, one device-local setting for both) while still installed, otherwise the best Premium/Enhanced voice for the app language (`bestVoiceId` in `utils/speechVoices.ts`), otherwise undefined (system default, and also until the voices have loaded, so a stale id never reaches `expo-speech`, which throws on an unknown iOS identifier). `expo-speech` reports an iOS Premium voice as "Default" (it only maps `.enhanced`), so `voiceTier` also reads `.premium.` / `.enhanced.` from Apple's identifier and drops `com.apple.speech.synthesis.voice.*` novelty voices; Siri voices are not exposed to apps. The picker is `useSpeechVoiceChoices`, rendered by `RecordingVoiceCard` (Record screen, while voice cues are on) and `GuidedWorkoutSettingsSection`. The words come from `utils/recordingCues.ts` with an injected `t` (durations are spelled out, since a speech engine reads "5:24" as a time of day). The audio session is `startRecordingCueSession()` in `sounds.ts` (silent-switch override plus iOS `shouldPlayInBackground`): the location task already keeps the app running on iOS, so no keep-alive track is used. Each resume starts a new `seg`, so the jump across a pause is never distance or time. The first fix of a stretch (the start, or after a resume) must be within `STRETCH_START_MAX_ACCURACY_M` (25 m), tighter than the 50 m cutoff for the rest, so a cold-start fix does not put a spike at the head of the route. Coaching audio (#250): on top of splits and plan steps, `advanceCoaching` (next to `advanceIntervals`, from each fix batch and `tickIntervals`) speaks the cues `nextCoachingCues` (`utils/coachingCues.ts`, pure) says are due, only with voice cues on and at most one a tick (the rest are marked said): "three, two, one" in the last three seconds of such a step (one utterance, `stepCountdownCue`; it makes the ten-second warning of that step stale, so both are marked said together) after a ten-second warning before a step of 30 s or more that names the next step, a halfway call and a last-minute call for plan workouts of 5 and 3 minutes or more, a milestone word when the recorded distance passes 5K, 10K, a half marathon or a marathon (`RACE_MILESTONES_METERS`, outdoors only, whatever the unit; the furthest one after a gap), and a spoken check-in (time, distance and average pace; time only indoors) every `session.checkInMinutes` (the `CHECK_IN_CHOICES` 0, 5, 10 or 15, set in `CheckInCard` before Start, changed mid-run with `setRecordingCheckIn`, remembered as a preference). `session.coaching` holds what was said so a restore never repeats it (a state saved before the countdown and milestones existed is read with their defaults). A split announced at the same fix is spoken first, in the same breath as the coaching cue (`advanceCoaching(now, splitText)`), because a second utterance replaces the first; the countdown alone skips that, as it is for this second only. A program workout adds two sentences (`programSpokenCues` in `utils/programCues.ts`, already in the app language): `start` ("Week 2, run 3. 25 minutes.", plus "Eased for today." when its running time is 10% or more under the original) spoken in the same breath as the first step, and `done` ("Workout 4 of 27 done. Next up, Week 2, run 4.", or "Program complete. Well done.") added to the finish cue once every step of the plan has run for its full unpaused duration (a run stopped early gets none). The phone service reads them from `session.program`/`session.intervals`; the watch gets them as `startText`/`doneText` on the plan (`buildWatchRunPlans`, `buildWatchStartPlan`) and speaks them as they are. Splits also say how much faster or slower they were than the split before when it differs by 5 s or more (`splitTrendNote`). A watch run speaks the same cues: `Domain/WatchCoaching.swift` is the Swift port of `nextCoachingCues` (constants and cue order must stay in step), `WatchRunStore.cueCoachingIfDue` runs on its 1 s timer (which now also runs for a run with a check-in interval but no plan, and for any outdoor run, for the milestones) and speaks through `WatchRunCues` (`stepCountdownText` (spoken with `interrupt`), `milestoneText`, `stepWarningText`, `halfwayText`, `lastMinuteText`, `checkInText`, localized like the other watch cues; check-ins give only the time indoors). The interval comes from `runStart` (`checkInMinutes`, sent with voice cues on) or, for a run begun on the wrist, the context's `runCheckInMinutes`; it lives in `WatchRunSettings.checkInMinutes` (optional, so older snapshots decode), and after a relaunch `WatchCoachingState.resumed` counts what has passed as said. Only while-using permission is requested: iOS keeps recording through `UIBackgroundModes: location` set in `app.config.ts`, Android through the plugin's foreground service, so `ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` stays off (Google Play reviews it closely). `utils/gpsRecording.ts` is the pure maths (distance, pace, smoothed elevation gain/loss, per-km/mile splits); `services/gpsRecordingSave.ts` finds or creates the Walking/Running/Cycling cardio exercise, creates a normal entry with `createExerciseEntry` (no `calories_burned`, so the server estimates it), then posts the route and split windows to `POST /api/exercise-entries/:id/gps-track` (`attachExerciseEntryGpsTrack`). It does NOT go through `/api/health-data`, whose per-source delete-then-insert would wipe other recorded activities on the same day. The entry id is kept on the session, so a failed track upload retries without logging the activity twice, and the server replaces an existing track on re-post. The live map reuses `RouteMap`, so Android shows a plain line without a Google Maps key. Changing the recorder's permissions, background modes or foreground-service options needs `npx expo prebuild --clean` and an outdoor check on a real iPhone and Android phone. Apple Watch tie-in (phone GPS + watch): the phone still records; `hooks/useWatchRecordingBridge.ts` (mounted in `WatchWorkoutGate`, gated on `WatchConnectivity.isSupported()`) sends the session to the watch as `recordingState` through `updateRecordingState` (`sendMessage` when reachable, plus `transferUserInfo` for status changes only, never application context, which belongs to the check-in data; `sentAt` lets the watch drop an out-of-order queued copy; a `status: 'ended'` message tells the watch the recording was saved or discarded, with `discarded` true when the session left the store without a `savedEntryId`), refreshes distance and pace every 3s while recording, and applies the watch's `recordingControl` (`pause`/`resume`/`finish`/`lap`) to `gpsRecordingService` for the live session id only. A `lap` carries `at`, when the wearer pressed it (controls ride the queued transport and can arrive late); `markLap(at)` stores that time (a future or pre-recording time counts as now, a press within 3 s of an existing mark is dropped as a re-delivery, marks are kept sorted), and the state sent to the watch carries `lapCount` for the page's "Lap N" line. Heart-rate batches arrive as `recordingHeartRate` (`onRecordingHeartRate`, deduped by `clientId`), go to `addHeartRateSamples` (persisted beside the session, still accepted after Finish because the last batch is queued and lands late, cleared by `discardRecording`), and `gpsRecordingSave` posts them to the existing `watch-telemetry` route after the route upload (best effort: a failure is logged and never blocks the save; fewer than two readings are skipped). The current reading is also shown live: the watch puts the newest one beside the status on `RecordingView` (`RecordingStore.liveHeartRate`, cleared after 15 s without a new reading or when the recording stops being live) and sends it to the phone as the same live-only `liveHeartRate` message the strength workout uses, with the recording's id as `sessionId` and an empty `exerciseEntryId` (`WatchSessionManager.sendRecordingLiveHeartRate`, at most every 3 s and only while the phone is reachable; never queued). `useWatchRecordingBridge` takes it for the live session into `liveHeartRateStore`, and `RecordActivityScreen` shows a Heart rate tile through `useRecordingHeartRate` (hidden once the reading is 15 s old, so a watch that goes quiet does not leave a stale number). No new message type, so nothing to rename in four places. On the watch, `RecordingStore` mirrors the state and `RecordingView` shows it on the Workout page (`WorkoutView`, after a strength workout and before the idle picker; `ContentView` keeps the Workout page showing and lands on it while `RecordingStore.isActive`, as for a strength workout; the old `WatchPage.recording` page is no longer put in the list); `RecordingHealthKitController` runs an `HKWorkoutSession` for heart-rate sampling only (skipped while a strength workout owns the session) and stamps `SparkyFitnessSessionId` so the saved HKWorkout is not imported back. It writes nothing to Apple Health until the phone decides: on Finish the collection stops but the workout is held (`Outcome.hold`, `heldFor`), and the phone's `ended` message then finishes it (`resolveHeld(save: true)`) or discards it, so a discarded recording never reaches Health (a workout already written cannot be reliably removed: deleting the `HKWorkout` leaves attached samples). An older phone build that sends no `discarded` is treated as saved, and a watch app that is relaunched before the phone decides loses the held workout (the diary entry is unaffected). Message type and key strings (`recordingState`, `recordingControl`, `recordingHeartRate`) are duplicated in `index.ts`, `WatchConnectivityModule.swift`, `ContextPayloadMapper`/`OutboundPayloads` and `WatchSessionManager.route`; rename all or none. The watch Swift has not been compiled outside Xcode; verify on a real watch after `npx expo prebuild --clean`.
- GPS activity recording (#125): `RecordActivityScreen` (root-stack `RecordActivity`, opened from `AddSheet`'s Record Activity row) records a walk, run or ride. `services/gpsRecordingService.ts` owns the session: an `expo-location` task registered at module scope (`index.js` imports it, so a location fix can wake a killed app) feeds fixes through `acceptFix` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts` (accuracy, impossible-speed, out-of-order and standing-still filtering) and writes accepted points to AsyncStorage in 100-point chunks as they arrive, so a killed app or a dropped connection loses nothing; `hydrate()` restores them, and the screen reads live state through `useGpsRecording()`. The screen offers Discard (with a confirmation) while recording or paused as well as when finished. Leaving the screen does not hide a recording: while no strength workout is active, `ActiveWorkoutBar` shows it above the dock (`useRecordingBarContent`: clock, distance and pace, discard on the left, pause/resume on the right, a check that opens the screen when finished; the bar is hidden on `RecordActivity` itself and `useActiveWorkoutBarPadding` reserves room through the cheap `useHasGpsRecording`). Indoor sessions (`session.indoor`, chosen with the Outdoor/Indoor control before Start) skip the location permission and the task entirely: `startRecording({ indoor: true })` never touches `expo-location`, pause and resume only flip the status, the Auto-pause switch is hidden and sent off (there are no fixes for it to judge by), and the screen shows the clock (plus watch heart rate) with no map or pace. On Finish the screen asks for an optional distance typed in the user's distance unit, and `saveRecordedActivity(session, points, unit, indoorDistanceKm)` logs it with no track upload under `Indoor Walking`/`Indoor Running`/`Indoor Cycling` (never `Treadmill Walk`: shared `activitySport` reads `treadmill` as running, so a walk would match two sports and fall back to `other`). On iPhone and Android the distance field is prefilled from the step counter: `useIndoorDistanceEstimate` calls `services/stepDistance.estimateIndoorDistance`, which reads the steps over the session window from the system pedometer (`services/stepCounter`; iOS queries the window after Finish and asks for Motion & Fitness the first time; Android stores the hardware counter at Start in `stepCounterAtStart` via `modules/step-counter` and subtracts it at Finish, so the app can be killed in between, and asks for Activity recognition at the start of an indoor walk or run only), scales them by the moving share of the window because pauses are only a total, and multiplies by the learned stride (`services/strideCalibration`, AsyncStorage on this device only, walk and run kept apart, blended with `utils/stride.blendStride`) or a typical default. The screen says whether the stride is learned or typical. A distance the person changes teaches the stride (`learnFromIndoorEntry`; an accepted estimate teaches nothing), and a saved outdoor walk or run teaches it from its GPS distance when Motion & Fitness is already allowed (`learnFromOutdoorRecording`, which never prompts). Both run once, with the entry's first save, and never fail it. The same step count gives running form: `saveRecordedActivity` calls `measureForm` before creating the entry (`measureOutdoorForm` for a GPS recording, which never prompts; the indoor estimate's steps for an indoor session) and sends `avg_cadence` (steps per minute over moving time) and `stride_length_cm` (distance over steps; left off for implausible values) with `createExerciseEntry`; it is best effort and a failure just leaves the fields off, and a recording that paused gets neither, since the step window has no pause boundaries to isolate moving-time steps. Power is not recorded. `/api/exercise-stats/query` returns them as `avgCadence` and `strideLengthCm`, and `CardioSessionScreen` shows them as Avg cadence (spm) and Stride length (metres, or feet with the miles setting). The prompt text is `motionUsagePermission` in `app.config.ts`, so changing it needs a prebuild. Whether the distance was entered or estimated is not stored on the entry (that needs a server field), and indoor sessions are not yet kept out of personal records. The watch still shows distance 0 and pace `—` for an indoor session and starts its HealthKit session as outdoor; nothing in the recording payload says indoor yet. The recording screen shows a live Calories (est.) tile: `estimateRecordingKcal` (`utils/recordingCalories.ts`) is the logged exercise's calories per hour over active time, the same rule the server applies to a saved entry that sends no `calories_burned` (rate times minutes). The rate comes from `recordingCaloriesPerHour` (`services/gpsRecordingSave.ts`, via `useRecordingCaloriesPerHour`): the library Walking/Running/Cycling exercise's `calories_per_hour` when it is a cardio exercise, the fallback rate when the library has none, and null (tile hidden) for a non-cardio exercise, which the server works out from weight, age and sex. A watch run's calories are the watch's measured energy instead. `RecordActivityScreen` shows a spinner until that `hydrate()` resolves (so Start never flashes before a restored recording appears) and, for a live recording the screen did not start itself, a notice with its start time and a Discard button. Pause only flips the session status: the task keeps running and `ingestFixes` drops its fixes, because starting a location task again cannot be done from the background (iOS ignores a while-using start made out of sight, Android 12+ refuses the foreground service), which a resume sent from the watch with the phone locked would need. Resume restarts the task only if the OS dropped it. Auto-pause (`utils/autoPause.ts`, pure `stepAutoPause`) runs inside `ingestFixes` on the raw fixes before `acceptFix`, because standing-still jitter is filtered out of the track: stopped below a per-activity speed for 6 to 10 s pauses the session with `pausedAt` set to when the person stopped and `autoPaused: true`; moving above a higher speed for 2 to 3 s resumes it with a new `seg`, the pause counted up to when movement began. The location task drops the distance filter (`distanceInterval: 0`) only while auto-pause is on, because iOS delivers no fixes to a person standing still when one is set and the detector, which only advances on arriving fixes, would never reach its stop time; with auto-pause off it keeps the 2 m filter. A fix with no Doppler speed (iOS reports -1, common while standing still) falls back to speed from position, measured from the oldest fix in a 5 to 15 s trail and less the larger accuracy radius, so a phone drifting a few metres while its owner stands still reads as stopped; at Finish the app log gets one line counting the fixes auto-pause saw with a speed, without one and too inaccurate. Fixes with accuracy worse than 30 m are ignored, a manual pause is never auto-resumed (only `autoPaused` sessions are watched), and the toggle is `session.autoPause` set from the Record screen's switch (preference in AsyncStorage, on by default). While a recording runs, the screen shows compact Auto-pause and Voice cues switches for that session only (the preferences stay as they are): `setRecordingAutoPause(enabled, notification)` resets the detector, starts the running location task again with the matching distance filter (0 on, 2 m off; a failure is logged and the setting kept) and, when turned off while it holds the session auto-paused, resumes it (an indoor session stays off), and `setRecordingAudioCues(unit | undefined)` counts the splits already completed as said, so none is announced late. Start countdown: `RecordActivityScreen` holds `countdownLeft` (preference `countdownSeconds` from `COUNTDOWN_CHOICES`, off by default, stored beside the other recording preferences); Start with a countdown arms it, each second fires a selection haptic and (voice cues on) speaks the number, and at zero it calls `startRecording`. Nothing is recorded or requested until then, so the first-run location prompt follows the countdown, and Cancel leaves no session.
