Laps: the Lap button calls `markLap()` (recording only; presses within 3 s of the last are ignored) which stores epoch-ms marks in `session.laps`; `computeLaps(points, marks)` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts` turns them into splits (interpolating distance and active time at each mark, a mark inside a pause taking the fix before it, the stretch after the last mark `partial`), the finished view lists them above the splits, and `saveRecordedActivity` uploads them as the entry's lap windows instead of the automatic splits when any exist. `splitBars` draws the per-split pace bars and picks the fastest (never a partial). Intervals: `utils/intervals.ts` is the pure side (`IntervalPlan` = a style, `runWalk` or `fastEasy`, and timed `warmup`/`work`/`recovery`/`cooldown` steps; `buildIntervalPlan` clamps and builds one from options, `INTERVAL_PRESETS` are the built-in plans, `intervalPosition(plan, activeSeconds)` says where a plan stands). `startRecording({ intervals })` stores it as `session.intervals = { plan, cued }` (`cued` is the last step started, so a restored session never repeats one); `advanceIntervals` runs after each batch of fixes in `ingestFixes` and from `tickIntervals()` (the screen calls it every second while open), against `elapsedSeconds`, so paused time never counts. A new step buzzes (`fireImpactHaptic`), is spoken when voice cues are on (`intervalCue`; the first step replaces the "Recording started" cue) and is marked as a lap at its exact start (`session.laps`, deduped against the 3 s gap), so the saved activity is cut into the work and recovery stretches by the existing lap path; steps passed in one go are all marked but only the latest is spoken. UI: `components/recording/IntervalSetup.tsx` (picker plus custom steppers; the screen owns the choice) and `IntervalCard.tsx` (live step). On a phone recording the watch is told the step (`intervalIndex/Label/Remaining/Next` in `WatchRecordingStatePayload`; `useWatchRecordingBridge` re-sends durably when the step changes, `RecordingStore` buzzes via `Haptics.step()`); watch-only recordings follow no plan. Programs: `utils/runPrograms.ts` is a list of workouts, each an `IntervalPlan` (`RUN_PROGRAMS`: `walkFit` (8 weeks of brisk walking, `activity: 'walk'` so Record starts a walk and "Count it" matches a walk; `programActivity(id)` gives it), `walk10k` (10 weeks of brisk walking to a 10K walk, also `activity: 'walk'`), `returnToRunning` (4), `gentle5k` (12), `beginner5k` (9 weeks of run/walk), `fiveToTenK` (8), `zone2Base` (10 weeks of easy runs held to the person's Zone 2 heart rate: a `runWalk` plan of `zone2`-effort steps, a long run up to 70 minutes, a lighter fourth and eighth week, four strides after the first run in weeks 6, 7 and 9, and a zone check, the middle run of weeks 4, 8 and 10, flagged by `isZoneCheckWorkout` and noted by `ZoneCheckNote` on the program card and the Diary card), `faster5k` (6, speed work in `fastEasy` style), `beginner10k` (12), `faster10k` (8, speed work), `tenKToHalf` (10), `halfMarathon` (12), `fasterHalf` (10, speed work), `beginnerMarathon` (18), `marathon` (16) and `advancedMarathon` (16, five runs a week, speed work). `zone2Base`, `tenKToHalf`, `halfMarathon`, `fasterHalf` and `beginnerMarathon` also have a four-runs-a-week twin (`<id>4`, `variantOf` set); `PICKER_PROGRAMS` is the list the picker shows, and `RunProgramsScreen` puts a `SegmentedControl` on those cards (`programVersions(id)`) to choose 3 or 4 runs, starting the chosen id. A variant needs its own name, description and structure strings; the faster plans use `stridesRun`, `hills`, `tempo` and `marathonPaceLong` in `runPrograms.ts` for strides, hill repeats, tempo runs and marathon-pace blocks, built from run/walk pieces between a warm-up and cool-down; the longer ones are continuous runs timed in minutes, so a plan step may be up to 4 hours (`MAX_PROGRAM_STEP_SECONDS`); `RunProgramCard` shows a picker, and switching a running program to another one asks first and restarts at week 1), with `programStatus`/`advanceProgress` over a `ProgramProgress` (`next` = index of the workout due). `services/runProgramService.ts` is the server-backed side (`services/api/runProgramApi.ts` over `/api/v2/run-program`, a `run_programs` row per user holding the person's own copy of the workouts, the place `next_index`, the `enabled` flag and a change log; the React Query copy under `runProgramQueryKey` is set from each response, and `useRunProgram` re-reads on every mount because the AI assistant may have changed it): `setProgramEnabled`, `restartProgram`, `skipProgramWorkout`, `setProgramPosition`, `completeProgramWorkout`. The status is built with shared `storedProgramStatus`, so adjusted workouts reach the card, the picker and the recording. `RunProgramsScreen` wraps each change in `change`, which toasts a failure (offline); a failed tick-off after a save is logged, not shown, since the activity itself is saved. Programs are opt-in: `ProgramProgress.enabled` is the Record card's switch (absent in older data means on), the card always shows the stored place, but `getProgramStatus()` and the screen's `programStatus` are null while it is off, so nothing is preselected, nothing is ticked off and the Diary shows nothing; turning it off keeps the place. Setup and management live on `RunProgramsScreen` (root-stack `RunPrograms`, from a Library row; with no program it lists the sixteen `PICKER_PROGRAMS` with Start program (which asks Start today / Pick a date, then calls `startProgram(id, startDay)`; null = today), with one it shows `RunProgramCard`: switch, today's workout, Skip, Start over, workout picker, run days and start day); a collapsible `RunProgramGuide` ("How the plans work": what a workout is made of, effort by the talk test and a 1-10 scale rather than pace, and a brisk walking pace of about 5 to 6 km/h; `programStructure` describes each program's weeks and also shows on each program card; `programRest` adds a per-program "Rest days" section, e.g. no back-to-back workouts for the beginner plans) sits on that screen in both states; the Record screen only has the slim `RunProgramSelect` (today's workout, with a Manage link), and the Diary shows `RunProgramBanner` through `ExerciseSummary`'s `programBanner` prop, styled like a scheduled workout plan: `programRunForDate` (shared `utils/runProgramSchedule.ts`) decides what a Diary day shows: with run days chosen it follows the calendar `projectRuns` lays out from the next workout due (one workout per chosen weekday starting today, the first day or tomorrow once today's is done, so a missed run moves everything after it along and nothing is ever skipped), so today shows the workout due ("Scheduled Today") and later days show what is coming ("Scheduled", no Start button); with none chosen it offers the next workout today only ("Up next"); nothing before the first day, after the goal day, once a workout was done today (`doneDay`), or when the program is off or finished. Saving a recording replaces `RecordActivity` with `RunSummary` (`RunSummaryScreen`): `handleSave` builds `RunSummaryParams` with `buildRunSummary` (`utils/runSummary.ts`: figures, a route thinned to 250 points, per-unit splits, the person's laps, average heart rate from `getHeartRateSamples`; plain data, since the recording is cleared right after) and, for a program workout, `nextProgramLine` (`utils/runProgramSummary.ts`: the next workout and the day `projectRuns` puts it on, read from the program the server now holds) with the place in the program. The screen shows the stat cards, the route (`RouteMap`), the program progress with a "How hard was that?" row (1 to 10, shown when `programSummary` returned `rate`, i.e. the workout was ticked off: `rateProgramWorkout` saves it to the server's `effort_log` through `POST /api/v2/run-program/effort`; 8 or more offers "Repeat this workout" = `setProgramPosition(index)`, 3 or less offers "Skip the next workout" = `index + 2` unless that is the last, and shared `suggestFromEffort` adds a note after two hard or two easy in a row; nothing changes unless a button is tapped) and a View program link, and the splits or laps through the shared `SplitRow`/`Stat` (`components/recording/SplitRow.tsx`, also used by the Record screen); Done goes back. A wrist-only run has no summary screen. `RunProgramBanner` also offers "Count it" when `findLoggedRun` (`utils/loggedRun.ts`: a non-preset entry named like a run, at least half the planned length or 10 minutes) is among today's entries, completing the due workout on the person's say-so (a run recorded some other way, such as the watch or an import). Every tick-off passes the diary entry it was done as (`completeProgramWorkout`'s `entryId`: the saved recording, or the counted run), which the server keeps in `completion_log`; `ProgramWorkoutBadge` (`useProgramWorkoutForEntry`, shared lookup `programWorkoutForEntry` in `utils/runProgramSummary.ts`) then shows "Part of your training program" with the workout label on `CardioSessionScreen` and `ActivityDetailScreen`. `RunGoalsCard` tops that screen with the running goals (`useRunGoals`, `services/api/runGoalsApi.ts` over `/api/v2/run-goals`, query key `runGoalsQueryKey`, re-read on focus because a run saved elsewhere changes the week): a weekly distance target with this week's progress and a race with a day countdown, both optional and independent of the program; the card edits in place and converts between the display unit and the km the server stores, and Remove race clears the name, distance and day together (the server refuses a name or distance without a day). `RunProgramCalendar` on the screen lists the dates, the finish day (`projectedFinishDay`) and whether it fits before the goal day. The Record screen never chooses the workout for the person: it is offered on `RunProgramSelect` and in the `IntervalSetup` picker (`programLabel`), and chosen as the interval choice `'program'` only when the screen is opened with the `programWorkout` route param (the Diary card's Start and the program screen's Start today's workout), so a plain walk or run starts with no plan. For a walk or ride, `IntervalSetup` (given `activity`) leaves out the run/walk presets and style, the Record screen drops a run/walk preset when the activity changes, and `intervalPlan` turns a `runWalk` plan into `fastEasy`, so the card, spoken cues and watch steps say Fast and Easy rather than Run. While a recording exists the screen's header names it (`recordingTitle`: "Outdoor run", "Indoor walk", "Outdoor ride"), and outdoors `GpsSignalBadge` shows bars from the newest fix's accuracy (`useLatestGpsFix`, its own listener set in `gpsRecordingService` so the bars do not re-render the screen; `gpsSignalLevel` in `utils/gpsRecording.ts`, the watch's thresholds). The watch-run live screen is titled "Run on your watch" or "Walk on your watch". `startRecording({ program })` stores `session.program = { id, index }`, and `handleSave` calls `completeProgramWorkout` only when `planCompleted(session)`, so a run cut short is offered again. `setProgramPosition(index)` lets the person jump to any workout (the card's "Choose a different workout" picker lists the weeks, finished runs ticked), clamped to the program. Schedule: the weekdays the workout shows in the Diary (`run_days`, 0 = Sunday, empty = every day), the first day it shows (`start_date`, null = from today), the goal day (`end_date`, null = none; nothing is scheduled after it and the screen says whether the program fits before it) and the last local day a workout was completed (`last_done_date`, sent as `day` by `completeProgramWorkout`) are columns on the same `run_programs` row, like a workout plan's schedule, so they follow the person between devices; `useRunProgram` returns them as `schedule` and `doneDay`, `setProgramSchedule` writes them, and `RunProgramCard` edits them (day chips; a start date picked from a calendar, today unless one is set). Switching programs keeps the schedule. There are no run notifications; `cancelLegacyRunReminders` (`services/legacyRunReminders.ts`, run at startup) cancels weekly reminders an earlier build scheduled and deletes their stored key. An indoor session has no location task, so the plan moves on while the record screen is open and catches up when it is opened again. Voice cues (`session.audioCues` = `'km'`/`'miles'`, off by default, set from the Record screen's switch via `getRecordingPreferences`/`setRecordingPreference`): `ingestFixes` calls `cueSplit` after new points, which runs `computeSplits` and speaks only the latest newly completed full split (`session.cuedSplits` is persisted so a restore never repeats one); start, manual pause/resume, auto-pause/resume and finish speak through `services/recordingCues.ts` (`Speech.speak` with `useApplicationAudioSession`, each cue replacing the one before; it reuses `beginCueDucking`/`endCueDucking`, so ducking follows `duckMusicDuringCues`). The voice is `resolveSpeechVoice` (`services/speechVoice.ts`), shared with guided workouts: the installed voices are read ahead (`loadSpeechVoices`, called by `beginRecordingCues` and `speakGuided`), then it returns the person's pick (`guidedVoiceId`, one device-local setting for both) while still installed, otherwise the best Premium/Enhanced voice for the app language (`bestVoiceId` in `utils/speechVoices.ts`), otherwise undefined (system default, and also until the voices have loaded, so a stale id never reaches `expo-speech`, which throws on an unknown iOS identifier). `expo-speech` reports an iOS Premium voice as "Default" (it only maps `.enhanced`), so `voiceTier` also reads `.premium.` / `.enhanced.` from Apple's identifier and drops `com.apple.speech.synthesis.voice.*` novelty voices; Siri voices are not exposed to apps. The picker is `useSpeechVoiceChoices`, rendered by `RecordingVoiceCard` (Record screen, while voice cues are on) and `GuidedWorkoutSettingsSection`. The words come from `utils/recordingCues.ts` with an injected `t` (durations are spelled out, since a speech engine reads "5:24" as a time of day). The audio session is `startRecordingCueSession()` in `sounds.ts` (silent-switch override plus iOS `shouldPlayInBackground`): the location task already keeps the app running on iOS, so no keep-alive track is used. Each resume starts a new `seg`, so the jump across a pause is never distance or time. The first fix of a stretch (the start, or after a resume) must be within `STRETCH_START_MAX_ACCURACY_M` (25 m), tighter than the 50 m cutoff for the rest, so a cold-start fix does not put a spike at the head of the route. Coaching audio (#250): on top of splits and plan steps, `advanceCoaching` (next to `advanceIntervals`, from each fix batch and `tickIntervals`) speaks the cues `nextCoachingCues` (`utils/coachingCues.ts`, pure) says are due, only with voice cues on and at most one a tick (the rest are marked said): "three, two, one" in the last three seconds of such a step (one utterance, `stepCountdownCue`; it makes the ten-second warning of that step stale, so both are marked said together) after a ten-second warning before a step of 30 s or more that names the next step, a halfway call and a last-minute call for plan workouts of 5 and 3 minutes or more, a milestone word when the recorded distance passes 5K, 10K, a half marathon or a marathon (`RACE_MILESTONES_METERS`, outdoors only, whatever the unit; the furthest one after a gap), and a spoken check-in (time, distance and average pace; time only indoors) every `session.checkInMinutes` (the `CHECK_IN_CHOICES` 0, 5, 10 or 15, set in `CheckInCard` before Start, changed mid-run with `setRecordingCheckIn`, remembered as a preference). `session.coaching` holds what was said so a restore never repeats it (a state saved before the countdown and milestones existed is read with their defaults). A split announced at the same fix is spoken first, in the same breath as the coaching cue (`advanceCoaching(now, splitText)`), because a second utterance replaces the first; the countdown alone skips that, as it is for this second only. A program workout adds two sentences (`programSpokenCues` in `utils/programCues.ts`, already in the app language): `start` ("Week 2, run 3. 25 minutes.", plus "Eased for today." when its running time is 10% or more under the original) spoken in the same breath as the first step, and `done` ("Workout 4 of 27 done. Next up, Week 2, run 4.", or "Program complete. Well done.") added to the finish cue once every step of the plan has run for its full unpaused duration (a run stopped early gets none). The phone service reads them from `session.program`/`session.intervals`; the watch gets them as `startText`/`doneText` on the plan (`buildWatchRunPlans`, `buildWatchStartPlan`) and speaks them as they are. Splits also say how much faster or slower they were than the split before when it differs by 5 s or more (`splitTrendNote`). A watch run speaks the same cues: `Domain/WatchCoaching.swift` is the Swift port of `nextCoachingCues` (constants and cue order must stay in step), `WatchRunStore.cueCoachingIfDue` runs on its 1 s timer (which now also runs for a run with a check-in interval but no plan, and for any outdoor run, for the milestones) and speaks through `WatchRunCues` (`stepCountdownText` (spoken with `interrupt`), `milestoneText`, `stepWarningText`, `halfwayText`, `lastMinuteText`, `checkInText`, localized like the other watch cues; check-ins give only the time indoors). The interval comes from `runStart` (`checkInMinutes`, sent with voice cues on) or, for a run begun on the wrist, the context's `runCheckInMinutes`; it lives in `WatchRunSettings.checkInMinutes` (optional, so older snapshots decode), and after a relaunch `WatchCoachingState.resumed` counts what has passed as said. Only while-using permission is requested: iOS keeps recording through `UIBackgroundModes: location` set in `app.config.ts`, Android through the plugin's foreground service, so `ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` stays off (Google Play reviews it closely). `utils/gpsRecording.ts` is the pure maths (distance, pace, smoothed elevation gain/loss, per-km/mile splits); `services/gpsRecordingSave.ts` finds or creates the Walking/Running/Cycling cardio exercise, creates a normal entry with `createExerciseEntry` (no `calories_burned`, so the server estimates it), then posts the route and split windows to `POST /api/exercise-entries/:id/gps-track` (`attachExerciseEntryGpsTrack`). It does NOT go through `/api/health-data`, whose per-source delete-then-insert would wipe other recorded activities on the same day. The entry id is kept on the session, so a failed track upload retries without logging the activity twice, and the server replaces an existing track on re-post. The live map reuses `RouteMap`, so Android shows a plain line without a Google Maps key. Changing the recorder's permissions, background modes or foreground-service options needs `npx expo prebuild --clean` and an outdoor check on a real iPhone and Android phone. Apple Watch tie-in (phone GPS + watch): the phone still records; `hooks/useWatchRecordingBridge.ts` (mounted in `WatchWorkoutGate`, gated on `WatchConnectivity.isSupported()`) sends the session to the watch as `recordingState` through `updateRecordingState` (`sendMessage` when reachable, plus `transferUserInfo` for status changes only, never application context, which belongs to the check-in data; `sentAt` lets the watch drop an out-of-order queued copy; a `status: 'ended'` message tells the watch the recording was saved or discarded, with `discarded` true when the session left the store without a `savedEntryId`), refreshes distance and pace every 3s while recording, and applies the watch's `recordingControl` (`pause`/`resume`/`finish`/`lap`) to `gpsRecordingService` for the live session id only. A `lap` carries `at`, when the wearer pressed it (controls ride the queued transport and can arrive late); `markLap(at)` stores that time (a future or pre-recording time counts as now, a press within 3 s of an existing mark is dropped as a re-delivery, marks are kept sorted), and the state sent to the watch carries `lapCount` for the page's "Lap N" line. Heart-rate batches arrive as `recordingHeartRate` (`onRecordingHeartRate`, deduped by `clientId`), go to `addHeartRateSamples` (persisted beside the session, still accepted after Finish because the last batch is queued and lands late, cleared by `discardRecording`), and `gpsRecordingSave` posts them to the existing `watch-telemetry` route after the route upload (best effort: a failure is logged and never blocks the save; fewer than two readings are skipped). The current reading is also shown live: the watch puts the newest one beside the status on `RecordingView` (`RecordingStore.liveHeartRate`, cleared after 15 s without a new reading or when the recording stops being live) and sends it to the phone as the same live-only `liveHeartRate` message the strength workout uses, with the recording's id as `sessionId` and an empty `exerciseEntryId` (`WatchSessionManager.sendRecordingLiveHeartRate`, at most every 3 s and only while the phone is reachable; never queued). `useWatchRecordingBridge` takes it for the live session into `liveHeartRateStore`, and `RecordActivityScreen` shows a Heart rate tile through `useRecordingHeartRate` (hidden once the reading is 15 s old, so a watch that goes quiet does not leave a stale number). No new message type, so nothing to rename in four places. On the watch, `RecordingStore` mirrors the state and `RecordingView` shows it on the Workout page (`WorkoutView`, after a strength workout and before the idle picker; `ContentView` keeps the Workout page showing and lands on it while `RecordingStore.isActive`, as for a strength workout; the old `WatchPage.recording` page is no longer put in the list); `RecordingHealthKitController` runs an `HKWorkoutSession` for heart-rate sampling only (skipped while a strength workout owns the session) and stamps `SparkyFitnessSessionId` so the saved HKWorkout is not imported back. It writes nothing to Apple Health until the phone decides: on Finish the collection stops but the workout is held (`Outcome.hold`, `heldFor`), and the phone's `ended` message then finishes it (`resolveHeld(save: true)`) or discards it, so a discarded recording never reaches Health (a workout already written cannot be reliably removed: deleting the `HKWorkout` leaves attached samples). An older phone build that sends no `discarded` is treated as saved, and a watch app that is relaunched before the phone decides loses the held workout (the diary entry is unaffected). Message type and key strings (`recordingState`, `recordingControl`, `recordingHeartRate`) are duplicated in `index.ts`, `WatchConnectivityModule.swift`, `ContextPayloadMapper`/`OutboundPayloads` and `WatchSessionManager.route`; rename all or none. The watch Swift has not been compiled outside Xcode; verify on a real watch after `npx expo prebuild --clean`.
- Catch-up: `RunProgramCalendar` calls `catchUpFor` (`shared/src/utils/runProgramCatchUp.ts`, pure) with the program, run days and goal day. When the last workout would land after the goal day it offers to skip the fewest of the next workouts (at most `MAX_CATCH_UP_SKIP`, 3; the last workout is never skipped) through `setProgramPosition(next)`; when more than that is needed it only says how many workouts will not fit. Nothing changes unless the button is tapped.
- Realign: `RunProgramRealignCard` (on `RunProgramsScreen` under the program card, only while the program is on) runs shared `realignProgram` on the cached program (`realignInputFromProgram`, today and the last plan change as local days) and shows, when the plan no longer fits the goal day, after a gap, or when a week or more of room is spare, a before/after preview (workouts left and finish day, each dropped, folded or repeated workout, the ease after a break, the kept taper) with **Realign plan** and **Not now**. Accept calls `applyProgramRealign` (`POST /api/v2/run-program/realign` with `confirmed`, the preview `token`, `force` for continue anyway; a refusal re-reads the program) and `RunProgramsScreen` toasts the outcome. An unrealistic goal shows the reasons and the options: move the goal to the day the whole plan would end (`setProgramSchedule({ endDay })`), plan a shorter race (`RunProgramRace` takes optional `distance` and `raceDay` params) or continue anyway (a second preview from `continuePlan`, accepted with `force`; absent when even the taper does not fit). The card text is `runPrograms.realign.*`. Nothing changes until Accept. The web training programs (a separate PR) must use the same endpoint and shared result.
- Training programs screen layout: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#training-programs-screen-layout).
- Race plan builder: `RunProgramRaceScreen` (root-stack `RunProgramRace`, "Plan a race" button on `RunProgramsScreen`) takes distance, level, race date, run days and optional goal time and calls `buildRacePlan` (`shared/src/utils/racePlan.ts`, pure, timed steps only, race step chunked at 180 min, taper before the race, `MAX_RACE_PLAN_WEEKS`). Create flow: `saveCustomProgram` -> `startProgram(custom:<id>, startDay or null)` -> `setProgramSchedule({days, startDay, endDay})`; asks to confirm if a program is already active.
- Fitness tests: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#fitness-tests).
- Reports hub: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#reports-hub).
- Fitness tests: `FitnessTestScreen` (root-stack `FitnessTest`, from the training programs screen's `FitnessTestPanel`) picks a 5K time trial, a 30-minute all-out run or a 12-minute Cooper test and lists the history (latest ten, with the result and the threshold and easy paces each gave); it opens `RecordActivity` with the `fitnessTest` route param. With that param the Record screen shows `FitnessTestSetup` instead of the ordinary form (outdoor GPS or treadmill chosen with the Outdoor (GPS) / Treadmill control, which opens on the last test's surface via the `indoor` route param that `FitnessTestScreen` passes; no plan, pace alert or auto-pause. With a paired watch an outdoor test also offers Phone GPS / Watch GPS: the watch path sends `startRunOnWatch` a one-step plan from `buildWatchFitnessTestPlan` (`utils/watchRunPlans.ts`; id `fitness-test:<type>`, a 5000 m distance step for the 5K, timed for the others) and opens `WatchRunLive`; treadmill tests stay on the phone), `startRecording({ fitnessTest, indoor })` stores `session.fitnessTest`, and while recording `FitnessTestCard` guides from `fitnessTestProgress` (`utils/fitnessTest.ts`) and a success haptic (plus a spoken cue with voice cues on) marks the test's mark. A watch test is scored when the run arrives: `watchRunQueue.fileFitnessTest` reads the test from `fitnessTestFromPlanId(payload.planId)`, runs `measureFitnessTest` on `watchRunPoints`, and posts `saveFitnessTest` against the entry id (outdoor only, retried with the queue; the server replaces the test of an entry). On the watch `WatchRunStore.cueStepIfDue` buzzes when the plan finishes and, with voice cues on, says `watch.run.cue.fitnessTestComplete` instead of "Plan complete." for a plan whose id starts with `fitness-test:`; the 30-minute run's 10-minute point is not marked on the watch. After Finish `FitnessTestFinishedCard` previews the outcome; `handleSave` calls `measureFitnessTest(type, points)` (time to 5 km, distance at 720 s, or distance at 600 s and 1800 s, read from the recording's active time with `activeSecondsAtDistance` / `distanceAtActiveSeconds`, so jogging on changes nothing) and, once `saveRecordedActivity` has returned the entry id, `saveFitnessTest` posts the measurements to `/api/v2/fitness-tests` (the server works out the result and paces; a retry reuses the saved activity and the server replaces the test of that entry) and replaces the screen with `FitnessTestResult` instead of `RunSummary`. A test stopped before its mark is saved as an ordinary run. Treadmill tests (`session.indoor`) reuse the indoor run path instead of a parallel one: the existing indoor Distance box (step estimate or typed) is the test's distance and the activity is logged by `saveRecordedActivity` as an indoor run (a 5K with no distance is logged as 5 km), and `IndoorTestFields` adds the 5K time (clock by default), the 30-minute run's distance at 10:00 (empty means a steady pace) and the incline (default 1%) with an "Adjust for incline" switch; `measureIndoorTest` (`utils/fitnessTest.ts`) builds the measurement (Cooper and 30-minute tests need the clock to have run out and a distance, else the run is saved ordinarily) and the finished card shows the raw and adjusted numbers. `saveFitnessTest` sends `surface`, `incline_percent` and `incline_adjusted`; the server applies shared `applyIncline`. `fitnessTestSurfaceText` / `inclineAdjustmentText` word the surface on the history, result screen and badge, and `FitnessRetestPrompt` says the retest starts on the same surface. `services/fitnessTestService.ts` (`useFitnessTests`, query key `fitnessTestsQueryKey`) is the server-backed copy. The latest test sets the person's paces: `usePaceZones` (`paceZonesFromTest`, source `test`) and `useSuggestedRanges` (`suggestedPaceRangesFromTest`, ahead of a typed race and of session history; `PaceReferenceCard` says so via `overriddenByTest`). `FitnessTestResultScreen` shows the result, the zones (`FitnessTestPaceList`) and, against the test before it, how they moved (`comparePaces`); `FitnessTestBadge` marks the activity on `CardioSessionScreen` and `ActivityDetailScreen`. From the latest test: `FitnessTestPanel` starts the program `programIdForFiveK` points to (through the screen's start flow) and opens the race planner, where `RunProgramRaceScreen` has a "Plan from my latest test" switch (level from `levelFromFiveK`, race day at `racePaceFromFiveK` via `buildRacePlan`'s `racePaceSecondsPerKm`). `FitnessRetestPrompt` offers a retest from six weeks (`retestStatus`: due to eight weeks, then overdue) on the programs screen's panel and, as `ProgramRetestPrompt`, on today's Diary program card while a program is followed (Later hides it for the app session).
- Fitness tests: `FitnessTestScreen` (root-stack `FitnessTest`, from the training programs screen's `FitnessTestPanel`) picks a 5K time trial, a 30-minute all-out run or a 12-minute Cooper test and lists the history (latest ten, with the result and the threshold and easy paces each gave); it opens `RecordActivity` with the `fitnessTest` route param. With that param the Record screen shows `FitnessTestSetup` instead of the ordinary form (outdoor GPS or treadmill chosen with the Outdoor (GPS) / Treadmill control, which opens on the last test's surface via the `indoor` route param that `FitnessTestScreen` passes; no plan, pace alert or auto-pause. With a paired watch either surface also offers Phone / Watch: the watch path sends `startRunOnWatch` a one-step plan from `buildWatchFitnessTestPlan` (`utils/watchRunPlans.ts`; id `fitness-test:<type>` or `fitness-test:<type>:<incline>` for a treadmill test, which has no step afterwards to ask in, so `FitnessTestSetup` asks for the incline first; a 5000 m distance step for the 5K, timed for the others) and opens `WatchRunLive`), `startRecording({ fitnessTest, indoor })` stores `session.fitnessTest`, and while recording `FitnessTestCard` guides from `fitnessTestProgress` (`utils/fitnessTest.ts`) and a success haptic (plus a spoken cue with voice cues on) marks the test's mark. A watch test is scored when the run arrives: `watchRunQueue.fileFitnessTest` reads the test and incline from `fitnessTestFromPlanId(payload.planId)`, measures it (outdoors `measureFitnessTest` on `watchRunPoints`; on a treadmill `measureWatchIndoorTest`, which reads the 5K time, the 12-minute distance or the 10- and 30-minute distances off the run's `distanceSeries` curve, with the total distance being what the wearer confirmed on the wrist) and posts `saveFitnessTest` against the entry id (retried with the queue; the server replaces the test of an entry). A treadmill 5K counts from 4.5 km, since the wearer ends it when the machine shows 5 km. On the watch `WatchRunStore.cueStepIfDue` buzzes when the plan finishes and, with voice cues on, says `watch.run.cue.fitnessTestComplete` instead of "Plan complete." for a plan whose id starts with `fitness-test:`; the 30-minute run's 10-minute point is not marked on the watch. After Finish `FitnessTestFinishedCard` previews the outcome; `handleSave` calls `measureFitnessTest(type, points)` (time to 5 km, distance at 720 s, or distance at 600 s and 1800 s, read from the recording's active time with `activeSecondsAtDistance` / `distanceAtActiveSeconds`, so jogging on changes nothing) and, once `saveRecordedActivity` has returned the entry id, `saveFitnessTest` posts the measurements to `/api/v2/fitness-tests` (the server works out the result and paces; a retry reuses the saved activity and the server replaces the test of that entry) and replaces the screen with `FitnessTestResult` instead of `RunSummary`. A test stopped before its mark is saved as an ordinary run. Treadmill tests (`session.indoor`) reuse the indoor run path instead of a parallel one: the existing indoor Distance box (step estimate or typed) is the test's distance and the activity is logged by `saveRecordedActivity` as an indoor run (a 5K with no distance is logged as 5 km), and `IndoorTestFields` adds the 5K time (clock by default), the 30-minute run's distance at 10:00 (empty means a steady pace) and the incline (default 1%) with an "Adjust for incline" switch; `measureIndoorTest` (`utils/fitnessTest.ts`) builds the measurement (Cooper and 30-minute tests need the clock to have run out and a distance, else the run is saved ordinarily) and the finished card shows the raw and adjusted numbers. `saveFitnessTest` sends `surface`, `incline_percent` and `incline_adjusted`; the server applies shared `applyIncline`. `fitnessTestSurfaceText` / `inclineAdjustmentText` word the surface on the history, result screen and badge, and `FitnessRetestPrompt` says the retest starts on the same surface. `services/fitnessTestService.ts` (`useFitnessTests`, query key `fitnessTestsQueryKey`) is the server-backed copy. The latest test sets the person's paces: `usePaceZones` (`paceZonesFromTest`, source `test`) and `useSuggestedRanges` (`suggestedPaceRangesFromTest`, ahead of a typed race and of session history; `PaceReferenceCard` says so via `overriddenByTest`). `FitnessTestResultScreen` shows the result, the zones (`FitnessTestPaceList`) and, against the test before it, how they moved (`comparePaces`); `FitnessTestBadge` marks the activity on `CardioSessionScreen` and `ActivityDetailScreen`. From the latest test: `FitnessTestPanel` starts the program `programIdForFiveK` points to (through the screen's start flow) and opens the race planner, where `RunProgramRaceScreen` has a "Plan from my latest test" switch (level from `levelFromFiveK`, race day at `racePaceFromFiveK` via `buildRacePlan`'s `racePaceSecondsPerKm`). `FitnessRetestPrompt` offers a retest from six weeks (`retestStatus`: due to eight weeks, then overdue) on the programs screen's panel and, as `ProgramRetestPrompt`, on today's Diary program card while a program is followed (Later hides it for the app session).
- Reports hub (`ReportsScreen`, reached from a Library row, Settings → Reports and a Reports link beside the Dashboard's Health Trends heading) links `NutritionReportScreen`, `HydrationReportScreen`, `SubstancesReportScreen` (caffeine and alcohol), `SleepAnalyticsScreen`, `MeasurementsReportScreen`, `MoodReportScreen`, `ExerciseStatisticsScreen` and `CompareReportScreen` ("Does A go with B?": a sentence of pickers, its own per-mode span control instead of the 7/30/90d one, and an answer, chart and checks card from `useCompare` over `GET /api/v2/reports/compare`; the phone only formats `response.result`, and a 404 means the server is too old; `'compare'` has no report sections, so the customize screen only reorders/hides it. Its "Since a habit started" choice reads "Since I started [X] regularly, has [Y] changed?" and does not offer weight trend (it drifts too slowly to tell a change from its own wandering; the pickers and `isAskable` share `offeredInMode`) and swaps the span control for `CompareSinceRows` ("Since [date]" through `CalendarSheet`, held to `sinceStartBounds`: 730 days back to yesterday, showing the server's `suggestedStart` marked "(suggested)" until a day is picked, or "Pick a date" when there is none; "Line: [Y] ≥ [value]" through `BottomSheetPicker`, steps on the display scale from `sinceLineOptions`, sent in the stored scale, hidden for a yes/no outcome and for a start-less answer) and the answer, chart and checks for `CompareSinceCard` (verdict, typical range, share at or above the line, regularity, already-changing warning, stats line, and always the not-proof-of-cause caveat) and `CompareSinceChart` (the outcome by day, before and since stretches shaded, a marker at the start, the line dashed across; no dot view) from `useCompareSince`. A picked start is kept per habit and a picked line per outcome, and the rows keep the last answer for the pair while a new one loads). Every new screen shares `components/reports/ReportScreenLayout` (header, 7/30/90d control, scroll body) and the 7/30/90d `utils/trendRange.ts` range. Sleep analytics reads `GET /api/sleep` through `useSleepAnalytics` on the same `sleepRangeQueryKey` as the Health Trends sleep chart, and `utils/sleepAnalytics.ts` takes each day's main sleep (`selectMainSleep`) so a nap never replaces the night. Hydration (`useHydrationReport`, `utils/hydrationReport.ts`) reads `GET /api/measurements/water-intake-range` and the water goal on the keys the Health Trends hydration chart uses; days with no water are left out of every average, like Nutrition. Measurements (`useMeasurementsReport`, `utils/measurementsReport.ts`) reads the check-in range endpoint on `measurementsRangeQueryKey`, keeps the newest edit per day and field in stored kg/cm and converts at the screen edge; weight change is latest minus first in the window and stays a neutral color because whether a loss is good depends on the goal. Nutrition adds macro goal progress, calories by weekday and logging streaks, a fat breakdown card, a vitamins-and-minerals card and a "Your nutrients" card whose rows are picked and ordered in Customize Reports (`shownReportNutrients` + `reportNutrientOrder`; standard nutrients from `REPORT_NUTRIENT_KEYS` plus the account's custom ones, which `GET /api/reports/mini-nutrition-trends` already returns by name). `buildNutrientAverages` averages any key over logged days, drops ones never eaten, and compares with the day's goal (`water_goal_ml` for water, `custom_nutrients[name]` for custom); each row opens `NutrientTrends`, which ends in a Top Sources card (`NutrientTopSourcesCard`): the foods contributing most to that nutrient, from `GET /api/food-entries/range` (`useFoodEntriesRange`, `foodEntriesRangeQueryKey`) ranked by `buildTopSources` in `utils/nutrientSources.ts`, scaled per entry by `quantity / serving_size` like the daily totals. Touching a chart bar (`NutrientBarChart`'s `onSelectDay`) narrows it to that day (that bar stays highlighted, the others dimmed) until cleared, chips narrow it to one meal type, and tapping a source expands the entries behind it, each opening `FoodEntryView`. sleep adds stage shares, weekday-vs-weekend nights, nights of 7h+ and longest/shortest night (7h is `FULL_NIGHT_HOURS`, not a stored goal). Sleep also shows the web's sleep science figures, each a section card that renders nothing until its figure loads: tonight's sleep need (`useSleepNeed`, `sleep.need`), sleep debt or surplus (`sleep.debt`), energy today (`sleep.energy`), chronotype (`sleep.chronotype`) and the baseline sleep need with social jetlag, a data-sufficiency note and a Recalculate button (`sleep.baseline`); the last four are in `components/sleepScience/SleepScienceCards.tsx` over `services/api/sleepScienceApi.ts` and `hooks/useSleepScience.ts`. Their query keys share `sleepScienceQueryKeyRoot`, which a health sync and a recalculation invalidate. A negative debt from the server is a surplus; `utils/sleepReportFormat.ts` formats signed durations once (never "-1h -7m"). Mood reads `GET /api/mood` (`moodApi.ts`, `useMoodReport`, `utils/moodReport.ts`); bands and labels come from the shared mood model. The hub is customizable from its header (`ReportsSettingsScreen`, Customize Reports): the report order and which are hidden (`reportOrder` + `hiddenReports`, reconciled against `constants/reports.ts` with `resolveKeyOrder`), the window a report opens on (`reportDefaultRange`, still switchable on the report without changing the saved default) and, per report, which sections show (`hiddenReportSections`, keys namespaced by report such as `sleep.stages` or `sleep.metric.hrv`; each report screen reads them through `useReportCustomization().isSectionShown`). The window is a `ReportWindow` (`utils/trendRange.ts`): a 7/30/90d preset ending today or, via the Custom option in `components/reports/ReportRangeControl` (a `DateRangeSheet`), days the user picks, clamped to today and `MAX_CUSTOM_RANGE_DAYS` (180). `useReportCustomization` returns `window`, `rangeProps` (spread onto `ReportScreenLayout`) and `isSectionShown`; every report hook takes `{ window }`, and charts that only know 7d/30d/90d get `window.chartRange`. `ExerciseStatisticsScreen` keeps its own scaffold but uses the same control, default range and section keys (`useExerciseDashboard` / `useCardioSessions` take the custom days as a third argument). Caffeine and alcohol reads the daily totals and goals the Nutrition report loads (same cache keys; no new endpoint), counts an alcohol-free day only when something was logged, and uses a day's caffeine goal else `CAFFEINE_DAILY_LIMIT_MG` (400). Each report can show a `*.insights` card (`ReportInsights`): up to four plain-language lines the screen writes from numbers it already has, each with its own translation key. Each report opens on a row of big-number tiles (`components/reports/ReportHighlights`, section `*.overview`) whose change chip is green or red only where a direction is good or bad (time asleep, sleep score, mood) and grey for calories. A section added to a report needs its key in `constants/reports.ts` and a name in `ReportsSettingsScreen`'s `SECTION_LABELS`, which is a total record. Not on mobile yet: raw stress and body battery series (custom measurement categories), weekly alcohol, and the web's remaining exercise reports.
- Plate calculator: `PlateInventoryScreen` (root-stack `PlateInventory`, a row on `EquipmentScreen`) edits the bars and plates the person owns through `usePlateInventory` / `usePlateInventoryMutations` (`GET/PUT/DELETE /api/v2/plate-inventory`, key `plateInventoryQueryKey`; until something is saved the hook returns the standard preset for the weight unit, so nothing needs setting up first). The action is a **Plate calculator** item in `SetTypeMenu` (the menu a set number opens) shown only for barbell, EZ bar and trap bar exercises (`usesBarbell`), in `ActiveWorkoutScreen` (kg weight shown in the display unit with `storedWeightInUnit`) and `WorkoutFormExerciseList` (the typed weight); it opens `PlateCalculatorSheet`, which runs shared `calculatePlateLoad` and never rounds: a weight the plates cannot make shows the nearest loadable weights below and above. Maths, presets and bar suggestion live in `shared/src/utils/plateCalculator.ts`.
- Ready-made workout programs (strength splits): `WorkoutSplitsScreen` (root-stack `WorkoutSplits`, Library > Workout programs) filters the shared `WORKOUT_SPLITS` catalog by where the person trains (a bigger venue also shows the smaller ones, `splitFitsVenue`), level, goal and days a week, expands a program's sessions and opens `WorkoutSplitImportScreen` (`WorkoutSplitImport`, param `splitId`), which asks for the plan name, a make-active switch and the medical disclaimer (a switch that must be on before **Add program** works). Importing is `POST /api/v2/workout-splits/:splitId/import` through `services/api/workoutSplitsApi.ts` and `hooks/useImportWorkoutSplit.ts`, which refreshes the preset caches (`invalidateWorkoutPresetCaches`) and the plan, diary and exercise caches (`useInvalidateWorkoutPlans`); on success it opens `WorkoutPlans`. The catalog holds ids only, so English names, descriptions and session names are static `t()` keys with inline defaults in `utils/workoutSplits.ts` (`splitName`, `splitDescription`, `sessionName`), mirrored by `workoutSplits.splits.<id>.*` and `workoutSplits.sessions.<id>` in the English catalog. Exercises show as readable free-exercise-db ids until imported.
- Ready-made workout programs (strength splits): `WorkoutSplitsScreen` (root-stack `WorkoutSplits`, Library > Workout programs) filters the shared `WORKOUT_SPLITS` catalog by where the person trains (a bigger venue also shows the smaller ones, `splitFitsVenue`), level, goal and days a week, expands a program's sessions and opens `WorkoutSplitImportScreen` (`WorkoutSplitImport`, param `splitId`), which asks for the plan name, a make-active switch and the medical disclaimer (a switch that must be on before **Add program** works). Importing is `POST /api/v2/workout-splits/:splitId/import` through `services/api/workoutSplitsApi.ts` and `hooks/useImportWorkoutSplit.ts`, which refreshes the preset caches (`invalidateWorkoutPresetCaches`) and the plan, diary and exercise caches (`useInvalidateWorkoutPlans`); on success it opens `WorkoutPlans`. The catalog holds ids only, so English names, descriptions and session names are static `t()` keys with inline defaults in `utils/workoutSplits.ts` (`splitName`, `splitDescription`, `sessionName`), mirrored by `workoutSplits.splits.<id>.*` and `workoutSplits.sessions.<id>` in the English catalog. Exercises show as readable free-exercise-db ids until imported. `WorkoutSplitCard` (`components/`) is the program card both screens use. `WorkoutFinderScreen` (`WorkoutFinder`, from the browse screen's Find my program button) is the program finder: goal, experience, days a week, workout length and where I train as segmented rows plus a focus-muscle chip row feed the shared `recommendWorkoutSplits`, each result card shows the program fitted by `applyTrainerOptions` (its sessions with about-N-minutes), and Add to my workouts opens `WorkoutSplitImport` with a `trainer` param (`venue`, `sessionMinutes`, `focus`) which the import screen sends as `trainer` on the request; the server rebuilds the same program from the answers.
- Reports hub (`ReportsScreen`, reached from a Library row, Settings → Reports and a Reports link beside the Dashboard's Health Trends heading) links `NutritionReportScreen`, `HydrationReportScreen`, `SubstancesReportScreen` (caffeine and alcohol), `SleepAnalyticsScreen`, `MeasurementsReportScreen`, `MoodReportScreen`, `ExerciseStatisticsScreen` and `CompareReportScreen` ("Does A go with B?": a sentence of pickers, its own per-mode span control instead of the 7/30/90d one, and an answer, chart and checks card from `useCompare` over `GET /api/v2/reports/compare`; the phone only formats `response.result`, and a 404 means the server is too old; `'compare'` has no report sections, so the customize screen only reorders/hides it. Its "Since a habit started" choice reads "Since I started [X] regularly, has [Y] changed?" and swaps the span control for `CompareSinceRows` ("Since [date]" through `CalendarSheet`, held to `sinceStartBounds`: 730 days back to yesterday, showing the server's `suggestedStart` marked "(suggested)" until a day is picked, or "Pick a date" when there is none; "Line: [Y] ≥ [value]" through `BottomSheetPicker`, steps on the display scale from `sinceLineOptions`, sent in the stored scale, hidden for a yes/no outcome and for a start-less answer) and the answer, chart and checks for `CompareSinceCard` (verdict, typical range, share at or above the line, regularity, already-changing warning, stats line, and always the not-proof-of-cause caveat) and `CompareSinceChart` (the outcome by day, before and since stretches shaded, a marker at the start, the line dashed across; no dot view) from `useCompareSince`. A picked start is kept per habit and a picked line per outcome, and the rows keep the last answer for the pair while a new one loads). Every new screen shares `components/reports/ReportScreenLayout` (header, 7/30/90d control, scroll body) and the 7/30/90d `utils/trendRange.ts` range. Sleep analytics reads `GET /api/sleep` through `useSleepAnalytics` on the same `sleepRangeQueryKey` as the Health Trends sleep chart, and `utils/sleepAnalytics.ts` takes each day's main sleep (`selectMainSleep`) so a nap never replaces the night. Hydration (`useHydrationReport`, `utils/hydrationReport.ts`) reads `GET /api/measurements/water-intake-range` and the water goal on the keys the Health Trends hydration chart uses; days with no water are left out of every average, like Nutrition. Measurements (`useMeasurementsReport`, `utils/measurementsReport.ts`) reads the check-in range endpoint on `measurementsRangeQueryKey`, keeps the newest edit per day and field in stored kg/cm and converts at the screen edge; weight change is latest minus first in the window and stays a neutral color because whether a loss is good depends on the goal. Nutrition adds macro goal progress, calories by weekday and logging streaks, a fat breakdown card, a vitamins-and-minerals card and a "Your nutrients" card whose rows are picked and ordered in Customize Reports (`shownReportNutrients` + `reportNutrientOrder`; standard nutrients from `REPORT_NUTRIENT_KEYS` plus the account's custom ones, which `GET /api/reports/mini-nutrition-trends` already returns by name). `buildNutrientAverages` averages any key over logged days, drops ones never eaten, and compares with the day's goal (`water_goal_ml` for water, `custom_nutrients[name]` for custom); each row opens `NutrientTrends`, which ends in a Top Sources card (`NutrientTopSourcesCard`): the foods contributing most to that nutrient, from `GET /api/food-entries/range` (`useFoodEntriesRange`, `foodEntriesRangeQueryKey`) ranked by `buildTopSources` in `utils/nutrientSources.ts`, scaled per entry by `quantity / serving_size` like the daily totals. Touching a chart bar (`NutrientBarChart`'s `onSelectDay`) narrows it to that day (that bar stays highlighted, the others dimmed) until cleared, chips narrow it to one meal type, and tapping a source expands the entries behind it, each opening `FoodEntryView`. sleep adds stage shares, weekday-vs-weekend nights, nights of 7h+ and longest/shortest night (7h is `FULL_NIGHT_HOURS`, not a stored goal). Mood reads `GET /api/mood` (`moodApi.ts`, `useMoodReport`, `utils/moodReport.ts`); bands and labels come from the shared mood model. The hub is customizable from its header (`ReportsSettingsScreen`, Customize Reports): the report order and which are hidden (`reportOrder` + `hiddenReports`, reconciled against `constants/reports.ts` with `resolveKeyOrder`), the window a report opens on (`reportDefaultRange`, still switchable on the report without changing the saved default) and, per report, which sections show (`hiddenReportSections`, keys namespaced by report such as `sleep.stages` or `sleep.metric.hrv`; each report screen reads them through `useReportCustomization().isSectionShown`). The window is a `ReportWindow` (`utils/trendRange.ts`): a 7/30/90d preset ending today or, via the Custom option in `components/reports/ReportRangeControl` (a `DateRangeSheet`), days the user picks, clamped to today and `MAX_CUSTOM_RANGE_DAYS` (180). `useReportCustomization` returns `window`, `rangeProps` (spread onto `ReportScreenLayout`) and `isSectionShown`; every report hook takes `{ window }`, and charts that only know 7d/30d/90d get `window.chartRange`. `ExerciseStatisticsScreen` keeps its own scaffold but uses the same control, default range and section keys (`useExerciseDashboard` / `useCardioSessions` take the custom days as a third argument). Caffeine and alcohol reads the daily totals and goals the Nutrition report loads (same cache keys; no new endpoint), counts an alcohol-free day only when something was logged, and uses a day's caffeine goal else `CAFFEINE_DAILY_LIMIT_MG` (400). Each report can show a `*.insights` card (`ReportInsights`): up to four plain-language lines the screen writes from numbers it already has, each with its own translation key. Each report opens on a row of big-number tiles (`components/reports/ReportHighlights`, section `*.overview`) whose change chip is green or red only where a direction is good or bad (time asleep, sleep score, mood) and grey for calories. A section added to a report needs its key in `constants/reports.ts` and a name in `ReportsSettingsScreen`'s `SECTION_LABELS`, which is a total record. Not on mobile yet: raw stress and body battery series (custom measurement categories), weekly alcohol, and the web's remaining exercise reports.
- Fasting uses `FastingDetailScreen` (Fasting / History / Report tabs), `FastingCard`, `FastingProtocolSheet`, `FastingSettingsScreen`, `FastingReport`, `useFasting`, `useFastingTimer`, `utils/fasting.ts`, `utils/fastingReport.ts`, and `services/api/fastingApi.ts`. Settings read and write `GET/PUT /api/fasting/preferences`; the protocol picker matches a saved preset id or name to the preset name, and treats `Custom Fast` as the custom option. The Report tab reads one 90-day `GET /api/fasting/history/range/:start/:end` query and narrows it client-side. The range query waits until preferences have loaded, then uses the profile timezone (`todayInZone`), or UTC when the saved timezone is missing, matching the range endpoint. A failed preferences load shows the report error instead of querying. `pre_end_alert_minutes` feeds the pre-goal notification (0 turns it off), and goal reconciliation waits until those preferences have loaded. A failed load with no cached preferences stays blocked so it does not schedule the 30-minute default. The eating-window alert and auto-calculate flags are stored server-side only.
- Watch-only walk and run: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#watch-only-walk-and-run).
- Distance interval steps: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#distance-interval-steps).
- Watch-only walk/run: started from the Workout page's picker (`WaitingForWorkoutView`), which shows a **Cardio** row above the saved presets whenever `WatchRunStore.canStart`; it opens `CardioChoicesView` (a sheet titled Cardio: Run and Walk tiles side by side under Outdoor and Indoor, then any plans, each opening `PlanPlaceView` to pick outdoor or indoor). The live screens of a wrist-only run (`WatchRunView`) and of the phone's GPS recording (`RecordingView`) share `CardioLiveLayout` (`Presentation/CardioLiveLayout.swift`), drawn like the strength workout: a one-line header (glyph and "Indoor walk", replaced by an orange "Paused"/"Auto-paused" while paused so it never runs under the system clock), the clock large, distance, pace and heart rate in `WatchStyle` tiles, an optional `CardioPlanStep` strip, and `SquircleButton`s (lap, pause/resume in white, finish with a red glyph; `SquircleButton` takes a `tint` for that), with the run's gear (auto-pause, voice cues, Test voice) as a fourth button in that row: in the header it sat beside the system clock and was missed. An outdoor wrist-only run shows `GpsSignalBars` in the header from `WatchRunStore.gpsAccuracy` (the controller's `onGpsAccuracy`, each fix's radius) through `GpsSignal` (10 m or better good, 25 m fair, else weak, none after 15 s without a fix). Once started, `WorkoutView` shows `WatchRunView` in place of the picker (and `ContentView` treats an active run like an active workout, so a hidden Workout page and Now Playing still show), so there is no page of its own and nothing to register on the phone. It is driven by `WatchRunStore` and `WatchRunHealthKitController`: an `HKWorkoutSession` with a live builder collecting distance, active energy and heart rate, shown with the builder's own `elapsedTime`, started indoors (`locationType = .indoor`, distance estimated from motion) or outdoors (`.outdoor`, the watch's GPS gives the distance). Outdoors the controller also runs a `CLLocationManager` (the watch target's Info.plist carries `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription`, written by `expo-target.config.js`) and feeds accurate-enough fixes (horizontal accuracy 0 to 50 m, and only while not paused) to an `HKWorkoutRouteBuilder`, whose route is attached to the workout with `finishRoute(with:)` once `finishWorkout` returns it (and discarded with a discarded workout); a denied location permission leaves a workout with no route. Finish writes the workout to Apple Health AND sends the run to the phone as a queued `runFinished` message (`OutboundPayloads.runFinished`, `WatchSessionManager.sendRunFinished`; a `WatchRunResult` with the builder's distance, energy and active seconds, the accepted route as `[t, lat, lon, alt, hacc, seg]` rows (`seg` goes up on every resume; no altitude is `-9999`) and heart-rate `[t, bpm]` rows, thinned to 1500 and 600 rows). The Health workout is stamped with `SparkyFitnessSessionId` (the run's `clientId`), so the HealthKit import skips it: the message is the only route into the diary, so the phone writes it to a stored queue before anything else. Phone side: `onRunFinished` (module event, same four-place rename rule as the other messages) → `useWatchRunBridge` (mounted in `WatchWorkoutGate`) validates with `parseWatchRunPayload` (`utils/watchRun.ts`) → `enqueueWatchRun` (`services/watchRunQueue.ts`, AsyncStorage, deduped by `clientId` against the queue and the last 50 filed) → `processWatchRunQueue` calls `saveWatchRun` (`services/watchRunSave.ts`: a normal Walking/Running entry from the watch's distance and active time, then the gps-track with splits for an outdoor run, then `watch-telemetry` with heart rate and the measured energy, best effort). When it creates the entry it also gets the steps over the run window (`measureWatchRunForm` in `services/stepDistance.ts`: Apple Health's steps first through `getHealthStepsBetween`, which the watch writes so the phone can be left behind, else the phone's pedometer with `askForPermission: false`, steps scaled by the moving share, ignored when there are under 150 of them or when they imply an impossible stride for the distance, i.e. the phone was not carried) and sends `avg_cadence` and `stride_length_cm` from `computeFormMetrics` against the stored distance (for a treadmill run the figure the wearer entered on the wrist), best effort. `saveWatchRun` resolves to `{ entryId, form }`; the queue stores the form beside the entry id so a retry does not measure again, and hands it to `onFiled` / `announceWatchRun` / `watchRunSummary` as `RunSummaryParams.form` (with `steps`). It retries whenever the server is connected, keeps the entry id so a retry never logs twice, and drops a run only for a non-retryable 4xx. The risk to know: a run whose message never reaches the phone is in Apple Health but not the diary (the stamp hides it from the import). Only one workout session can exist on the watch, so `WatchRunStore.canStart` refuses while a strength workout or the phone-GPS recording's session is live, and `RecordingStore` does not start its heart-rate session while a wrist run is active. A run left going when the watch app is killed is picked back up: while it runs the controller writes a `WatchRunSnapshot` (`Domain/WatchRunSnapshot.swift`: identity, kind, place, route and heart-rate rows, last figures; a file in Application Support, rewritten at most every 15 s and on pause/resume, removed when the run ends or fails) and `WatchSessionManager.recoverLiveWorkoutIfNeeded` asks `WatchRunStore.recoverIfNeeded` first, before the strength-workout path (which would otherwise end the session as a leftover). Only a run with a snapshot is claimed, and only when the recovered Health session is the same activity type; the route builder cannot be re-attached, so the route in Health restarts at the relaunch (the phone still gets every fix, with a new `seg` across the gap). If the Health session was lost but the snapshot survived and holds at least a minute and some distance, the run is sent to the phone as it stood and the summary is shown; a snapshot older than 12 h is dropped. A saved run's snapshot is kept until `WatchRunStore` has queued the message for the phone (so a kill in between still sends it on relaunch, and the phone dedupes by `clientId`); a failed Health session (`didFailWithError`) sends the run on the same way instead of discarding it; a phone-armed strength workout (`handle(workoutStart:)`) is ignored while a wrist run is active, and `canStart` waits for the launch recovery when a snapshot exists. Split cues and laps: `WatchRunStore.cueSplitIfDue` (on each metrics update) buzzes via `Haptics.step()` at every whole km/mile in the phone's distance unit and, when the run's voice cues are on (`WatchRunStore.settings`, a `WatchRunSettings` of `autoPause` and `voiceCues`: taken from the phone's `runStart` request, or for a run begun on the wrist from the context's `runVoiceCues`/`runAutoPause`, the phone's preferences read by `useWatchCheckInBridge` from `getRecordingPreferences` and re-pushed by `onRecordingPreferenceChange` when a switch flips; kept in `WatchRunSnapshot.settings`; changed mid-run from the gear button's sheet on `WatchRunView` or from the phone's `WatchRunLive` switches, sent as `runControl` actions `autoPauseOn`/`autoPauseOff`/`voiceCuesOn`/`voiceCuesOff`, and reported back in `runLive` as `autoPause`/`voiceCues`; turning auto-pause off while it holds the run paused resumes it, and the controller's `updateSettings` resets both detectors), speaks it through `WatchRunCues` (`AVSpeechSynthesizer` on a `.playback`/`.voicePrompt` session that ducks other audio, a session error printed to the device console; only the latest of several splits passed at once). With voice cues on the run also says when it starts (unless a plan's first step does), pauses, resumes, auto-pauses and is saved (`WatchRunCues.finishText`: distance and time), switching them on mid-run says "Voice cues on.", and the gear sheet has a Test voice button that speaks whatever the switch says; a recovered run does not replay splits already passed. The flag button calls `WatchRunStore.lap()` → the controller's `markLap()` (epoch-ms `lapMarks`, 3 s dedupe, kept in `WatchRunSnapshot.lapMarks` and `WatchRunResult.laps`, sent in `runFinished` as `laps`); `saveWatchRun` cuts an outdoor route with `computeLaps` when laps exist, otherwise per-km/mile splits. An indoor run has no route to cut, so the watch also records the distance covered at each mark (`lapDistances`, parallel to `laps`, kept in the snapshot too) and `saveWatchRun` files the laps through `indoorLapWindows` (`utils/watchRun.ts`: start to first mark, mark to mark, last mark to the end; distance from the differences, average heart rate from the readings inside the window) and `attachExerciseEntryLaps` (`POST /api/exercise-entries/:id/laps`, best effort; the posted laps are the whole list, so a repeat replaces them, and the server answers 409 for an entry that has a GPS track). The windows are wall-clock, so a paused stretch counts in a lap's duration. Pace chart: an indoor run also records `distanceSeries` (`[active seconds, metres]` about every 10 s from the builder's elapsed time and distance, thinned to 300 and scaled with the distance by `scalingDistance`; kept in the recovery snapshot), sent in `runFinished`, parsed by `parseWatchRunPayload` and passed by `watchRunSummary` as `RunSummaryParams.distanceSeries`; `RunSummaryScreen` draws it with `PaceChart` through `paceSeriesFromDistance` (`utils/cardioSession.ts`, the same 30 s windows as a track) and moves it with the Treadmill distance correction. `saveWatchRun` also posts the series (best effort) to `POST /api/exercise-entries/:id/distance-series` (`exercise_entry_distance_series`, one row per entry; the treadmill distance correction rescales it server-side like the laps), and `useCardioSessionDetail` reads `GET` of the same path when an entry has no route, so `CardioSessionScreen` draws the same Pace card for an indoor run from the diary. Plans: the phone puts `runPlans` in the context (`buildWatchRunPlans` in `utils/watchRunPlans.ts`: the program workout due, tagged `programId`/`programIndex`, then `INTERVAL_PRESETS`, step names already translated; `useWatchCheckInBridge` reads `useRunProgram`), the watch decodes them as `WatchRunPlan` (`ContextPayloadMapper.runPlans`) and `CardioChoicesView` lists them under **Follow a plan** (then Outdoor/Indoor). `WatchRunHealthKitController.start(_:place:plan:)` keeps the plan (and in `WatchRunSnapshot.plan`, so a relaunch carries on), `WatchRunStore` runs a 1 s timer (`cueStepIfDue`) against the builder's active `elapsed`, so pauses never count: a new step taps (`Haptics.step`), is spoken with Voice cues on, and forces a lap mark (`markLap(force: true)`, not the first step), and `WatchRunView.planStep` shows the step, the time left and the next one. `runFinished` carries `planId`, `programId`/`programIndex` (a program workout only) and `planCompleted` (`WatchRunPlan.completed(at:)`, the same rule as `planCompleted` on the phone: every step has run its full length); the module forwards them as `onRunFinished` fields (`programIndex` -1 = none), `parseWatchRunPayload` reads them and `watchRunQueue.tickOffProgram` calls `completeProgramWorkout(programId, index, day of the run)` after the entry is filed (best effort, safe to repeat since the server only moves on for the due index). Summary: when `processWatchRunQueue` files a run it calls its `onFiled` listener, which `useWatchRunBridge` points at `announceWatchRun` (`services/watchRunToast.ts`): a toast (8 s) saying the run is saved with its distance and time, whose tap opens `RunSummary` with `watchRunSummary` (`utils/watchRunSummary.ts`: the watch's own distance and time, the splits, elevation and marked laps from the route outdoors, the laps from `indoorLapWindows` indoors, average heart rate from the readings, and for a completed program workout the program part from the cache the queue just updated via `programSummary`). A listener failing never undoes a filed run. Queue behaviour: a program tick-off that fails for a reason that may pass (offline, 5xx, 401/408/429) keeps the run queued and unannounced, since the stored entry id makes the retry repeat only the later steps; `markDone` is written before the run leaves the queue, so a stop between the two refiles onto the stored entry instead of filing a second one; a caller that joins a running pass makes it look again before it ends (`again`); `useWatchRunBridge` does not drain until the preferences are loaded (the distance unit decides the lap windows) and retries every 60 s while `hasQueuedWatchRuns()`. The plan list reaches the watch with the next context push, so a program change shows after the phone app is opened. Outdoor runs auto-pause: `Domain/WatchAutoPause.swift` is a Swift port of `stepAutoPause` (same thresholds; keep them in step), fed by the controller from every `CLLocation` (speed, accuracy) even while paused, calling `onAutoPause`; `WatchRunStore.applyAutoPause` pauses (only while the run's `settings.autoPause` is on) (`autoPaused`, "Auto-paused" headline, stop haptic) and only ever resumes a pause it made. It is not backdated. Indoors there is no speed, so `Domain/WatchIndoorAutoPause.swift` works from `CMPedometer` step counts instead (the controller's `startMotion`, only for `.indoor`, including after a recovery; `NSMotionUsageDescription` is in the watch Info.plist written by `expo-target.config.js`): a once-a-second timer feeds it the latest cumulative count (the pedometer keeps reporting while the Health session is paused, which is how a pause is undone), no new step for 8 s running / 10 s walking pauses, and 5 steps after an auto-pause resume. Same `onAutoPause` path and the same rules (only an auto pause is auto-resumed). It only judges once the pedometer has delivered a reading (`pedometerLive`), so a denied permission, a still-open first prompt or a missing step counter leaves only the pause button; a pedometer error switches it off. `isWatchRecordedRun` (source bundle `<phone-bundle>.watchkitapp`) still titles an unstamped watch-app workout "Outdoor walk"/"Indoor run" on import (runs recorded before the hand-off existed). The watch Swift has not been compiled outside Xcode.
- Training calendar and load by sport: `TrainingCalendarScreen` (root-stack `TrainingCalendar`, a Library row, also opened from the load card) shows a month or week of planned against completed work from `GET /api/reports/training-calendar?start&end` (`fetchTrainingCalendar`, `useTrainingCalendar`, refreshed on focus). It always asks for the month grid of the anchor day (`monthRange` in `src/utils/trainingCalendar.ts`, from the account's first day of the week), so switching to the week view and back does not refetch. Each day cell shows a small sport icon per planned item (running, cycling, walking, cardio (gym machines such as a stair climber, swimming, rowing), strength, other; `SPORT_ICONS`) coloured by status (`done`, `partial`, `missed`, `upcoming`) and a faded icon per extra, unplanned workout (`DayMarks`, at most 3, then `+N`; every icon and the cell carry a screen reader label with the sport and status). Today has a ring around its number and the selected day a filled box, so both show when they are the same day; an empty day's number is muted. Each grid row ends with that week's total (`done/planned`, or just the sessions logged when nothing was planned) and tapping it selects the week and marks its row in the list. Tapping a day lists its planned and completed items below the grid (a day with nothing says Rest day, except far ahead) (a run program item opens `RunProgramWorkout` for that workout; the Training programs screen's "Open the training calendar" row opens this screen, which replaced the old full-screen program calendar), and the totals card lists the weeks with a done / partly done / missed progress bar each (`WeekProgressBar`), the week holding today first, then the two most recent past weeks and the weeks ahead, older weeks behind Show earlier weeks (`orderWeeks`). "Of N" is shown only when something was planned, and then compares only the time and load that counted for a planned item with a length (`matchedMinutes`, `matchedLoad`), with the rest shown as "+N extra", and tapping the training load figure opens a short explanation that reuses the Exercise Statistics load wording. `SportLoadCard` (in the `exercise.load` section of Exercise Statistics, under `FitnessFatigueCard`) reads the same endpoint for the last ~9 weeks (`sportLoadRange`): this week's and this month's load split by sport (running, cycling, walking, strength, other), eight stacked weekly bars, today's fitness, fatigue and form for context, and a line saying how load is estimated (heart-rate weighted; moderate weight 2 without one; effort ratings are not used). `invalidateExerciseCache` also invalidates `trainingLoad`, `fitnessFatigue` and `trainingCalendar`, and `runProgramService.setCache` invalidates `trainingCalendar`, so a saved workout, a plan change or a program change refreshes all three.
- Zone bar and pace standing on the watch's cardio screens: the phone sends the five zone floors as `runHeartRateZoneFloors` in the context (`utils/watchHeartRateZones.ts` from `useHeartRateZones`, validated again by `WatchHeartRateZones.normalized`; the zone rule is a port of shared `heartRateZoneFor`, keep them in step) and `CardioLiveLayout` draws `ZoneBar` (five segments, the current zone at full strength, a marker at the reading) between the clock and the tiles for both the wrist-only run (`metrics.heartRate`) and the phone recording (`liveHeartRate`); no floors, no bar. `WatchRunStore.paceStanding` (`WatchPaceStanding`: too fast, on target, too slow against the run's or the plan step's range, no margin or sustain time, unlike the alert) turns the pace tile's caption into that word and tints it. Wear OS does not draw either yet. The watch Swift has not been compiled outside Xcode.
- Pace by zone and Recommended today: `PaceByZoneCard` (Exercise Statistics, Cardio view) reads `usePaceByZone` (four calendar months of runs through `fetchCardioSessionsWindow`, placed in zones 2 and 3 by average heart rate with shared `buildPaceByZone`; the same window fetch feeds `useRunningTrends`). `RecommendedTodayCard` (top of `RunProgramsScreen` while a program is on) lays the next workout out with `buildRecommendedSession` (`utils/recommendedSession.ts`: zone and pace per step, repeated pairs folded, no pace quoted for a beginner run/walk) and words why it fits from `useTrainingLoad` through `sessionReason`.
- Fuel and drink reminders on the watch: a wrist-only run can give fuel and drink reminders on its own clock, so they keep coming with the phone left at home. `Domain/WatchFueling.swift` is the Swift port of the phone's schedule (`WatchFuelingPlan`: carbohydrate interval and portion, drink interval and amount; `WatchFueling.next` / `upcoming` / `WatchFuelingState.resumed`; the first carbohydrate reminder comes after one interval or 45 minutes if that is longer, then every interval, drinks every interval from the start, matching `shared/src/utils/fuelingPlan.ts` and `drinkPlan.ts`; keep them in step). The plan is `WatchRunSettings.fueling` (optional, so older snapshots decode and the settings the controller already saves carry it), chosen per run in the Alerts list of the Cardio sheet (`FuelingSetupRows`, shared by the standard and classic layouts through `WatchRunAlertSetup.fueling`; off until turned on, like the other alerts) and switchable mid-run from the gear sheet (`WatchRunStore.setFueling`, which counts what has passed as given). The store's 1 s timer (`cueFuelingIfDue`, which also runs for a run with only fueling on) follows the builder's active clock, so a pause never brings one early; a due reminder is a `Haptics.fuel()` tap, the orange `CardioFuelStrip` under the clock for a minute (a tap dismisses it) and, with Voice cues on, the words (`WatchRunCues.fuelingText`); otherwise the strip counts down to the next one. Several due at once (a gap) give one reminder, and a recovered run (`WatchFuelingState.resumed`) does not repeat what has passed. The amounts start from what the phone last planned: the Fueling plan screen (Use on Apple Watch, iOS only, or setting phone reminders) saves them with `services/watchFuelingPreference.ts` and `useWatchCheckInBridge` sends them in the context as `runFuelIntervalMin`, `runFuelPortionGrams`, `runDrinkIntervalMin` and `runDrinkMl` (`WatchContext.runFueling`, clamped to the stepper ranges on both sides); with none the watch uses 25 min / 25 g and 20 min / 150 ml. A run is reminded by the watch or by the phone's notifications, not both: the phone's reminders mirror to the watch when it is locked, so use the watch's own for a run with the watch. Not yet: logging the portions taken to the diary from the watch (the watch has no taken-it button and `runFinished` carries no fuel events), and Wear OS. The pure parts (the schedule, the state, the context mapping and the store's cue and strip logic) were compiled and run with a Swift 6 toolchain on Linux; the SwiftUI views, the strip layout and the start-screen rows have only been read, so verify them on a real watch after `npx expo prebuild --clean`.
- Distance interval steps (#244): a plan step can carry `meters` (a distance step that ends once that many metres were recorded in it; `seconds` is only its expected time at `EXPECTED_SECONDS_PER_METER`); `workMeters` in `buildIntervalPlan` makes the work steps distance steps and `sixBy400m`/`fiveBy1k` are presets. `advanceIntervalCursor(plan, cursor, activeSeconds, activeMeters)` in `shared/src/utils/runIntervals.ts` moves an `IntervalCursor` past every step that has ended and `intervalPosition` takes `{ cursor, meters }` and reports `remainingMeters` on a distance step; the phone stores the cursor as `session.intervals.cursor` (a session from an earlier build is placed again from the clock). Distance plans are outdoor only (`IntervalSetup`'s `distanceAllowed`). A distance step is shown and spoken in the unit it was set in (`IntervalStep.unit`, `'mi'` or absent for metres; `stepDistanceText`, `spokenStepDistance`): presets are metres, as on a track, and Custom offers a Meters / Miles switch only when the account distance unit is miles (`IntervalSetup`'s `milesAvailable`; `workUnit` in `IntervalOptions`, quarter-mile steps from 0.25 to 6 mi, stored as whole metres). The "to go" cue is 100 m, or 0.1 mi on a mile step (`stepToGoMeters`). The watch gets the unit as the step's `unit` and mirrors these rules in `WatchRunPlan.distance` and `WatchRunCues`. On the watch `WatchRunPlan.Step.meters` marks a distance step and the controller keeps a `WatchRunPlan.Cursor` (a Swift copy of `advanceIntervalCursor`, saved as `WatchRunSnapshot.planCursor`; keep the two cursor rules in step); `runLive` puts the metres left in `stepLabel` with no `stepRemaining`. A plan with a distance step is complete once every step has ended, by time or by the distance recorded in it (`planCompleted` on the phone passes the cursor and metres; the watch uses `WatchRunPlan.completed(cursor:)`), and the voice coach gives such a step no ten-second warning or countdown and the plan no halfway or last-minute call, since its length is only a guess (`nextCoachingCues` and `WatchCoaching.next` take the cursor and metres).
- Workout counted twice in the calorie balance (#2353): `ActivityDetailScreen`'s edit mode has a **Counted in daily total** card (`calories_applied`, held as `caloriesApplied` in `useActivityForm`; blank sends null so the estimate counts, 0 counts nothing). Session and workout totals (`getSessionCalories`, `calculateExerciseStats`, `WorkoutDetailScreen`) go through the shared `entryCaloriesCounted`, never `calories_burned` directly. Linking a logged workout to the same session synced from a watch: `components/LinkSyncedWorkoutSheet.tsx` (opened from a Link a synced workout row on `ActivityDetailScreen` and `WorkoutDetailScreen`, only for a workout whose source is `manual`) over `useCalorieLink` and `exerciseApi` (`/api/exercise-entries/:id/calorie-link`). It lists that day's synced workouts with the server's match hint, pre-selects a `sets_within` match but never links on its own, and the screens update their local session from the returned `applied` values. Preset workouts have no per-entry counted-calories field; they only get the link.
- Personal records: `PersonalRecordsScreen` (root-stack `PersonalRecords`, a Library row) lists the cardio records from `GET /api/exercise-stats/prs` (`fetchPersonalRecords`, `usePersonalRecords`, `staleTime: 0` and re-read on focus, since a run saved a moment ago can set one; the server finds them per sport group and distance standard, so only `cardioPRs` is shown and `groupPersonalRecords` keeps sports apart). `RunSummaryScreen` shows a "New personal record" card for the records the saved run now holds (`recordsHeldBy(records, entryId)`), which needs `RunSummaryParams.entryId`: `handleSave` passes the saved entry, and for a watch run `drainOnce` hands `saveWatchRun`'s entry id to the `onFiled` listener, `announceWatchRun` and `watchRunSummary`.
