- Routes: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#routes).
- Pace alert: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#pace-alert).
- Voice cues on the phone: `WatchRunCues.speak` hands each cue to the phone when it is reachable and the watch is not already on Bluetooth headphones (`WatchSessionManager.relayRunCue`: a live `runCue` message `{id, text, interrupt}`), because the wearer's headphones are usually paired to the phone. The module emits `onRunCue`, `useWatchRunBridge` answers at once with `sendRunCueAck(id)` (a live `runCueAck`) and `services/watchRunCueRelay.ts` speaks it through `recordingCues` (an `interrupt` cue goes first and drops queued ones, others queue). With no ack within 2 s, or a failed send, the watch says the cue itself, so a phone that is asleep or has the bridge off loses nothing. The words are the watch's own text in the watch's language: the message carries it as `language` (a BCP 47 tag of the language the watch's catalog resolved to, not the settings language; empty from Wear OS and for cues that contain words the phone wrote in the app language, such as a plan's step names and start and done sentences or a route's own wording); when the watch has to speak such a cue itself it uses the context's `appLanguage` for the voice, and its own words and durations are formatted in the catalog language and the phone speaks it with a voice for that language, falling back to the app language when none is sent. Same four-place rename rule (`runCue`, `runCueAck`).
- Live view of a wrist-only run on the phone: while `WatchRunStore` is running or paused it sends `runLive` (`OutboundPayloads.runLive` via `WatchSessionManager.sendRunLive`: `sendMessage` only while reachable, never queued, at most every 2 s unless forced by a status change, a lap or an auto-pause; `ended` goes first when the run is finished) carrying the run id, kind, place, status, active seconds, distance, heart rate, energy, laps and the plan step (label, time left, next). The phone module routes it as `onRunLive` (same four-place rename rule), `useWatchRunBridge` feeds `stores/watchRunLiveStore.ts` (in memory; ignores a message older than the one held, clears on `ended`, on `onRunFinished`, and after `WATCH_RUN_LIVE_MAX_AGE_MS` of silence through a 5 s sweep) and `useWatchRunLive()` moves the clock and step countdown on locally between messages while running. `ActiveWorkoutBar` shows it through `useWatchRunBarContent` (after a strength workout or a phone recording, which take the bar first; `useActiveWorkoutBarPadding` reserves room via `useHasWatchRunLive`) and a tap opens `WatchRunLiveScreen` (root-stack `WatchRunLive`, hidden-bar route): stats plus Pause/Resume, Lap, Finish and Discard that are sent to the watch (see the next bullet).
- Phone starts the watch: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#phone-starts-the-watch).
- Treadmill distance: see [exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md](agent-docs/exercise-workouts-and-fasting.md#treadmill-distance).
- Phone starts the watch (walk and run): when a watch is paired (`useWatchConnectivity`) and the activity is a walk or run, `RecordActivityScreen` asks Outdoor/Indoor first (`indoor`) and then shows **Record with** (`recordWith`: `phone` (default) or `watch`, labelled Phone GPS / Watch GPS outdoors and Phone / Watch indoors, each with its own hint); a ride is phone-only. Indoor + Phone is the step-counted phone session above, Indoor + Watch the watch's treadmill run. The Auto-pause and Voice cues switches (`components/recording/RecordingSwitchRow.tsx`) show for both devices and save the same preferences; auto-pause is hidden (and sent off) only for a phone indoor session, which gets no location fixes to judge by (the watch judges indoors by its step counter), and the voice picker is phone-only. With a watch choice Start calls `startRunOnWatch` (`services/watchRunControl.ts`, which sends the two switches as `autoPause`/`voiceCues` in `runStart`) instead of `startRecording`, then opens `WatchRunLive`. Messages (same four-place rename rule): `runStart` {requestId, kind, place, requestedAt (ms), optional plan from `buildWatchStartPlan`} is sent by the module's `startRun` with `transferUserInfo` (queued) plus `sendMessage` when reachable, then `launchWatchApp(activity:indoor:)` opens the watch app so the `HKWorkoutConfiguration` location type matches; `runControl` {runId, action pause|resume|lap|finish|discard, controlId} via `sendRunControl`, same transports. On the watch `WatchSessionManager.route` hands them to `WatchRunStore.startRequested` (dedupes by requestId, ignores a request older than 120 s or while `!canStart`) and `applyRemote` (dedupes by controlId, needs `runId == healthKit.runId`). The watch's `runLive` carries the `requestId` (nil for a run begun on the wrist), which settles the phone's pending request in `watchRunLiveStore` (`pending`, shown as "Starting on your watch…" for up to `WATCH_RUN_PENDING_MAX_AGE_MS`; the stale-run sweep leaves it alone). The run still files through `runFinished`, so a phone that goes away mid-run loses nothing; the wrist-only start (Cardio row) is unchanged. The watch Swift has not been compiled outside Xcode.
- Treadmill distance: a watch run recorded indoors has no route, so its distance is the watch's motion estimate. `watchRunSummary` sets `RunSummaryParams.editableDistance` (indoor and filed), and `RunSummaryScreen` then shows a "Treadmill distance" card: the person types what the machine showed (in their distance unit), `setExerciseEntryDistance` posts it to `POST /api/exercise-entries/:id/distance` (km), and the screen swaps the figure, the pace and the lap distances (scaled by the same factor, as the server does). Indoor distance learning: the watch's indoor figure is Apple's motion estimate, which the app cannot retrain, so it learns a correction factor instead, one per walk and run (`utils/indoorDistanceScale.ts` `learnScale`: the first correction is taken as is, later ones averaged geometrically over at most three, clamped to 0.5–2, an entry within 1% of what was shown teaches nothing). `services/watchIndoorScale.ts` stores it and `useWatchCheckInBridge` sends it as `indoorDistanceScale` (`{walk, run}`). The watch (`WatchRunStore.distanceScale`, set at start and on recovery from `IndoorDistanceScale.factor`) multiplies its live indoor distance and the finished run by it, and sends the factor as `distanceScale` in `runFinished` (the phone module's `normalizedRunEvent` passes it and `estimatedDistanceMeters` through; it forwards only listed keys). An indoor finish first asks "Treadmill distance?" on the wrist (`Phase.confirmingDistance`, `TreadmillDistanceView` with the strength keypad, or Keep watch distance); the run is held, its snapshot kept, until the answer, and an entered figure goes out as `distanceMeters` with the shown one as `estimatedDistanceMeters`. The indoor Health workout is held until that answer (`WatchRunHealthKitController.end` keeps the builder, `finishHeld` writes it): HealthKit cannot lower distance already collected, so a larger final figure is topped up with a sample for the difference and a smaller one leaves the measured distance. The phone learns from that pair in `watchRunQueue` once the run is filed, and from a correction on the Treadmill distance card (`RunSummaryParams.watchIndoor`, the factor applied and the distance shown before any correction). It is only reachable from the filed-run toast; there is no later edit screen.
- Fasting uses `FastingDetailScreen`, `FastingCard`, `FastingProtocolSheet`, `useFasting`, `useFastingTimer`, `utils/fasting.ts`, and `services/api/fastingApi.ts`.
- `FastingGoalReconciler` is mounted headlessly on `DashboardScreen`; it owns goal-notification reconciliation and app-resume refetch even when the visible fasting card is hidden.
- Fasting goal notifications are gated by the app notifications toggle; ending/canceling a fast clears scheduled notifications.

## Dashboard, Diary, Measurements, And Nutrients

- `DashboardScreen` and `DiaryScreen` share date navigation patterns and support gesture-driven date movement.
- `DashboardScreen` drives hydration quick-add, card visibility, fasting summary, health trends, and widget sync.
- Hydration reminders are local notifications owned by the headless `HydrationReminderReconciler` on `DashboardScreen` (always today, never `selectedDate`). `computeReminderSchedule` (`utils/hydrationReminder.ts`) builds a chain of up to 12 reminder times from the day's latest water `logged_at`, each inside the user's `[start, end)` window; `reconcileWaterReminders` (`hooks/useHydrationReminder.ts`) persists the scheduled ids under a signature and cancels/reschedules only when that signature changes. A chain rather than one ping because a scheduled notification cannot reschedule itself while the app is closed. Any mutation that logs water must invalidate `waterIntakeLogQueryKey(date)` or the reconciler keeps the old anchor. Paired watches receive these through OS notification mirroring; there is no watch-side code.
- Mood check-in reminders are device-local prefs (`checkinRemindersEnabled`, `checkinReminderTimes`: sorted, de-duplicated `HH:MM`, max 5, default `['20:30']`; `utils/checkinReminderTimes.ts`). Settings → Notifications → Mood check-ins (`NotificationSettingsScreen`) only edits the prefs: the toggle requests OS permission and turns on only when granted, rows share one `TimeSheet` keyed by an editing index, and Add picks noon or the next free hour. Scheduling is the job of `MoodCheckinReminderReconciler`, mounted in `App.tsx`: it cancels every `mood-checkin` notification and reschedules daily `mood_checkin_<index>` triggers (channel `mood-checkin`), and schedules nothing without the master notifications toggle or granted permission. Tapping a reminder opens MoodLog via `initMoodCheckinNotificationResponses` (`services/moodCheckinNotificationHandler.ts`, wired in `useAppStartup`; dedupes repeated deliveries and handles cold start through `getLastNotificationResponse`). After a user's first new check-in, `services/offerCheckinReminders.ts` (once-per-device flag in `services/checkinReminderOffer.ts`) offers reminders once per device, in both the one-entry-a-day form and the multi-check-in composer (new check-ins only, never edits); it is shown after `goBack` and never blocks leaving the check-in. Inside the guided check-in it waits until the photos step closes the flow (Finish, the X, system Back or a swipe; the step-bar Back only returns to step 2). Leaving at an earlier step keeps it in the recorded progress, so a later Continue makes it when that check-in finishes; otherwise it waits for the next new check-in.
- Fueling reminders: `FuelingPlanScreen` (root-stack `FuelingPlan`, a Library row) shows the plan from the shared `computeFuelingPlan` (duration, heart rate zone, gut training, optional carbs-per-hour and portion overrides) and starts or stops one reminder session. `utils/fuelingReminder.ts` (`fuelingReminderTimes`) turns the plan's offsets into dates from a start time (offsets already past are dropped, capped at 30 so iOS's 64 pending limit stays free for the other reminders); `services/fuelingReminders.ts` (`startFuelingSession`, `cancelFuelingSession`, `getFuelingSession`) is the one-session-at-a-time store under `@SparkyFitness/fuelingSession` behind a promise queue, replaces the old session only once there is something to schedule, and cancels what it scheduled if the ids cannot be saved. `scheduleFuelingReminderNotifications` (`services/notifications.ts`, channel `fueling`) is all-or-nothing and never prompts; the screen asks for permission when Start is pressed. Unlike water reminders it is something the person starts on purpose, so there is no preference flag, only the master notifications toggle. Not built yet: logging the carbs from the reminder, live recalculation, drink and sodium reminders.
- Fueling reminders: `FuelingPlanScreen` (root-stack `FuelingPlan`, a Library row) shows the plan from the shared `computeFuelingPlan` (duration, heart rate zone, gut training, optional carbs-per-hour and portion overrides) and starts or stops one reminder session. `utils/fuelingReminder.ts` (`fuelingReminderTimes`) turns the plan's offsets into dates from a start time (offsets already past are dropped, capped at 30 so iOS's 64 pending limit stays free for the other reminders); `services/fuelingReminders.ts` (`startFuelingSession`, `cancelFuelingSession`, `getFuelingSession`) is the one-session-at-a-time store under `@SparkyFitness/fuelingSession` behind a promise queue, replaces the old session only once there is something to schedule, and cancels what it scheduled if the ids cannot be saved. `scheduleFuelingReminderNotifications` (`services/notifications.ts`, channel `fueling`) is all-or-nothing and never prompts; the screen asks for permission when Start is pressed. Unlike water reminders it is something the person starts on purpose, so there is no preference flag, only the master notifications toggle. Each reminder carries a **Log it** action (category `fueling-reminder`, `opensAppToForeground: true`, so the app opens and uses its signed-in client): `services/fuelingNotificationHandler.ts` (`initFuelingNotificationResponses`, wired in `useAppStartup`, handles the cold-start press through `getLastNotificationResponse`, dedupes a repeat delivery and lets a failed log be pressed again) calls `services/fuelingLog.ts` (`logFuelingPortion`), which creates a standalone diary entry (`utils/fuelingLog.ts`: product name, one serving, the portion as carbs, 4 kcal per gram, no saved food) in the visible Snacks meal type (else the one for the time of day) and invalidates the food caches. A plain tap on the reminder only opens the app and logs nothing. The product name comes from the screen's Fuel product field (default Energy gel) and rides in the notification `data`. Not built yet: picking a saved food as the product, live recalculation, drink and sodium reminders.
- Fueling reminders: `FuelingPlanScreen` (root-stack `FuelingPlan`, a Library row) shows the plan from the shared `computeFuelingPlan` (duration, heart rate zone, gut training, optional carbs-per-hour and portion overrides) and starts or stops one reminder session. `utils/fuelingReminder.ts` (`fuelingReminderTimes`) turns the plan's offsets into dates from a start time (offsets already past are dropped, capped at 30 so iOS's 64 pending limit stays free for the other reminders); `services/fuelingReminders.ts` (`startFuelingSession`, `cancelFuelingSession`, `getFuelingSession`) is the one-session-at-a-time store under `@SparkyFitness/fuelingSession` behind a promise queue, replaces the old session only once there is something to schedule, and cancels what it scheduled if the ids cannot be saved. `scheduleFuelingReminderNotifications` (`services/notifications.ts`, channel `fueling`) is all-or-nothing and never prompts; the screen asks for permission when Start is pressed. Unlike water reminders it is something the person starts on purpose, so there is no preference flag, only the master notifications toggle. Each reminder carries a **Log it** action (category `fueling-reminder`, `opensAppToForeground: true`, so the app opens and uses its signed-in client): `services/fuelingNotificationHandler.ts` (`initFuelingNotificationResponses`, wired in `useAppStartup`, handles the cold-start press through `getLastNotificationResponse`, dedupes a repeat delivery and lets a failed log be pressed again) calls `services/fuelingLog.ts` (`logFuelingPortion`), which creates a standalone diary entry (`utils/fuelingLog.ts`: product name, one serving, the portion as carbs, 4 kcal per gram, no saved food) in the visible Snacks meal type (else the one for the time of day) and invalidates the food caches. A plain tap on the reminder only opens the app and logs nothing. The product name comes from the screen's Fuel product field (default Energy gel) and rides in the notification `data`. The product can be a saved food: `components/FuelFoodSheet.tsx` (a searchable list over `useFoodsLibrary`, opened from the screen's Choose a saved food) sets the name and makes the portion that food's carbohydrates per serving (a food with none is refused). The session and the reminder `data` then carry a `FuelingFoodRef` (food id, variant id, serving size and unit), and Log it creates a normal linked entry of one serving (quantity = the variant's serving size) so the server takes the nutrition from the food; typing over the name or the portion drops the link, and if the food was deleted since, `logFuelingPortion` falls back to a standalone entry so the carbs are not lost. Not built yet: live recalculation, drink and sodium reminders.
- Fueling reminders: `FuelingPlanScreen` (root-stack `FuelingPlan`, a Library row) shows the plan from the shared `computeFuelingPlan` (duration, heart rate zone, gut training, optional carbs-per-hour and portion overrides) and starts or stops one reminder session. `utils/fuelingReminder.ts` (`fuelingReminderTimes`) turns the plan's offsets into dates from a start time (offsets already past are dropped, capped at 30 so iOS's 64 pending limit stays free for the other reminders); `services/fuelingReminders.ts` (`startFuelingSession`, `cancelFuelingSession`, `getFuelingSession`) is the one-session-at-a-time store under `@SparkyFitness/fuelingSession` behind a promise queue, replaces the old session only once there is something to schedule, and cancels what it scheduled if the ids cannot be saved. `scheduleFuelingReminderNotifications` (`services/notifications.ts`, channel `fueling`) is all-or-nothing and never prompts; the screen asks for permission when Start is pressed. Unlike water reminders it is something the person starts on purpose, so there is no preference flag, only the master notifications toggle. Each reminder carries a **Log it** action (category `fueling-reminder`, `opensAppToForeground: true`, so the app opens and uses its signed-in client): `services/fuelingNotificationHandler.ts` (`initFuelingNotificationResponses`, wired in `useAppStartup`, handles the cold-start press through `getLastNotificationResponse`, dedupes a repeat delivery and lets a failed log be pressed again) calls `services/fuelingLog.ts` (`logFuelingPortion`), which creates a standalone diary entry (`utils/fuelingLog.ts`: product name, one serving, the portion as carbs, 4 kcal per gram, no saved food) in the visible Snacks meal type (else the one for the time of day) and invalidates the food caches. A plain tap on the reminder only opens the app and logs nothing. The product name comes from the screen's Fuel product field (default Energy gel) and rides in the notification `data`. The product can be a saved food: `components/FuelFoodSheet.tsx` (a searchable list over `useFoodsLibrary`, opened from the screen's Choose a saved food) sets the name and makes the portion that food's carbohydrates per serving (a food with none is refused). The session and the reminder `data` then carry a `FuelingFoodRef` (food id, variant id, serving size and unit), and Log it creates a normal linked entry of one serving (quantity = the variant's serving size) so the server takes the nutrition from the food; typing over the name or the portion drops the link, and if the food was deleted since, `logFuelingPortion` falls back to a standalone entry so the carbs are not lost. Race day: `components/fueling/FuelingRaceCard.tsx` reads the race from the running goals (`useRunGoals`), keeps the build-up's start day per race (`services/gutTrainingStart.ts`, AsyncStorage) and shows shared `buildGutTrainingRamp` against the plan's own race-day amount (the plan with nothing typed over it, so using this week's amount does not move the target); **Plan this run at N g/h** fills the carbohydrate field. Drink reminders are a separate chain beside the carbohydrate one (`scheduleDrinkReminderNotifications`, same `fueling` channel, no Log it action; amounts from shared `computeDrinkPlan`, off by default, flagged above 800 ml an hour); the session stores both sets of ids so Stop reaches all, and a drink chain that cannot be set cancels the carbohydrate one. Not built yet: live recalculation, sodium and sweat planning.
- Mood check-in reminders are device-local prefs (`checkinRemindersEnabled`, `checkinReminderTimes`: sorted, de-duplicated `HH:MM`, max 5, default `['20:30']`; `utils/checkinReminderTimes.ts`). Settings → Notifications → Mood check-ins (`NotificationSettingsScreen`) only edits the prefs: the toggle requests OS permission and turns on only when granted, rows share one `TimeSheet` keyed by an editing index, and Add picks noon or the next free hour. Scheduling is the job of `MoodCheckinReminderReconciler`, mounted in `App.tsx`: it cancels every `mood-checkin` notification and reschedules daily `mood_checkin_<index>` triggers (channel `mood-checkin`), and schedules nothing without the master notifications toggle or granted permission. Tapping a reminder opens MoodLog via `initMoodCheckinNotificationResponses` (`services/moodCheckinNotificationHandler.ts`, wired in `useAppStartup`; dedupes repeated deliveries and handles cold start through `getLastNotificationResponse`). After a user's first new check-in, `services/offerCheckinReminders.ts` (once-per-device flag in `services/checkinReminderOffer.ts`) offers reminders once per device, in both the one-entry-a-day form and the multi-check-in composer (new check-ins only, never edits); it is shown after `goBack` and never blocks leaving the check-in.
- Oral care (#1862): `OralCareScreen` (route `OralCare`, the day's goal, streak, last 7 days, sessions and goal pickers) and `OralCareLogScreen` (modal `OralCareLog`: `date`, `session` to edit, `careType`, `startTimer`), reached from the + menu's `oralCare` row and the opt-in `OralCareCard` (`oralCareCardVisible`, off by default). Data is `/api/v2/oral-care` through `services/api/oralCareApi.ts` and `hooks/useOralCare.ts` (keys under `oralCareQueryKeyRoot`, every write invalidates the root). `components/oralCare/BrushingTimerModal.tsx` times a brush from the wall clock in four quarters and saves it with `provider: 'timer'`. Counts, the goal and the streak come from the server; a manual brush a synced one also recorded arrives with `covered_by_sync` and counts once. Apple Health (iOS only, Health Connect has no record): the `toothbrushing` read metric (`Toothbrushing` → `HKCategoryTypeIdentifierToothbrushingEvent`, shared `handleCategorySession`, transformer skips `isOwnRecord`) and the `toothbrushing` writeback metric (`platforms: ['ios']` in `WritebackMetrics.ts`; writes only `manual`/`timer` brushing sessions with a duration, tracked by UUID per day). Brushing reminders: `oralCareRemindersEnabled` / `oralCareReminderTimes` (max 3, default 08:00 and 21:00), scheduled by `OralCareReminderReconciler` through `services/oralCareReminderService.ts` (channel `oral-care`); the time rows on `NotificationSettingsScreen` share `useReminderTimeRows` (`components/ReminderTimeRows.tsx`) with mood check-ins. The timer plays `playIntervalCue('rest')` at each quarter and `playRestCompleteSound` at the target (the rest timer sound setting), schedules `scheduleBrushingTimerNotification` for the target while running (shown only when the app is not active), and drives the `BrushingLiveActivity` (`services/brushingLiveActivity.ios.ts`, layout `BrushingLiveActivityLayout.tsx`, link `oral-care`; also the Apple Watch Smart Stack). All three Live Activities copy their icon through `services/liveActivityAppIcon.ts`. `OralCareReportScreen` (Reports hub `oralCare`, sections `ORAL_CARE_SECTIONS`) reads `useOralCareRange` and the shared `summarizeOralCareRange`. The `oral-care` icon is Material Community `tooth-outline` on both platforms (`mci` in `Icon.tsx`); the read and write metrics sit in the `Hygiene` category.
- Allergen tracking: `AllergenSettingsScreen` (reached from Food Settings) manages `/api/allergen-preferences` through `useAllergenPreferences`; `AllergenBadges` marks tracked allergens on food rows and diary entries, and `AllergenWarning` is the banner on `FoodEntryAdd` (so barcode scans warn). Matching lives in `utils/allergens.ts`; foods carry `allergens`/`traces` on their default variant.
- App icon quick actions (long-press on the app icon, `expo-quick-actions`, iOS and Android): `useQuickActions` (mounted by `QuickActionsGate` in `App.tsx`, needs a server connection) registers Scan food, Log food, Log water and Fasting. Scan/Log food/Fasting navigate; Log water logs one drink of the primary (or only) container with no screen. On Android the list leaves Log water out, because the native background-water shortcut (see the Siri and Shortcuts bullet) logs it without opening the app at all. The library registers on Android by replacing every dynamic shortcut, that one included, so the hook then calls `refreshBackgroundWaterShortcut`, which puts it back labelled in the app language; it is queued with the background-water syncs and clears, so it never brings the shortcut back after a sign-out. The Android icons are adaptive drawables in `targets/android-background-water/res`, copied in by `plugins/withBackgroundWater.ts`. The launch action (`QuickActions.initial`) is read once per JS runtime, which on Android outlives the activity, so the hook remembers the one it ran at module scope; and since Android brings back a killed task with the intent that started it, `plugins/withQuickActionLaunchGuard.ts` strips a shortcut intent from a restored `MainActivity` (saved state present) before expo-quick-actions reads it, so it is not run a second time. A shortcut tapped while the activity is gone but the process lives on (Recents swiped during a GPS recording) still opens the app without running, as `initial` is not read again. On Android the action arrives as an intent to `MainActivity`, which is exported, so any installed app can send one with any id; the hook runs only the ids it registered for the platform, which keeps another app from logging water through the JS `log-water` action (the native shortcut's `LogWaterActivity` is not exported). Strings live under `quickActions.*`.
- Quick Settings tiles (Android): see [dashboard-diary-measurements-and-nutrients.md](agent-docs/dashboard-diary-measurements-and-nutrients.md#quick-settings-tiles-android).
- Siri and Shortcuts actions: see [dashboard-diary-measurements-and-nutrients.md](agent-docs/dashboard-diary-measurements-and-nutrients.md#siri-and-shortcuts-actions).
- iOS water widget (`targets/widget/waterWidget.swift`, kind `waterWidget`; Home Screen small/medium plus the three Lock Screen shapes): `useWidgetSync(summary, water)` writes a `waterSnapshot` (`consumedMl`, `goalMl`, `drinkMl`, `unit`, `canLog` — numbers only, 0 means none/off) from the Dashboard. The `−` and `+` buttons are `Button(intent: RemoveWaterIntent())` (change_drinks -1, not offered in Siri or Shortcuts) and `Button(intent: LogWaterIntent())` (iOS 17+) and appear only when `canLog`, which the Dashboard sets, so a snapshot written without it shows no button; `WaterSnapshotWriter` in `ShortcutIntents.swift` adds the drink to the snapshot and reloads the widget so it updates before the app next syncs.
- Android water widget: see [dashboard-diary-measurements-and-nutrients.md](agent-docs/dashboard-diary-measurements-and-nutrients.md#android-water-widget).
- `DiaryScreen` owns meal type sections, measurement summaries, serving quick-adjust, swipe/long-press deletes, and AddSheet date propagation.
- `DailyNutritionDetailsScreen`'s top macro card (`NutritionMacroCard`, via `onMacroPress`) makes Protein, Carbs and Fat rows open `NutrientTrends` like the breakdown rows below it; the net carbs row stays untappable.
- Dashboard tabs: with `dashboardTabsEnabled` on (the default), `DashboardScreen` shows an accent `SegmentedControl` (Summary, Nutrition, Activity, Wellness) and only the cards of the open tab (`dashboardTab`, remembered on the device). Each card has one home tab in `constants/dashboardTabs.ts` (`homeTabOf`; Ask Sparky has none and is pinned to the top of every tab, its prompt worded per tab). Summary also shows a person-chosen list of cards from the other tabs (`dashboardSummaryCards`, null until edited, resolved by `resolveSummaryCards`, which always keeps At a Glance). Nutrition, Activity and Wellness each keep their own order (`dashboardTabCardOrders`), falling back to the shared `dashboardCardOrder` for any card a tab does not list (`resolveHomeTabCards`); `cardsForTab` and `cardShowsOnTab` put it together. A card's own switch hides it everywhere; taking it off Summary only removes the repeat. A card counts as on screen only while its tab is open (`isOnTab` in `DashboardScreen`), so the caffeine, stand-hour and heart-rate-zone reads and the At a Glance "already shown" check skip cards on other tabs. A new card key needs a home tab in `HOME_TAB` (a total record, so a missing key is a compile error). Each tab gets a fresh `ScrollView` (keyed by tab) rather than a scroll to y = 0, which under the iOS large-title header lands below the top. The tab-only cards live in `components/dashboard/` and share `DashboardCardHeader` (accent glyph, Title Case title, one action) and `StatusBadge` (a quiet, untappable pill): `SleepDashboardCard` (the selected day's night from `useSleepDay`, stages through `buildStageShares`), `ReadinessDashboardCard` (today only, `useFitnessFatigue`; wording shared with the Exercise report through `utils/readinessCopy.ts`), `MealsDashboardCard` (every visible meal type, empty ones included, via `utils/dashboardMeals.ts`), `WorkoutsDashboardCard` (logged sessions, plus what the active plans have due today), `StepsDashboardCard` (the day against the goal and seven days of bars from the steps health trend) and `HeartRateDashboardCard` (resting heart rate against the days before it, and the day's time in zones from `useWorkoutHeartRateZones`, never recomputed). Summary also shows `SummaryStatusLine` under Ask Sparky (today's readiness in words and the scheduled doses still due); it is not a card key, so it is rendered through the `summaryStatus` slot in `DashboardScreen`. Opening a logged session and starting a planned one go through `utils/openExerciseSession.ts` and `hooks/useStartPlanAssignment.ts`, which the Diary uses too.
- `DashboardSettingsScreen` controls the tabs switch, card visibility and custom nutrient display preferences, and is the entry point to `HealthTrendsSettingsScreen`. With tabs on it shows an Ask Sparky row and one section per tab, each a `DashboardCardReorderList` (its own drag state): Summary lists only its own cards, switched on (turning one off removes the repeat; At a Glance's switch is its visibility), with an "Add to Summary" list of the other cards under it; the other tabs' switches hide the card and their drags save that tab's order. With tabs off it is the one shared list, as before.
- `HealthTrendsSettingsScreen` orders and hides the Health Trends graphs. One drag list holds the shown graphs, a `Hidden` divider, then the hidden ones; dragging a graph across the divider is what hides or shows it, so there are no switches. Every row including the divider shares `REORDER_ROW_HEIGHT`, keeping the stride uniform for the reorder worklets it shares with `WorkoutReorderList` and `MealTypeSettingsScreen` (`useReorderRowGeometry`, `useReorderRowPreviewStyle`, `createReorderRowPanGesture`, `resetReorderDragPreview`, all exported from `components/WorkoutReorderList.tsx`). The `vo2max` graph (`Vo2MaxLineChart`, `useVo2MaxRange`) reads the health-synced `vo2_max` custom category through `/api/measurements/custom-measurements-range/:categoryId/:start/:end` (latest reading per day, `vo2MaxRangeQueryKey`, invalidated by `refreshHealthSyncCache`); with no synced reading `useVo2MaxRange` falls back to the run-based estimate (`useRunEstimates`, the optional `vo2Max.series` of one value per run day inside the window, at least `VO2_MIN_RUNS` points; only requested once the synced query came back empty, so a real reading always wins and the two are never mixed) and returns `isEstimate`, which the chart shows as "VO2 max (estimated)" with a note and `≈` tooltips; it hides itself when neither exists, and `useVo2MaxRange` also reads `/api/generic-health/metrics` (`fetchDailyVo2Max`, where Garmin files it; the server carries the last earlier reading onto a day with a row but no reading), the phone's own reading winning on a day that has both. Register a new graph in `constants/healthTrends.ts`; `HealthTrendsPager`'s render map is a total `Record<HealthTrendKey, ...>`, so registering one without rendering it is a compile error.
- Progress photos live in one screen, `ProgressPhotosScreen`: the selected day's three angles with their management on top (`PhotoDaySlots`, date picked through `CalendarSheet`), over a timeline of that angle's recent shoots, each row carrying its weight and the delta against the previous one - a preview rather than the archive, see the History note below. `ProgressPhotoCompareScreen` (two days side by side) and `ProgressPhotoTimelapseScreen` (cross-faded playback) hang off it. Reached from the `AddSheet` row and `ProgressPhotosCard` on the Dashboard, both passing an optional `date`. `ProgressPhotoViewer` is the full-screen pinch-zoom viewer - deliberately not `ImageLightbox`, which resolves URIs through the food image source context and takes plain string URLs.
- `ProgressPhotosCard` is scoped to the Dashboard's selected day, not the latest shoot whenever it was: every other card there answers for the selected date, so a card showing a different day read as today's photo and contradicted the date in the header. It reads the day off the gallery rather than the per-day endpoint because it shows that day's weight, which only the gallery carries. It gates that query on `progressPhotosCardVisible`, so a hidden card costs no request at app open, and prompts into the day when it has no photos rather than hiding itself - nothing else on the Dashboard advertises the feature.
- Adding and removing write immediately rather than staging behind a Save: this screen is somewhere you browse, and unsaved state plus a back-guard does not belong on it. One pick is one request, so uploads stay serial anyway. Replacing needs no delete first because the server upserts on `(user_id, entry_date, photo_type)`; removal confirms first.
- `PhotoDaySlots` shows all three angles at once so a day's gaps read at a glance. Viewing and managing are separate targets on purpose - the photo opens the viewer, a corner button opens replace/remove - because a long-press would hide the only way to replace a photo behind an undiscoverable gesture.
- History is a preview, not the archive: it lists the `HISTORY_PREVIEW_LIMIT` (7) most recent shoots for the angle, with a line under the heading saying so and pointing at Compare / Time-lapse. Counted in shoots rather than calendar days because a weekly shooter would see one row and no delta under a seven-day window. The cut happens **after** the deltas are computed, so the oldest visible row still compares against the shoot before it; and `canCompare` reads the full history, not the preview, or the two tools the line recommends would be disabled for the people sent to them.
- The screen carries **two** angle concepts, which is its one real ambiguity: the day block is angle-agnostic and always shows all three, while the `SegmentedControl` under the History heading scopes the timeline and the Compare / Time-lapse params. Keep them in separate blocks so position tells them apart, and query the control by its `tab` role in tests since the angle names appear twice on screen.
- The comparison loads only the two photos on screen: each pane picks its day through `CalendarSheet` (marked with the days that have that angle) rather than a thumbnail strip, which used to mount one image per shoot. The time-lapse mounts one frame and prefetches `PREFETCH_AHEAD` (3) beyond it, and its header menu windows playback to 30 days / 3 months / all time or a range picked in `DateRangeSheet`. Every window is evaluated as absolute `YYYY-MM-DD` bounds, presets included, so there is one filter rather than a relative and an absolute one. An empty preset window falls back to all time so an old history is not a dead screen; a custom range does not, because widening dates the user picked by hand would misreport what is in them.
- `CheckInPhotosSummary` sits under `MeasurementsSummary` in the diary: the day's angles as small thumbnails, a gap where one is missing, and a tap into `ProgressPhotos` on that same date. Both halves are one check-in, keyed on `(user_id, entry_date)` server-side. It carries no weight or delta because the measurements summary directly above already shows them. A day with no photos gets the same `Tap to add photos` prompt food and exercise get, so the row is a way in and not just a readout.
- `DiaryScreen` owns that day's photo query and passes them down as a prop, the way it already does for measurements. It needs the same answer itself: a photo is something the user recorded, so it defeats `isDayEmpty` exactly as a logged supplement does - the summary lives in the non-empty branch, so a predicate that ignored photos would hide them on the very day they were taken. That arm is gated on the query's load like the sleep arm above it, or a day with photos flashes the empty illustration until they arrive.
- `PhotoDayWeight` renders the weight under a photo and, when the day has none, a prompt into `MeasurementsAdd` for that date. Used by the gallery, comparison and time-lapse; the Dashboard card keeps plain text because the card is already a touchable.
- `CalendarSheet` takes an optional `markedDates`; Dashboard and Diary pass the photo days, fetched on first open of the picker rather than at mount (`useCheckInPhotoDates(calendarOpened)`). It also takes optional `minDate` / `maxDate` day strings that grey out the days outside them (the Compare since rows use them).
- `GoalsScreen` (Settings > Goals) edits today's goals through `POST /api/goals/manage-timeline`, nutrient directions through `/api/nutrient-goal-preferences`, goal presets through `/api/goal-presets` and weekly plans through `/api/weekly-goal-plans`. The same screen edits a preset when opened with `{ presetId }` or `{ newPreset: true }` (name field, no directions, saves to the preset); `WeeklyGoalPlanEditScreen` assigns a preset to each weekday. Preset and plan mutations invalidate the goals, goals range and daily summary caches because they change which goal applies on a day. Meal percentages use `MealDistributionEditor` / `PercentSlider` with the pure helpers in `utils/mealDistribution.ts`.
- Custom nutrients are fetched via `useCustomNutrients` from `GET /api/custom-nutrients`; nutrient display preferences use full-array replace through `preferencesApi.ts`.
- Supplements are medications with `is_supplement` and a per-serving `nutrients` payload (`MedicationNutrients` in `@workspace/shared`), edited in `MedicationFormScreen` through `SupplementNutrientsEditor` and the pure helpers in `utils/supplements.ts`. Nutrients picked from `MICRONUTRIENT_CATALOG` that are not built-in columns get their custom nutrient created on save through `POST /api/custom-nutrients/from-catalog` (`useEnsureCatalogNutrients`), never when picked. A supplement is pinned to one `serving` dose; the Medications list filters All / Meds / Supplements with `filterMedsBySubtype`. On a supplement the form's "Scan barcode to fill in" (shown only while an active `dsld` or `openfoodfacts` provider exists, via `useExternalProviders`) opens `FoodScan` in `capture-barcode` mode, which hands the code back as the `pendingScannedBarcode` / `scannedBarcodeNonce` route params; "Scan label to fill in" photographs the Supplement Facts panel (`prepareLabelPhoto` in `utils/labelPhoto.ts`, shared with the food label scan): `useSupplementLabelScan` tries `scanSupplementLabelOnDevice` first (Apple's on-device model, behind the same "Scan Labels On Device" setting, with lines checked against the recognised text) and otherwise posts the photo to the server's vision AI; the on-device reading is mapped to nutrients by the server. `useSupplementLookup` calls `GET /api/v2/medications/supplement-lookup` and `rowsFromLookup` turns the product into editor rows.
- Nutrient metadata and defaults live in `constants/nutrients.ts`; aggregation and visibility toggling live in `utils/nutrientUtils.ts`.
- Measurements and water routes are in `measurementsApi.ts`; date-sensitive flows should preserve calendar-day strings and shared timezone helpers.

## Chat (Ask Sparky)

- `Chat` is a root-stack route (`src/screens/ChatScreen.tsx`), reached from `AddSheet`'s "Ask Sparky" row and an optional dashboard card gated by the `askSparkyVisible` preference.
- The thread is an assistant-ui runtime: `@assistant-ui/react-native` primitives plus `useChatRuntime` / `AssistantChatTransport` from `@assistant-ui/react-ai-sdk`, streaming from `POST /api/chat/stream` (AI SDK UI message stream protocol).
- The transport must use `expo/fetch` — it exposes a real `ReadableStream` body. RN's global fetch buffers responses and silently breaks incremental streaming.
- Auth and proxy headers are resolved per request through an async `headers` callback; `service_config_id` (the user's active AI provider) is merged into the request body and required by the server.
- `chatApi.ts` is history persistence only: `GET /api/chat/sparky-chat-history` and `POST /api/chat/clear-all-history`. `useChatHistory` seeds the runtime with prior messages and uses `staleTime`/`gcTime` of 0 because the runtime ignores `messages` changes after mount — every chat open must re-seed cold. `mapHistoryToInitialMessages` normalizes stored text and image parts into AI SDK `UIMessage` parts so historical user photos render in the thread without synthetic `[Image message]` placeholders.
- Photo attachments in `ChatScreen.tsx` use `pickChatImageFromCamera` / `pickChatImagesFromLibrary` (`utils/pickImage.ts`, downscaled to `CHAT_MAX_IMAGE_DIMENSION = 1024` and encoded as `0.8` JPEG base64 data URLs, up to `MAX_CHAT_ATTACHMENTS = 5` per turn) and add `CreateAttachment` entries via `aui.composer().addAttachment(...)`. The composer's `+` opens `ActionSheet` in icon-grid mode (camera/library); native pickers launch only after sheet dismissal finishes. The sheet dismisses the keyboard and consumes Android Back; leaving chat cancels a queued picker launch. Pending composer attachments and user message attachments open in a shared `ImageLightbox` (`useImageLightbox`).
- Chat UI lives in `components/chat/`: `MarkdownMessage` (`react-native-enriched-markdown` + `remend` to repair unclosed streamed markdown), `ToolCallCard` (derives running/complete/error from `result`/`isError`), `TypingIndicator`. Tool-name display mapping lives in `constants/chat.ts`.
- Coach check-ins (#2013): `components/CoachCheckinSettingsSection.tsx` on the Coaches screen holds the server settings and the phone-only `coachMessageNotificationsEnabled`; the `coachMessages` Dashboard card (`components/CoachMessagesCard.tsx`, visible by default, renders nothing without messages) lists what coaches left (`hooks/useCoachCheckins.ts`, `services/api/coachCheckinsApi.ts`, wording in `utils/coachMessageText.ts`). There is no server push: `services/coachMessageNotificationService.ts` `announceNewCoachMessages` runs at the end of `performBackgroundSync` and raises a local notification for each unread message under 24 hours old not yet in `announcedCoachMessageIds`; it never asks for permission.
- Coaches (#2013): `components/chat/ChatCoachPicker.tsx` above the chat picks one coach (Sparky, a built-in from shared `BUILTIN_COACHES`, or the person's own). `chatCoachId` in `appPreferencesStore` holds it; `setChatCoachId` also clears `chatConversationId`, so `ChatScreen` starts a new conversation and sends `coachId` in the transport body. Each coach has its own thread: `useChatHistory({ coachId })` keys the history by coach (`chatThreadQueryKey`) and Clear chat calls `clearChatThread` for the thread shown only. `Coaches` (`src/screens/CoachesScreen.tsx`, Settings → Coaches) creates, edits and deletes the person's own (`hooks/useCoaches.ts`, `services/api/coachesApi.ts`); names and tool category labels are in `constants/coaches.ts`.
- `SparkyMemory` (`src/screens/SparkyMemoryScreen.tsx`, Settings → What Sparky remembers) holds the `assistant_memory_enabled` switch and the saved-preferences editor (`hooks/useAssistant.ts`, `services/api/assistantApi.ts`). The `sparkySummary` Dashboard card (`components/SparkySummaryCard.tsx`, `sparkySummaryCardVisible`, off by default) shows Sparky's daily summary for today only; each new summary costs the person an AI request, so it loads once and refreshes only from its button (#2013).
- `ChatSettings` (`src/screens/ChatSettingsScreen.tsx`) is a root-stack settings route reached from Settings → Voice settings. `voice_input_enabled` and `active_voice_ai_service_id` are server-backed preferences shared with web, not local Zustand settings. Disabling voice hides the microphone without erasing the model. Null selects System; compatible Chat models are suggestions and AI always requires explicit selection. `useVoicePreferencesRefresh` refreshes choices on screen focus and foreground return.
- Voice dictation defaults to platform recognition in `ChatScreen.tsx`. `active_voice_ai_service_id` selects optional server transcription via `components/chat/AiVoiceDictationButton.tsx` and `services/api/voiceTranscriptionApi.ts`. `useVoiceInputLifecycle` cancels work on blur/background/unmount; temporary recordings are deleted, and the composer stays locked until processing finishes. `VoiceWaveform` uses a shared microphone level (no per-sample chat re-renders) and respects Reduce Motion. Sending always remains manual.
- There is no chat Zustand store; thread state lives in the assistant-ui runtime and history seeding in React Query.

## Auth, Networking, And Settings

- Server configs support `apiKey` and `session` auth. URLs/IDs are in AsyncStorage; API keys, session tokens, and proxy headers are in SecureStore.
- `OnboardingScreen` handles first-run setup, session sign-in, API keys, MFA, theme, external food source defaults, and finish-without-connection.
- `ServerSettingsScreen` handles server list management, active server switching, connection tests, web dashboard launch, and `ServerConfigModal`.
- `useAuth`, `ReauthModal`, `ServerConfigModal`, `authService.ts`, and `MfaForm` coordinate auth recovery, MFA, session expiry, and API-key fallback.
- Production rejects HTTP server URLs. Preserve HTTPS guards in onboarding, settings, raw fetch paths, and health sync.
- Proxy headers support reverse-proxy auth. They must be injected before auth headers in `apiClient.ts` and raw fetch clients.
- During login before a config is saved, `authService` manages pending proxy headers via `setPendingProxyHeaders()` / `clearPendingProxyHeaders()`.
- Prefer `getApiErrorMessage` / API error helpers over ad hoc error parsing in UI.
- Session expiry keeps the offline queue. `notifyIdentityChanged` (every re-login, server switch and edit) clears the query cache and the persisted read cache but not the queue; each item carries `configId` and `accountId`, and the next drain resolves `GET /api/identity/user` and parks items stamped for another account under needs-attention (`account_mismatch`) instead of sending them. Deleting a server config purges its queued items.
- Screens that need the server wrap their content area (below the header) in `ServerGate` (`components/ServerGate.tsx`) instead of their own "No server configured" block. `useServerConnection().status` is `loading`, `notConfigured`, `unreachable` or `connected`; the gate shows a spinner, "No server configured", or "Can't reach your server" with Try again, and owns the copy. Use `ServerStatusView` directly only where the screen needs its own layout around the message (Dashboard, Diary, Library hide their date header while offline). The connection check runs on first load and on screen focus, and the shared query client (`hooks/queryClient.ts`) re-runs it, at most every 10 s, when a query fails with a network error, timeout, 5xx or 429, so Refresh and Retry also reach the offline state.

## Logging And Diagnostics

- `LogService.ts` is the single source of truth for app logs. Prefer `addLog(message, status?, details?)` over `console.*`.
- Valid log statuses are `DEBUG`, `INFO`, `WARNING`, and `ERROR`. Legacy `SUCCESS` is migrated to `INFO` on read.
- Capture and view filtering are separate thresholds; do not conflate storage filtering with `LogScreen` filtering.
- Use structured `details` arrays for diagnostic context instead of cramming multiline strings into `message`.
- `diagnosticReportService.ts` and `healthDiagnosticService.ts` power diagnostic exports. Android-only raw Health Connect diagnostics belong in `healthDiagnosticService.ts`.

## Styling And UI

- Styling uses Uniwind with TailwindCSS v4 tokens in `global.css`.
- `DESIGN.md` describes the current visual language (theme tokens with hex values per theme, typography roles, component styling, layout rules) in the five-section format AI UI design tools read, plus how to translate HTML/Tailwind mockups into this codebase, and (section 10) the Apple Watch, watch complication and Wear OS design. Read it before visual or redesign work, and update its color tables in the same change whenever a token in `global.css` is added, renamed, or retuned.
- Themes are Light, Dark, AMOLED, and System. `themeService.ts` owns persistence; `App.tsx` syncs Android navigation bar style.
- Many visual components read CSS variables with `useCSSVariable`, especially Skia charts and themed controls.
- Animate Skia paths from Reanimated `useSharedValue` / `useDerivedValue`, not Skia's deprecated animation API.
- `Icon.tsx` maps semantic names to SF Symbols on iOS and Ionicons on Android; verify identifiers before adding icons.
- Use shared primitives where they fit: `FormInput`, `Button`, `SettingsRow`, `SettingsRowGroup`, `SegmentedControl`, `StepperInput`, `BottomSheetPicker`, `CalendarSheet`, `DateRangeSheet`, `AnchoredMenu`, `FooterActionBar`, `FullScreenModalBody`, and `FormScreenChrome`.
- An action pinned below a list goes in `FooterActionBar`, never a hand-rolled `View` with flat bottom padding. A list's own `contentContainerStyle` inset does not reach a sibling pinned under it, so a flat padding leaves the action beneath Android's navigation bar; the component applies `Math.max(insets.bottom, 16)` in one place so the next screen cannot rediscover that. `FormScreenChrome`'s `FooterSaveBar` stays separate — it is the save-specific variant, with a top divider and the duplicate-press guard. A bar that only makes sense with a keyboard is the opposite case: it is never visible without one. Park it behind the keyboard with `KeyboardStickyView` `offset={{ closed: <its own height>, opened: 0 }}` (the sticky view translates by keyboard height plus that offset over keyboard progress, so at rest it sits just below the screen edge, off Android's navigation buttons and the docked rest bar, and rises from behind the keyboard's top edge), and never animate a layout property (height, padding, margins) from keyboard progress, which re-lays the screen out on every keyboard frame. The set surfaces' Done / Next / Log bar is `StickySetInputAccessoryBar` (`components/SetRowChrome.tsx`): screens render it unconditionally with `visible` (a set cell is focused) and an optional `restSlot` (the live screen's `ActiveWorkoutCompactRestRow` while a rest runs, left out below `MIN_WINDOW_HEIGHT_FOR_COMPACT_REST_ROW` (740 dp) of window height, where it would cover the set being edited); `useSetEditAccessoryBar` returns the same bar for the edit forms, and their `KeyboardAwareScrollView` `bottomOffset` adds `SET_ACCESSORY_BAR_HEIGHT` (plus `COMPACT_REST_ROW_HEIGHT` on the live screen while the bar carries the rest row).
- Never import `SafeAreaView` from `react-native`: it only applies insets on iOS and is a plain `View` on Android, where the app runs edge-to-edge, so a full-screen `Modal` built on it draws its header under the status bar and its actions under the navigation bar. Full-screen modal content goes in `FullScreenModalBody` (status-bar inset plus 16 on top, `Math.max(insets.bottom, 16)` below); anything else reads `useSafeAreaInsets()` from `react-native-safe-area-context`.
- `DateRangeSheet` takes optional `title` and `confirmLabel`; they default to the writeback removal wording, so a consumer that is not removing anything (the time-lapse) must pass its own. It also takes `markedDates`, like `CalendarSheet`: both dot their days through the shared `useMarkedDayComponent` (`components/calendarMarkedDays.tsx`), which inverts the dot on a selected day and on either end of a range, and supplies no `Day` override at all when there is nothing to mark so every other caller keeps the library's own cell.
- `BottomSheetPicker`, `CalendarSheet`, and sheets shown over native modals use `FullWindowOverlay` on iOS to avoid nested-provider inset bugs.
- `BottomSheetModal` does not handle Android's hardware Back, so the press falls through to navigation with the sheet still up: it pops the screen under the sheet, switches another tab to the Dashboard, or exits the app from the Dashboard. Only `ActionSheet` and `AddSheet` take it today, through `useSheetHardwareBack(isOpen, onBack)` from `components/ui/sheetChrome.tsx`, with `isOpen` set when the sheet opens and cleared in `onDismiss`; the other sheets (`BottomSheetPicker`, `CalendarSheet`, `DateRangeSheet`, `TimeSheet`, `CopyMealSheet`, the fasting sheets and the rest) still let Back fall through. Set `isOpen` from both `onAnimate` and `onChange`: when the open finishes before `onAnimate` reaches JS (Android's "Remove animations", or a busy JS thread), the library drops `onAnimate`, and `onChange` is the only signal that arrives.
- Keep button text and compact cards within their stable dimensions across mobile sizes. Avoid layout shifts from dynamic labels, loading states, or icon swaps.

## Widgets And Native Config

- iOS launches through the UIScene life cycle, which the iOS 27 SDK (Xcode 27) requires — a build that still creates its window in `AppDelegate` is killed at launch in `_UIApplicationEvaluateRuntimeIssueForNoSceneLifecycleAdoption`. The SDK 57 prebuild template predates this, so `plugins/withSceneLifecycle.ts` applies the SDK 58 template's changes: `AppDelegate` conforms to `ExpoReactNativeFactoryProvider` and stops starting React Native, a `SceneDelegate.swift` subclassing expo's `ExpoAppSceneDelegate` (needs `expo` >= 57.0.25) is added to the app target, and `Info.plist` gets `UIApplicationSceneManifest`. The plugin throws if `AppDelegate.swift` no longer matches the template; delete it when moving to SDK 58, whose template already does all three.
- iOS widgets live under `targets/widget/`, share data through the app group from `app.identifiers.js`, and reload through `ExtensionStorage` in `useWidgetSync`.
- Current iOS widgets are calorie, macro and water widgets, plus the generic `customWidget`, whose numbers the person picks in Edit Widget (iOS only); Android has the same three fixed widgets. Custom widgets: see [widgets-and-native-config.md](agent-docs/widgets-and-native-config.md#custom-widget-ios-edit-widget-pickers). When changing display, update Swift views, shared helpers, TS snapshot shape, and reload kind handling together.
- Widget string keys are derived from the Swift sources, not tracked by hand: `__tests__/config/helpers/widgetSwiftKeys.ts` discovers every `.swift` file under `targets/widget/` (recursively) and extracts the literal keys passed to `localizedWidgetString`, `configurationDisplayName` and `.description`. A new key must therefore be added to `targets/widget/en.lproj/Localizable.strings`, and — for `localizedWidgetString` keys — to the `fallbackWidgetString` map, or the contract tests fail. Target-language files stay optional and fall back to EN.
- Android widgets live under `targets/android-widget/`. `plugins/withCalorieWidget.ts` copies Kotlin/templates/resources, registers receivers, wires the native module package, and documents the pattern for adding another widget.
- Resizable Android widgets: see [widgets-and-native-config.md](agent-docs/widgets-and-native-config.md#resizable-android-widgets).
- `src/services/CalorieWidgetBridge.ts` is the JS bridge for Android widget snapshot writes, Glance reloads and the clear on an account change.
- The scheduled "Rest complete" alert fires exactly only with the `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` special access ("Alarms & reminders", user-granted, denied by default on Android 13+) — without it expo-notifications falls back to inexact alarms the OS batches ~15s late. The `targets/android-exact-alarm/` Kotlin module (registered by `plugins/withExactAlarmModule.ts`) exposes `canScheduleExactAlarms`/`openExactAlarmSettings` through `src/services/ExactAlarmBridge.ts`; `maybePromptForExactAlarmPermission` in `notifications.ts` owns the one-time grant prompt at workout start.
- Widget snapshot shapes: the calorie snapshot (`food`, `burned`, `goal`, `remaining`, `progress`, all from `calorieBalance`) is built once by `buildCalorieWidgetSnapshot` (`src/services/calorieWidgetSnapshot.ts`) for iOS and Android; the other iOS snapshots are built in `useWidgetSync.ts`, and the Android macro and water contracts live in `src/services/androidWidgetSyncService.ts`. Keep them aligned with the Swift views and Kotlin `parseSnapshot`/`WaterWidgetStore`.
- Workout Live Activity: see [widgets-and-native-config.md](agent-docs/widgets-and-native-config.md#workout-live-activity).
- Medication reminder buttons on iOS (Taken / Skip / Snooze with the app closed): see [widgets-and-native-config.md](agent-docs/widgets-and-native-config.md#medication-reminder-buttons-on-ios).
- The run Live Activity (`RunLiveActivity`, a second type beside the workout one) shows a phone GPS recording or a watch run (`watchRunLiveStore`) on the Lock Screen and Dynamic Island. Same rules as the workout one: `src/services/RunLiveActivityLayout.tsx` is the self-contained `'widget'` layout, imported only from `runLiveActivity.ios.ts` (`runLiveActivity.ts` is the Android no-op). The phone recording wins over a watch run; a watch run that has been quiet for `WATCH_RUN_LIVE_MAX_AGE_MS` ends the activity. The clock is OS-ticked from `now - activeSeconds*1000`; the app pushes at most every 5 s (`MIN_REPAINT_MS`) with a trailing repaint, never polling. Buttons (`run-toggle`, `run-lap`) call the recording service or `sendRunControl`; the activity opens `sparkyfitnessmobile://record-activity` (linking entry in `App.tsx`). Labels are translated by the caller via `configureRunLiveActivity({ t, distanceUnit })` from `WatchWorkoutGate` in `App.tsx`; the layout never calls `t`.
- Android uses `src/services/workoutLiveActivity.android.ts` to mirror the active workout as an ongoing notification. `plugins/withWorkoutNotification.ts` copies the Kotlin module from `targets/android-workout-notification/` at prebuild. On Android 16+, the system ProgressStyle shows exercise segments and requests a Live Update status chip with the next set position (for example, `3/16`); older Android versions use a standard ongoing notification with overall set progress. Native notifications are reconciled after workout-store hydration; rest-complete alerts remain separate.
- `app.config.ts` controls bundle identifiers, Apple team IDs, iOS app group, Android permissions, navigation bar contrast, widget plugins, and production-only network security config.
- iOS entitlements in `app.config.ts` are the app group plus `com.apple.developer.usernotifications.time-sensitive`. The latter is what lets the `timeSensitive` rest-complete alert break through Focus modes and the Scheduled Summary; it is an App ID capability, so credentials have to be re-synced (profile regenerated) on the first build that carries it.
- `APP_VARIANT` selects dev vs production behavior; dev builds request extra Android Health Connect write permissions for local testing/seeding.
- After editing `targets/`, native config plugins, app groups, permissions, or native bridge shape, run `npx expo prebuild --clean`.

## Wear OS

- The Wear OS companion lives under `targets/wearos/` (Kotlin + Compose for Wear, `build.gradle.tmpl`), copied into the generated Android project and embedded by `plugins/withWearOs.ts` (registered in `app.config.ts`). Android-only; `targets/wearos/src/test/.../WearOsParityTest.kt` checks it against the Apple Watch behavior.
- It mirrors the Apple Watch pages: `WatchPage` in `Models.kt` (goals, water, medications, entry, trend, workout, nowPlaying, fasting, steps; same wire keys as `constants/watchPages.ts`), with `Screens.kt` / `WorkoutScreens.kt` for the UI, `WatchStores.kt` (`CheckInStore`, `WorkoutSessionStore`, `RecordingStore`, `WatchRunStore`) for state, `PayloadAdapters.kt` for the wire payloads, `Complications.kt` for the Calories, Protein, Carbs, Fat, Water, Fasting and Steps complications, and `Tiles.kt` (`NutritionTileService`, `SleepTileService`, `MedicationsTileService`, `ExerciseTileService`, `TileModels`, `TileUpdater`, `PackageReplacedTileReceiver`) for the four ProtoLayout Wear OS tiles (Nutrition, Sleep Health, Medications and Exercise; tapping the Sleep tile opens `SleepDetailPage` via `target_page = "sleep"`). `useWatchCheckInBridge` also sends day-scoped `sleep` and `exerciseSummary` snapshots in the context so the Sleep Health and Exercise tiles work with the phone away.
- Transport: `modules/watch-connectivity/android` (the Android half of the same Expo module, with `SparkyWearListenerService`) talks to `WearSessionManager` / `WearDataListenerService` on the watch through the Wearable Data Layer. `WatchConnectivity` resolves to `null` wherever the native module is absent (it loads on iOS and Android), so guard call sites. Bridge credentials are stored encrypted (`services/watchServerCredentials.ts`) and watch actions are bound to the account owner.
- Settings → Wear OS is `WatchSettingsScreen` with Android wording (RPE 5-10, no double-tap or Digital Crown set input). Changes to a message `type` or page key must be made on the iOS, Android and Wear OS sides together.
- Not on Wear OS: following or storing saved routes (Apple Watch only).
- Release: the watch app is a separate build, never delivered inside the phone app. `.github/workflows/android.yml` attaches `wearos-release.apk` to each GitHub release, signed with the same keystore as the phone APK (prebuild's generated `android/app/build.gradle` signs release with the debug key unless `withWearOs.ts` wires in `SPARKY_RELEASE_*`, so GitHub APKs up to v1.10.0 used the public debug key); `eas build -p android --profile wearos` builds the AAB for Play's Wear OS track, and `build.gradle.tmpl` reads EAS's `credentials.json` because EAS only signs `android/app`. A release build's version code is minutes since the epoch (`SPARKY_WEAR_VERSION_CODE` overrides it), because Play wants it unique across form factors. See `MobileApp-Dev-commands.md`.

## Apple Watch

- The watchOS companion app lives entirely under `targets/watch/` (a `@bacons/apple-targets` `type: 'watch'` extension, `expo-target.config.js`), iOS-only (Wear OS has its own app, see the Wear OS section below). Layers: `Presentation/` (SwiftUI views, `ContentView`'s `TabView` — one tab per `WatchPage` the phone's layout shows, in its order), `Application/` (`CheckInStore`, `WorkoutSessionStore` — `@MainActor ObservableObject` singletons, injected in `SparkyFitnessWatchApp.swift`), `Infrastructure/` (`WatchSessionManager` wrapping `WCSession`; `WorkoutHealthKitController` wrapping `HKWorkoutSession`/`HKLiveWorkoutBuilder`), `Adapters/` (`OutboundPayloads` builds outgoing `[String: Any]` dictionaries, `ContextPayloadMapper` parses incoming ones — the only two files that know wire key names), `Domain/` (plain `Codable` models).
- Look and touch targets: see [apple-watch.md](agent-docs/apple-watch.md#look-and-touch-targets).
- Watch pages and their order: see [apple-watch.md](agent-docs/apple-watch.md#watch-pages-and-their-order).
- Watch Fasting and Steps pages, and complications (`targets/watch-widget`): the phone adds `fast` (`{startedAt, targetEndAt, label}` in epoch ms, or null for none; omitted until the server has answered) from `useCurrentFast`/`toWatchFast`, plus `steps` (`{day, count}` from today's check-in measurements row) and `stepGoal` (fixed 10,000 until a per-user goal exists) to the watch context. `FastingView` ticks locally and can start a 16:8, 18:6 or 20:4 fast or end the running one: the watch sends `fastStart` (`presetId`, `clientId`) / `fastEnd` (`clientId`), `modules/watch-connectivity` emits `onFastStartRequested`/`onFastEndRequested`, and `useWatchFastingBridge` calls `startFast`/`endFast` (a start is skipped when a fast is already running; each request is handled once per `clientId`) and refreshes the fasting queries so the next context push carries the new `fast` back. `StepsView` shows the count against the goal. Complications: Protein, Carbs and Fat (`MacroComplications.swift`, reading the existing `energyGoalSnapshot`), Fasting (`FastingComplication.swift`, `fastingSnapshot` written by `ComplicationPublisher.publish(fast:)` when `fastSynced`) and Steps (`StepsComplication.swift`, `stepsSnapshot`), each in circular, corner, rectangular and inline sizes; kinds `proteinGoalComplication`, `carbsGoalComplication`, `fatGoalComplication`, `fastingComplication`, `stepsComplication`. Taps open Goals, Fasting or Steps via `ComplicationLink`/`WatchDeepLink`. Pages are registered in `constants/watchPages.ts` and `WatchPage.swift`.
- Phone side: `modules/watch-connectivity/` is the Expo native module bridge (`index.ts` the JS surface, `ios/WatchConnectivityModule.swift` the native implementation; resolves to `null` on Android, guard every call site). `src/hooks/useWatchCheckInBridge.ts` (weight/water/nutrition context) and `src/hooks/useWatchWorkoutBridge.ts` (workout set completions + heart rate) are the only two consumers; both are mounted headlessly in `App.tsx` (`WatchCheckInGate`/`WatchWorkoutGate`), gated on an active server connection.
- Watch weight check-in and trend: the check-in crown is inert until the wearer taps the number (`CheckInEntryView.crownArmed`; it disarms on a field change), so a stray turn cannot edit a weight that then gets saved. The trend page (`TrendView`) draws the 14 days up to today but the phone relays `HISTORY_DAYS` = 28 so the line is warmed up. `CheckInStore.weightTrend` and `weeklyRateKg` are Swift ports of `computeWeightTrend` / `weeklyRateOf` in `shared/src/utils/weightTrend.ts` (interpolate to calendar days, trailing 7-day mean; rate needs 4 weigh-ins over 2 weeks): keep them in step so the watch, the phone chart and the server's adaptive TDEE agree. The target weight rides the context as `goalWeightKg` (kg, from `profile.target_weight`; **0 = none**, because a null is stripped in transit and a cleared goal would otherwise stick), carried forward when absent, and drawn as a dashed line only when within `goalReachKg` of the plotted weights.
- Watch Fasting and Steps pages, and complications (`targets/watch-widget`): the phone adds `fast` (`{startedAt, targetEndAt, label}` in epoch ms, or null for none; omitted until the server has answered) from `useCurrentFast`/`toWatchFast`, plus `steps` (`{day, count}` from today's check-in measurements row) and `stepGoal` (fixed 10,000 until a per-user goal exists) to the watch context. `FastingView` ticks locally and can start a 16:8, 18:6 or 20:4 fast or end the running one: the watch sends `fastStart` (`presetId`, `clientId`) / `fastEnd` (`clientId`), `modules/watch-connectivity` emits `onFastStartRequested`/`onFastEndRequested`, and `useWatchFastingBridge` calls `startFast`/`endFast` (a start is skipped when a fast is already running; each request is handled once per `clientId`) and refreshes the fasting queries so the next context push carries the new `fast` back. `StepsView` shows the count against the goal. Complications: Calories (`EnergyGoalComplication.swift`, the ring in circular and a flame glyph with a curved calorie-percent gauge in corner), Protein, Carbs and Fat (`MacroComplications.swift`, reading the existing `energyGoalSnapshot`), Fasting (`FastingComplication.swift`, `fastingSnapshot` written by `ComplicationPublisher.publish(fast:)` when `fastSynced`) and Steps (`StepsComplication.swift`, `stepsSnapshot`), each in circular, corner, rectangular and inline sizes; kinds `proteinGoalComplication`, `carbsGoalComplication`, `fatGoalComplication`, `fastingComplication`, `stepsComplication`. Taps open Goals, Fasting or Steps via `ComplicationLink`/`WatchDeepLink`. Pages are registered in `constants/watchPages.ts` and `WatchPage.swift`.

Definitions are device-local, kept in the `watchComplications` app preference (`null` until the wearer has made or imported any; the starter set from `starterComplications` is sent meanwhile) and defined by `src/utils/watchComplications.ts` (sources, format, gauge, colour, icon; import/export JSON; wire payload). `useWatchComplicationSync` (mounted in `WatchCheckInGate`) sends them as the queued `complicationConfig` message (`WatchConnectivity.sendComplicationConfig`, iOS only, a still-queued older copy is cancelled), and the watch saves them under the app-group key `customComplicationDefinitions` through `ComplicationPublisher.publish(customDefinitions:revision:)`, keeping the newest revision. What a definition shows comes from `customMetricsSnapshot`: `CustomComplicationMetrics.build(from:)` turns the watch context into `CustomMetric`s in the wearer's units (calories, calories left, calories burned, macros, any Goals-list nutrient as `nutrient:<key>`, water, steps, fasting, weight, body fat), each stamped with its day so the widget hides yesterday's, and `publish(customMetrics:)` writes and reloads on every change. Renaming a source, format, colour or icon string means the phone util, `CustomComplicationMetrics` and `CustomComplication.swift` together. Definitions are device-local, kept in the `watchComplications` app preference (`null` until the wearer has made or imported any; the starter set from `starterComplications` is sent meanwhile) and defined by `src/utils/watchComplications.ts` (sources, format, gauge, colour, icon; import/export JSON; wire payload). `useWatchComplicationSync` (mounted in `WatchCheckInGate`) sends them as the queued `complicationConfig` message (`WatchConnectivity.sendComplicationConfig`, iOS only, a still-queued older copy is cancelled), and the watch saves them under the app-group key `customComplicationDefinitions` through `ComplicationPublisher.publish(customDefinitions:revision:)`, keeping the newest revision. What a definition shows comes from `customMetricsSnapshot`: `CustomComplicationMetrics.build(from:)` turns the watch context into `CustomMetric`s in the wearer's units (calories, calories left, calories burned, macros, any Goals-list nutrient as `nutrient:<key>`, water, steps, fasting, weight, body fat), each stamped with its day so the widget hides yesterday's, and `publish(customMetrics:)` writes and reloads on every change. Renaming a source, format, colour or icon string means the phone util, `CustomComplicationMetrics` and `CustomComplication.swift` together. The wearer makes and manages them in Settings → Apple Watch → Complications: `WatchComplicationsScreen` lists them (tap to edit, move up or down, delete, add; its header menu exports the list as a JSON file, imports one to add to the list or replace it, and resets to the starter set) and `WatchComplicationEditScreen` edits one (name, source, format, gauge, colour, icon) beside a `ComplicationPreview` on a sample day; both read the list through `useWatchComplicationList`, and saving writes the whole list, so editing a starter makes the list the wearer's own. Import and export go through `services/watchComplicationFile.ts` and the helpers in `src/utils/watchComplications.ts`; an imported entry always gets a new id. Nutrient sources offered are the ones shown on the watch's Goals page, because those are the ones the watch publishes. Rectangular and inline sizes and Wear OS are not done yet)), Protein, Carbs and Fat (`MacroComplications.swift`, reading the existing `energyGoalSnapshot`), Fasting (`FastingComplication.swift`, `fastingSnapshot` written by `ComplicationPublisher.publish(fast:)` when `fastSynced`) and Steps (`StepsComplication.swift`, `stepsSnapshot`), each in circular, corner, rectangular and inline sizes; kinds `proteinGoalComplication`, `carbsGoalComplication`, `fatGoalComplication`, `fastingComplication`, `stepsComplication`.
