Complications: Daily Energy Goal (`EnergyGoalComplication.swift`, the calorie and macro rings in circular, and in corner with an Apple-Activity-style curved row of coloured percents: calories, fat, carbs, protein), Calorie Goal (`CalorieGoalComplication.swift`, a calories-only gauge with the percent in circular and a flame glyph with a curved percent gauge in corner, reading the same `energyGoalSnapshot`; kind `calorieGoalComplication`, reloaded by `ComplicationPublisher.publish(goals:)`), Custom Complication (`CustomComplication.swift`, the wearer's own: one `AppIntentConfiguration` widget, kind `customComplication`, whose picker lists their definitions, so any number can exist and each watch-face slot picks one, in circular, corner, rectangular (name, value, then a gauge bar or an amount line such as "of 1,900 kcal") and inline (value first, so a long name is the part cut) sizes. Definitions are device-local, kept in the `watchComplications` app preference (`null` until the wearer has made or imported any; the starter set from `starterComplications` is sent meanwhile) and defined by `src/utils/watchComplications.ts` (sources, format, gauge, colour, icon; import/export JSON; wire payload). `useWatchComplicationSync` (mounted in `WatchCheckInGate`) sends them as the queued `complicationConfig` message (`WatchConnectivity.sendComplicationConfig`, iOS only, a still-queued older copy is cancelled), and the watch saves them under the app-group key `customComplicationDefinitions` through `ComplicationPublisher.publish(customDefinitions:revision:)`, keeping the newest revision. What a definition shows comes from `customMetricsSnapshot`: `CustomComplicationMetrics.build(from:)` turns the watch context into `CustomMetric`s in the wearer's units (calories, calories left, calories burned, macros, any Goals-list nutrient as `nutrient:<key>`, water, steps, fasting, weight, body fat), each stamped with its day so the widget hides yesterday's, and `publish(customMetrics:)` writes and reloads on every change. Renaming a source, format, colour or icon string means the phone util, `CustomComplicationMetrics` and `CustomComplication.swift` together. The wearer makes and manages them in Settings → Apple Watch → Complications: `WatchComplicationsScreen` lists them (tap to edit, move up or down, delete, add; its header menu exports the list as a JSON file, imports one to add to the list or replace it, and resets to the starter set) and `WatchComplicationEditScreen` edits one (name, source, format, gauge, colour, icon) beside a `ComplicationPreview` of all four sizes on a sample day (`complicationPreview` mirrors the widget's `display`); both read the list through `useWatchComplicationList`, and saving writes the whole list, so editing a starter makes the list the wearer's own. Import and export go through `services/watchComplicationFile.ts` and the helpers in `src/utils/watchComplications.ts`; an imported entry always gets a new id. Nutrient sources offered are the ones shown on the watch's Goals page, because those are the ones the watch publishes. Wear OS is not done yet)), Protein, Carbs and Fat (`MacroComplications.swift`, reading the existing `energyGoalSnapshot`), Fasting (`FastingComplication.swift`, `fastingSnapshot` written by `ComplicationPublisher.publish(fast:)` when `fastSynced`) and Steps (`StepsComplication.swift`, `stepsSnapshot`), each in circular, corner, rectangular and inline sizes; kinds `proteinGoalComplication`, `carbsGoalComplication`, `fatGoalComplication`, `fastingComplication`, `stepsComplication`. Taps open Goals, Fasting or Steps via `ComplicationLink`/`WatchDeepLink`. Pages are registered in `constants/watchPages.ts` and `WatchPage.swift`.
- Watch Fasting and Steps pages, and complications (`targets/watch-widget`): the phone adds `fast` (`{startedAt, targetEndAt, label}` in epoch ms, or null for none; omitted until the server has answered) from `useCurrentFast`/`toWatchFast`, plus `steps` (`{day, count}` from today's check-in measurements row) and `stepGoal` (fixed 10,000 until a per-user goal exists) to the watch context. `FastingView` ticks locally and can start a 16:8, 18:6 or 20:4 fast or end the running one: the watch sends `fastStart` (`presetId`, `clientId`) / `fastEnd` (`clientId`), `modules/watch-connectivity` emits `onFastStartRequested`/`onFastEndRequested`, and `useWatchFastingBridge` calls `startFast`/`endFast` (a start is skipped when a fast is already running; each request is handled once per `clientId`) and refreshes the fasting queries so the next context push carries the new `fast` back. `StepsView` shows the count against the goal. Complications: Daily Energy Goal (`EnergyGoalComplication.swift`, the calorie and macro rings in circular, and in corner with an Apple-Activity-style curved row of coloured percents: calories, fat, carbs, protein), Calorie Goal (`CalorieGoalComplication.swift`, a calories-only gauge with the percent in circular and a flame glyph with a curved percent gauge in corner, reading the same `energyGoalSnapshot`; kind `calorieGoalComplication`, reloaded by `ComplicationPublisher.publish(goals:)`), Custom Complication (`CustomComplication.swift`, the wearer's own: one `AppIntentConfiguration` widget, kind `customComplication`, whose picker lists their definitions, so any number can exist and each watch-face slot picks one, in circular, corner, rectangular (name, value, then a gauge bar or an amount line such as "of 1,900 kcal") and inline (value first, so a long name is the part cut) sizes. Definitions are device-local, kept in the `watchComplications` app preference (`null` until the wearer has made or imported any; the starter set from `starterComplications` is sent meanwhile) and defined by `src/utils/watchComplications.ts` (sources, format, gauge, colour, icon; import/export JSON; wire payload). `useWatchComplicationSync` (mounted in `WatchCheckInGate`) sends them as the queued `complicationConfig` message (`WatchConnectivity.sendComplicationConfig`, iOS only, a still-queued older copy is cancelled), and the watch saves them under the app-group key `customComplicationDefinitions` through `ComplicationPublisher.publish(customDefinitions:revision:)`, keeping the newest revision. The module holds the list until the `WCSession` is activated (a send at app launch is otherwise dropped), queues it and also sends it live when the watch is reachable, and until a list arrives the picker offers the starter set (`starterDefinitions` in `CustomComplication.swift`, mirroring the phone's `starterComplications` with the same ids). What a definition shows comes from `customMetricsSnapshot`: `CustomComplicationMetrics.build(from:)` turns the watch context into `CustomMetric`s in the wearer's units (calories, calories left, calories burned, macros, any Goals-list nutrient as `nutrient:<key>`, water, steps, fasting, weight, body fat), each stamped with its day so the widget hides yesterday's, and `publish(customMetrics:)` writes and reloads on every change. Renaming a source, format, colour or icon string means the phone util, `CustomComplicationMetrics` and `CustomComplication.swift` together. The wearer makes and manages them in Settings → Apple Watch → Complications: `WatchComplicationsScreen` lists them (tap to edit, move up or down, delete, add; its header menu exports the list as a JSON file, imports one to add to the list or replace it, and resets to the starter set) and `WatchComplicationEditScreen` edits one (name, source, format, gauge, colour, icon) beside a `ComplicationPreview` of all four sizes on a sample day (`complicationPreview` mirrors the widget's `display`); both read the list through `useWatchComplicationList`, and saving writes the whole list, so editing a starter makes the list the wearer's own. Import and export go through `services/watchComplicationFile.ts` and the helpers in `src/utils/watchComplications.ts`; an imported entry always gets a new id. Wear OS is not done yet)), Protein, Carbs and Fat (`MacroComplications.swift`, reading the existing `energyGoalSnapshot`), Fasting (`FastingComplication.swift`, `fastingSnapshot` written by `ComplicationPublisher.publish(fast:)` when `fastSynced`) and Steps (`StepsComplication.swift`, `stepsSnapshot`), each in circular, corner, rectangular and inline sizes; kinds `proteinGoalComplication`, `carbsGoalComplication`, `fatGoalComplication`, `fastingComplication`, `stepsComplication`. Taps open Goals, Fasting or Steps via `ComplicationLink`/`WatchDeepLink`. Pages are registered in `constants/watchPages.ts` and `WatchPage.swift`.
- Phone side: `modules/watch-connectivity/` is the Expo native module bridge (`index.ts` the JS surface, `ios/WatchConnectivityModule.swift` the native implementation; resolves to `null` when the native module is absent, guard every call site). `src/hooks/useWatchCheckInBridge.ts` (weight/water/nutrition context) and `src/hooks/useWatchWorkoutBridge.ts` (workout set completions + heart rate) are the primary consumers; `useWatchFastingBridge`, `useWatchRecordingBridge`, `useWatchRunBridge` and `useWatchRouteBridge` consume it too, and all are mounted headlessly in `App.tsx` (the check-in and workout ones as `WatchCheckInGate`/`WatchWorkoutGate`), gated on an active server connection or watch support. When changing the bridge, check every one of them.
- Watch Water page food water: `useWatchCheckInBridge` adds `waterFromFoodMl` to the context (`watchWaterFromFoodMl` in `utils/watchWater.ts`): the daily summary's `waterFromFood` when the `add_food_water_to_intake` preference is on and the amount is positive, otherwise null (the key is dropped before the transfer, so the watch does not show an "Includes 0" line the phone hides). The watch reads it into `WaterSnapshot.fromFoodMl` and `WaterIntakeView` shows an "Includes … from food" line under the total only when it is present and positive. The widget and complications do not show it.
- Two transports, chosen per message by durability need: `updateApplicationContext` (`WatchConnectivityModule.updateContext`) is a single latest-value slot for the WHOLE app — nutrition/water/weight context only, never reused for anything else, or a second push would silently clobber it. Everything else (check-ins, water taps, `startWorkout`, `setTargets`, `setCompleted`, `restChanged`, `heartRateBatch`, `workoutStop`) goes through `sendMessage`-when-reachable-falling-back-to-`transferUserInfo` (`WatchSessionManager.transfer(_:)` on the watch, mirrored ad hoc on the phone for `startWorkout`) — queued, delivered once, safe to layer many independent message types on.
- Renaming a message `type` string requires updating both `OutboundPayloads`/`ContextPayloadMapper` (whichever side sends it) and the matching `route()` switch on the other side (`WatchSessionManager.route` on the watch, `WatchConnectivityModule`'s `WatchSessionDelegateHandler.route` on the phone) — nothing else enforces the pairing.
- Medications page: see [apple-watch.md](agent-docs/apple-watch.md#medications-page).
- Double-tap (#83): on watchOS 11 and a supporting watch the gesture presses the set tick through `handGestureShortcut(.primaryAction)`, wrapped in `#available` because the target is watchOS 10. It is enabled only while the Workout page is the selected tab (`workoutPageActive`, set by `ContentView`; a page-style `TabView` keeps neighbouring pages alive) and while the set is not yet logged, so it cannot log a set from another page, and a sheet (keypad, exercise picker) takes the gesture first. Settings → Apple Watch → "Double-tap to log a set" (`watchDoubleTapEnabled`, on by default) turns it off: `useWatchCheckInBridge` sends it as `doubleTapEnabled` in the context, the watch carries it forward in `WatchContext.doubleTapEnabled`, and `effectiveDoubleTapEnabled` gates the gesture. While a set's stopwatch is running (`HoldStopwatch`) the gesture belongs to its Stop button and the tick's shortcut is off, so a double-tap stops the timer instead of logging the hold; once stopped, the next double-tap logs the set.
- Workout sync windows: the server replaces a source's workouts for every local day an upload touches (`preCleanEntriesBySourceAndDate`), keeping only the `source_id`s re-sent. So the `exerciseSession` read starts at the local day start (`windows.aggregatedStart`) in `healthSyncEngine.ts`, never at a mid-day `sessionStart` (the background cursor minus a 6h overlap), or an afternoon background sync would delete that morning's workouts.
- Workout tracking: see [apple-watch.md](agent-docs/apple-watch.md#workout-tracking).
- Starting from the watch: the context carries `startableWorkouts` and `workoutServerId` (the phone's active server). A tap sends both back as `workoutStartRequested`. The phone ignores the request when `serverId` is missing or is no longer the active server, so a tap queued across an account switch cannot start another account's preset that happens to share the id. The listener stays registered while the server is offline and runs the tap once the connection returns. A second delivery while that preset's session is already live is ignored. The context also carries `scheduledWorkouts` (`scheduledWorkoutsForWatch` in `utils/workoutPlanSchedule.ts`, built in `useWatchCheckInBridge` from `useActiveWorkoutPlans` and the day's logged sessions, with the same completion rule as the diary's plan banner, which shares `getLoggedAssignmentIds` / `getUncompletedActivePlans`): the workouts the active plans put on today, each with a plan name and caption (translated by the phone) and a `presetId`. The watch's idle workout page lists them first, in a blue row, and drops them from the saved list below; only ones also in `startableWorkouts` show, since a tap starts them like any other preset. Assignments without a saved workout (a single exercise) are not sent.
- Editing the running workout from the watch: the set screen scrolls (snapping a screen at a time) into Set options (Add Set, Set Type, Delete Set), Exercise options (Add Exercise, Delete Exercise) and Workout (Add Exercise, Finish, Discard); the exercise list also has Add Exercise. Each is a `workoutEdit` message (`addSet`, `deleteSet`, `setSetType`, `addExercise`, `deleteExercise`) with a `clientId`, queued like a logged set. `useWatchWorkoutEdit` applies it to the live session when the session id matches and ignores a repeated `clientId`; `useWatchPlanSync` already sends the changed plan back, and the watch's `updatePlan` keeps its place, so the watch shows the change a moment after the tap rather than editing locally. Add Exercise lists `suggestedExercises` from the context (`suggestedExercisesForWatch`: recent, then most used, capped at 20); the phone loads the exercise by id. Replace Exercise is not on the watch yet.
- Updating the saved workout after a finish: the "Update preset?" check only asks about structure (`structureOnly` in `buildPresetUpdateExercises`: exercises, order, supersets, set count and type, not weights or reps). On the watch, tapping Finish on a workout that started from a saved one (`fromPreset` on the plan) and changed (`WorkoutSessionStore.changedFromPreset`: exercises or sets differ from the plan it started with) asks "Update Workout?" first, as Hevy does, and sends `presetUpdateAnswer` (queued, `onPresetUpdateAnswer`) before it ends the workout. A watch finish holds the finished workout in `pendingPresetUpdateStore`; `PendingPresetUpdatePrompt` (mounted in `App.tsx`) records the answer by watch session id and applies it through `useWorkoutCompletePresetSync` once the phone's own check agrees the preset needs updating. The completion screen leaves the check to it (`presetCheckHandledElsewhere`). With no answer (the watch had nothing to ask), the phone's own prompt waits for the app to be active and asks as before.
- Discarding on the phone: `clearWorkout({ discarded: true })` (the HUD's discard of a live-start with no logged sets, a failed save's Discard anyway, and deleting the live workout; a finish that saved is not a discard) marks the session via `consumeWorkoutDiscarded`; `useWatchWorkoutBridge` then calls `stopWorkout(id, at, true)`, which sends `discarded: true` to the watch. The watch ends the HealthKit session with `discard()` (nothing written to Apple Health), shows no post-workout summary, and sends nothing back. A plain finish still saves to Health and shows the summary, and a finish already running on the watch cannot be taken back. A discard from the watch carries that arm's armedAt; one queued from an earlier arm of the same session does not clear the new one.
- PR celebration (#85): the phone decides what a record is (`activeWorkoutStore.prSetIds`); `useWatchSetTargetsSync` adds `prSetIds` (logged sets only) to every `setTargets` update. The watch's `WorkoutSessionStore.applyTargets` celebrates only ids it logged itself (`wristLoggedSetIds`), once each (`celebratedPrSetIds`), one banner at a time: a yellow "New PR!" banner over the active workout for ~4s (tap to dismiss) and a `.notification` haptic via `onPersonalRecord`, gated on the phone's haptics switch. Sets logged on the phone or carried in by a resumed session do not celebrate. An older phone build sends no `prSetIds`, so nothing fires.
- Post-workout summary (#84): `finishCollection` calls `WorkoutSessionStore.makeSummary()` just before `reset()` (only when no queued plan follows and at least one set was logged) and stores the result as `lastSummary`. It holds duration, sets, volume (Σ weight×reps of the values frozen when each set was logged, not a later target), avg/max heart rate (each measurement second once: live samples, the buffer still held at stop, and the final drain; the running totals survive a relaunch in the snapshot) and active energy. `WorkoutView` shows `WorkoutSummaryView` while no workout is active and a summary exists, until the wearer taps Done or a new plan is armed. A discard resets without one. It is in-memory only.
- Bodyweight (#89): `buildWatchWorkoutStartPayload` sends `bodyweight` on each planned exercise (`isBodyweightModality(resolveSnapshotModality(...))`), and `useWatchPlanSync`'s structure key includes it. The watch keeps it on `PlannedExercise.bodyweight` (optional, so old snapshots decode) and `WorkoutSessionStore.isBodyweight(_:)` reads it: the weight box shows `BW +10` / `BW −20` / `BW`, and the keypad gets a ± key (`allowsNegative`). The signed kg value is sent with the set unchanged.
- Workout heart rate on the phone: see [apple-watch.md](agent-docs/apple-watch.md#workout-heart-rate-on-the-phone).
- Following a saved route: see [apple-watch.md](agent-docs/apple-watch.md#following-a-saved-route).
- Routes kept on the watch (iOS only): `RouteDetailScreen` (shown only while `useWatchConnectivity().isPaired`) has **Start on watch** (Run or Walk, `startRunOnWatch` with the route) and **Send to watch** / **Remove from watch**. `services/watchRouteSync.ts` sends `routeSync` (`buildRouteSync`: the `toWatchRoutePayload` route plus `updatedAt` in epoch ms as its revision) and `routeRemove` through `WatchConnectivity.sendRoute` / `removeRoute`, both queued `transferUserInfo`; `removeRoute` in `routeService.ts` also drops the watch copy, best effort. The watch holds at most `WatchRouteLibrary.capacity` (10) routes in `watch-route-library.json` (`WatchRouteLibraryStorage`), refuses a new route when full instead of evicting one, ignores a copy not newer than the stored revision, and holds a removal for the route being followed until the run ends. After every change and on session activation it answers with `routeLibrary` (stored ids and revisions, rejected ids with `full`/`storage`, capacity), which `useWatchRouteBridge` feeds to `stores/watchRouteStore.ts` (persisted device-local record, never synced to the server); `watchRouteStatus` (`utils/watchRouteSync.ts`) turns the record into `none`/`pending`/`onWatch`/`outdated`/`full`, and an edited route is never re-sent on its own. The wrist lists them under **Routes** in the Cardio sheet (`CardioChoicesView`), outdoor only. The Record screen offers a route picker only for an outdoor watch run. Renaming `routeSync`, `routeRemove` or `routeLibrary` means updating `ContextPayloadMapper`/`OutboundPayloads` and `WatchSessionManager.route`.

- Workout effort: see [apple-watch.md](agent-docs/apple-watch.md#workout-effort).

## Shared Workspace Contracts

- `@workspace/shared` lives at `../shared/` and is source-first in this workspace.
- Prefer shared schemas and constants for API request/response contracts, exercise/workout types, precision constants, calorie constants, and timezone utilities.
- Keep `YYYY-MM-DD` values as calendar-day strings until a database or external API boundary requires UTC instants.
- For day-string logic, prefer shared timezone helpers such as `isDayString`, `addDays`, `compareDays`, `localDateToDay`, `todayInZone`, `instantToDay`, `dayToUtcRange`, and `dayRangeToUtcRange`.
- Mobile API contract changes usually require matching server and often web checks. Food photo, shared schemas, nutrition, meal copy, and auth changes are common cross-package surfaces.
- **Library Deletes & Cache Invalidation:** Library mutations for exercises (`useExerciseMutations.ts`) and foods (`useFoodMutations.ts`) must invalidate all dependent caches: library search, count, details, workout presets, and daily diary summaries (`dailySummaryRootQueryKey`).
- **Snapshot Preservation & Preset Seeding:** `mode: 'delete'` preserves logged workouts/meals using snapshots (`exercise_id` / `food_id` set to `null`). When creating/saving a preset from a logged session (`useWorkoutPresetForm.ts`), entries with null `exercise_id` are automatically dropped while valid exercises carry over. Empty presets are guarded against starting or logging.

## Server API Orientation

All endpoints require auth headers, and proxy headers are injected before auth headers when configured. Key mobile clients:

- `healthDataApi.ts` - `POST /api/health-data`, identity checks, chunking, timeout, retry, session-expiry handling.
- `dailySummaryApi.ts`, `goalsApi.ts`, `measurementsApi.ts`, `preferencesApi.ts` - daily summary, goals, check-ins, water, timezone bootstrap, nutrient display preferences.
- `checkInPhotosApi.ts` - progress photos: the gallery (every photo with that day's weight, in one request), a day's photos, the days that have any, multipart upload and delete. Image bytes come from the authenticated `/file/{id}` route, so `useCheckInPhotoSource` attaches auth and proxy headers and memoizes each source by photo id. It is a thin wrapper over `useAuthedImageSource`, the shared hook behind every authenticated image (see also `usePregnancyPhotoSource` for bump photos); that hook also refuses to build a source over plaintext HTTP outside `__DEV__`, so the session token never goes out in clear.
- `foodEntriesApi.ts`, `foodEntryMealsApi.ts`, `foodsApi.ts`, `mealsApi.ts`, `mealTypesApi.ts`, `mealPlansApi.ts` - diary food entries, grouped logged meals, saved foods/variants/barcodes, saved meals, meal types, and recurring meal plans.
- `externalFoodSearchApi.ts`, `aiSettingsApi.ts`, `aiConversionApi.ts` - provider-agnostic food search/details/barcode, label/photo estimate, AI availability, unit conversion.
- `exerciseApi.ts`, `externalExerciseSearchApi.ts`, `workoutPresetsApi.ts` - exercise history, suggested/search/import flows, preset/individual exercise sessions, workout presets.
- `fastingApi.ts` - `POST /api/fasting/start`, `POST /api/fasting/end`, current/stats/history/range reads, and `GET/PUT /api/fasting/preferences`.
- `authService.ts`, `profileApi.ts`, `externalProvidersApi.ts`, `customNutrientsApi.ts` - auth/session/MFA, profile, configured providers, custom nutrient definitions.
- `ChatScreen.tsx` (transport) + `chatApi.ts` - streaming chat via `POST /api/chat/stream`, history load/clear.

When reviewing an API issue, trace screen/hook -> API client -> server route -> service/repository -> shared schema before judging the fix. Deeper endpoint docs live in mobile `docs/` (`food_api.md`, `sync_api.md`, `measurements_api.md`, `external_providers.md`, `healthkit.md`, `bg_sync.md`).

## Localization And Reactive Helpers

- React UI gets `t` from `useTranslation()`; user-facing utility helpers accept an injected `TFunction` and never hide singleton `i18n.t()` fallbacks.
- Pass `t` through every presentation helper and include it in `useMemo` / `useCallback` dependencies when the derived result contains localized text; this keeps mounted UI correct after a runtime language switch.
- Translation keys are semantic and statically analyzable. Every static `defaultValue` is the English source fallback and must exactly match the EN catalog entry.
- A key used with `count` is a plural family: EN requires `_one` and `_other`; PL requires `_one`, `_few`, `_many`, and `_other`. Use grammatically correct forms rather than duplicating suffixes blindly.
- Apple Watch Swift text (`targets/watch`, `targets/watch-widget`) must use `String(localized: "watch.<key>", defaultValue: ...)` with the key in that target's `en.lproj/Localizable.strings` (each target is its own bundle; `%@`/`%lld` for interpolations, and a static `defaultValue` must match the English entry). `pnpm run i18n:audit:watch` (tested by `__tests__/scripts/watchI18nAudit.test.ts`) fails on bare `Text("...")`/`Button("...")`/`Label("...")`-style literals, missing catalog keys, and mismatched defaults; add `// i18n-ignore` for literals that must stay.
- Run `pnpm run i18n:audit` after localization work. `pnpm run validate` includes typecheck, lint with zero warnings, and this audit.
- Keep canonical storage/API values and user-generated content literal; localize only application-owned presentation labels.

## Testing Guidance

- Tests live in `__tests__/` with `jest-expo`, `jsdom`, and `jest.setup.js`.
- Run related tests for the touched surface, then lint/typecheck for cross-cutting changes.
- Run the full single-run suite after broad refactors, shared mock changes, navigation rewiring, root provider changes, import-path moves, native config changes, public type changes, or global mock edits.
- Be careful with global mocks in `jest.setup.js`; mock pollution can fail unrelated files.
- On macOS, Jest resolves `.ios.ts` by default. Android-specific service tests should require the Android file explicitly:

```ts
const androidService = require('../../src/services/healthConnectService.ts');
```

- Health sync changes: rerun `useSyncHealthData`, `backgroundSyncService`, `healthDataApi`, `healthConnectService`, `healthConnectService.ios`, and relevant `services/healthconnect` / `services/healthkit` tests.
- Health writeback changes: rerun `healthconnect/writeback`, `healthkit/writeback`, writeback mapper tests, `HealthDataWriteback`, `backgroundSyncService`, notifications where relevant, and sync tests.
- Food library/form/unit/barcode changes: rerun `FoodForm`, `FoodUnitSelectorSheet`, `LibraryScreen`, `FoodDetailScreen`, `FoodFormScreen`, `EditBarcodeScreen`, `useFoodsLibrary`, `useFoodVariants`, `useDeleteFood`, `foodsApi`, `foodDetails`, and unit conversion tests.
- Meal template/logged-meal changes: rerun meals library/detail/add/edit screens, `MealTypeDetailScreen`, copy meal tests, food search/entry picker tests, meal hooks/API tests, and meal builder/nutrition utils.
- Exercise/workout/preset changes: rerun exercise/preset library/detail/form/search/mutation tests, workout/activity form and draft tests, active workout store tests, rest-period tests, and `workoutSession` tests.
- Fasting changes: rerun `FastingCard`, `FastingGoalReconciler`, `FastingDetailScreen`, `useFasting`, `useFastingTimer`, `fastingApi`, notification tests, and fasting utility/constant tests.
- Diary quick-adjust/delete changes: rerun swipe row, serving adjustment, food entry update/delete, meal-type detail, and exercise mutation tests.
- Food scan/photo changes: rerun food scan, food photo flow screens, AI settings/external food APIs, food photo intro, food photo utils, and haptics tests.
- Settings/auth/networking changes: rerun onboarding, server settings, server config modal, auth hooks/services, storage, API client, raw fetch client tests, and proxy-header tests.
- Widgets/HUD/tab/add-sheet changes: rerun `useWidgetSync`, active workout store, `AddSheet`, `CustomTabBar`, `ActiveWorkoutBar`, and error-boundary tests.

## Quick Routing

- Treadmill / stride bug: `TreadmillSettingsScreen` (Settings > Workout > Treadmill & stride: sections in order: your stride (learned/hand-set/reset), treadmill display (the correction switch and percent), Measure (stride from a known distance, or the treadmill's error from steps times the stored stride via `treadmillCheckFromSteps`, which never changes the stride), and what the watch has learned), `utils/treadmillCorrection.ts` (`correctTreadmillDistance` divides a display figure by `1 + percent / 100`; `correctWatchRunDistance` corrects a wrist-typed watch figure once and records `treadmillDisplayMeters`), `utils/stride.ts` (`strideFromSample`, `treadmillCheckFromSteps`, `strideToDisplay`), `services/strideCalibration.ts` (`getStrideProfile`, `setStride`, `resetStride`; AsyncStorage on this device only), `services/stepDistance.ts`, `RecordActivityScreen` (corrects only a typed indoor distance), `RunSummaryScreen` (the Treadmill distance card sends and teaches the corrected figure), and `services/watchRunQueue.ts` (`enqueueWatchRun` applies the correction at arrival so a retry never reads the setting again). The correction is device-only like the stride: it describes one machine and the stride depends on this phone's step counter, and the stored distance is already the corrected one, so no server field is needed.
- Health sync bug: start at `healthConnectService.ts` or `.ios.ts`, then `services/healthconnect/` or `services/healthkit/`, `backgroundSyncService.ts`, `autoSyncCoordinator.ts`, `useSyncHealthData.ts`, `SyncScreen.tsx`, and `healthDataApi.ts`.
- Health writeback bug: inspect `HealthDataWriteback`, `services/writeback.ts` / `.ios.ts`, platform writeback modules, mapper files, tracking storage, app permissions, and inbound source filters.
- Food library/edit bug: inspect `LibraryScreen`, food library/detail/form/barcode screens, `FoodForm`, unit selector, food hooks, `foodsApi`, food unit types, and `foodDetails.ts`.
- Meal bug: inspect meals library/detail/add/edit screens, meal plan list/form screens, `MealTypeDetailScreen`, food picker routes, meal and meal-plan hooks/APIs, selection service, logged-meal API, and meal nutrition/form utils.
- Family diary bug: inspect `FamilyMembersScreen`, `FamilyDiaryScreen`, `FamilyMealDetailScreen`, `FamilyCopyReviewScreen`, family hooks/API, navigation params, and the server family/food-entry routes and models. The own `MealTypeDetailScreen` copy menu opens `FamilyMembers` with `copySource` to choose an eligible recipient. That mode opts into `useFamilyUsers({ refetchOnFocus: true })` and uses `useFamilyCopySource` for raw food-entry components, never the own diary's collapsed meal rows. Cached recipients stay visible during background refreshes. `FamilyCopyReview` defaults to copying from family; `direction: 'to-family'` switches the explicit target, uses the recipient's visible meal types (`familyMealTypes` cache), and selects its fingerprint through the shared `copyFingerprintFor` helper. Outbound success refreshes only the recipient's `familyDailySummary` and opens `FamilyDiary` at its optional `date`; it never changes the own diary date or global profile context.
- Heart rate report (`HeartRateReportScreen`): sections `heartRate.overview | insights | restingHrChart | rangeTimeline | dayTimeline | zones`. The range card (`HeartRateRangeCard`, daily averages) and day card (`HeartRateDayTimelineCard`, 24 h line with sleep and workout shading, arrows and a tap on the range chart pick the day) read raw samples through `hooks/useHeartRateTimelines.ts` (`/api/generic-health/samples`, same key as the cardio detail). The day card reads the day before and after too and keeps readings by device-local day, and its arrows stop at the report window; both cards still show when the window has no resting heart rate. The timeline maths (5-minute buckets, sleep dip, daily averages, sleep window) lives in `shared/src/utils/heartRateTimeline.ts` and is used only by mobile today; the web report shares just the resting HR trend delta (`restingHeartRateTrend.ts`). Device-clock helpers are in `utils/heartRateDayTimeline.ts`.
- Heart rate zones: see [quick-routing.md](agent-docs/quick-routing.md#heart-rate-zones).
- Exercise/preset bug: inspect library/detail/form/search screens, related hooks/API, selected-exercise handoff, rest-period controls, and workout session helpers.
- Workout/activity/HUD bug: inspect `AddSheet`, workout/activity screens, workout form hooks, `workoutDraftService`, `activeWorkoutStore`, `ActiveWorkoutBar`, rest notifications, and detail screen set interactions.
- Medication adherence, checklist, month calendar or dose-log bug: inspect `MedicationAdherenceScreen`, `components/medications/` (`AdherenceOverviewCard`, `MedicationLogCalendar`, `MedicationDayLog`), `useMedicationAdherence`, `useLogDose` in `useMedications.ts`, `invalidateMedicationEntryCaches`, and the shared maths in `../shared/src/medications/adherence.ts` (tests in `SparkyFitnessServer/tests/medicationAdherence.test.ts`). The same rules drive the web page (`SparkyFitnessFrontend/src/pages/Medications/`).
- Fasting bug: inspect `FastingDetailScreen`, `FastingCard`, `FastingGoalReconciler`, `useFasting`, `useFastingTimer`, `fastingApi`, `notifications`, and card visibility preferences.
- Measurements/hydration bug: inspect dashboard/diary/measurements screens, summaries/gauges, measurement/water/check-in hooks, API, date helpers, widget sync, writeback, and unit conversions.
- Scan/photo bug: inspect food scan/search, `FoodPhotoFlow`, photo screens, AI setting hook/API, estimate hook/API, intro persistence, haptics, icon usage, and route params.
- Custom widget bug: inspect `targets/widget/customWidget.swift` (the intent, enums and renderer), `services/widgetMetricSnapshot.ts`, `useWidgetSync` (numbers), `useWidgetExtras` and `useCustomWidgetActive` (extras gated on a placed widget) and the contract `../shared/src/utils/widgetLayout.ts`.
- Widget/deep-link bug: inspect `useWidgetSync`, `CalorieWidgetBridge`, widget targets, widget plugins, `app.config.ts`, `app.identifiers.js`, `App.tsx`, and dashboard.
- Widget string shows as a raw key: inspect `targets/widget/en.lproj/Localizable.strings`, the `fallbackWidgetString` map in `SharedHelpers.swift`, and the derived-key contract in `__tests__/config/helpers/widgetSwiftKeys.ts`.
- Chat/voice settings bug: inspect `ChatScreen`, `ChatSettingsScreen`, `components/chat/`, `useVoicePreferencesRefresh`, `aiSettingsApi`, `preferencesApi`, and `voiceTranscriptionApi`; settings and contracts also have web/server consumers.
- Shoe inventory bug: inspect `EquipmentScreen` (Library > Equipment, the hub a new kind of gear adds a row to), `ShoesScreen` / `ShoeFormScreen`, `components/ShoePickerCard.tsx` (the picker on `CardioSessionScreen` and, for cardio entries, `ActivityDetailScreen`), `hooks/useShoes.ts`, `services/api/shoesApi.ts`, and `assignDefaultShoe` in `services/gpsRecordingSave.ts` (a newly created recorded run, outdoor or indoor, is linked to the default pair, best effort and only on first create). When a run takes a pair to its retire-at distance, `services/shoeWear.ts` (`findShoeThatReachedLimit`) reports it: `saveRecordedActivity` returns it as `wornOutShoe` and `ShoePickerCard` checks after a pick, and both ask through `utils/retireShoePrompt.ts`. Distances are stored in km and edited in the app's distance unit; mileage is derived on the server from the linked entries, so every assignment invalidates `shoesQueryKey` and the cardio session caches.
- Fitness test bug: inspect `FitnessTestScreen`, `FitnessTestResultScreen`, `RecordActivityScreen` (the `fitnessTest` param and `handleSave`), `utils/fitnessTest.ts`, `services/fitnessTestService.ts`, `components/recording/Fitness*.tsx`, and the shared `fitnessTest.ts` plus the server's `fitnessTestRepository.ts`.
- Offline logging bug (entry lost, logged twice, stuck in the queue, banner wrong): inspect `services/offlineQueue/` (start with `queueLogic.ts` for classification and `queueRunner.ts` for the drain), `stores/offlineQueueStore.ts`, `hooks/useOfflineQueueSync.ts` (triggers), `useOfflineOverlay` / `overlay.ts` (what the screens show), `OfflineQueueBanner`, `OfflineQueueScreen`, `queryCachePersistence.ts`, and the server `client_id` handling in `SparkyFitnessServer/models/foodEntry.ts` and `services/measurementService.ts`.
- Settings/diagnostics bug: inspect settings screens, `SettingsRow`, haptics/theme/sounds/notification services, diagnostics services, `DevTools`, and screen error boundaries.
- Visual redesign or new screen from a mockup: start at `DESIGN.md` (tokens, type roles, component styling, and the mockup-to-React-Native mapping), then `global.css` and the shared primitives listed under Styling And UI.
- Calorie cycling (#2327): `components/CalorieCyclingCard.tsx` on `CalorieSettingsScreen` (Off / Training / rest / Custom, saved on each change like the rest of the screen) over `components/CalorieCyclingChart.tsx`, which draws the week from shared `cycleWeekTargets` using today's `summary.calorieCycling` base target and floor. A tap on a bar only selects the day; in Custom the grab handle on top of a bar drags it (relative to the start, a selection haptic per 1%, the bars follow a local draft and the value saves once on release), so scrolling the screen from the chart still works, and accessibility increment/decrement nudge it; in Training / rest tapping a day under the chart switches it. The selected day gets a ±1% stepper with a typed value (or the Training/Rest switch and boost) and a day card. Fills are `emerald-500` / `amber-400` like the web; the change text uses `text-success` / `text-warning`. Every displayed goal already comes from the server; `DiaryCalorieMacroSummary` adds a High/Low day label from `summary.calorieCycling`, `GoalsScreen` calls the calories the base while it is on, and `FoodEntryAddScreen`'s "% of goal" reads the adjusted day goal (`goalsRange` with adjust). Widgets and the watch show the server's cycled numbers without a label.
- Calorie goal mode: `CalorieSettingsScreen` keeps existing percentage modes and offers `weekly`; `WeeklyWeightGoalFields` edits direction plus kg/g/lb or stone + pounds per week, initially using the profile weight unit, saved in `goal_mode_weekly_change_kg` (negative loss, positive gain, zero maintenance). The shared weekly-rate helpers format/parse inputs without changing stored kilograms on unit-only switches, and `getGoalModeDailyAdjustment` supplies the estimated daily calorie offset. Actual targets and safety-floor limits come from the server; preference changes invalidate all daily summaries and goal caches.

## Priority Rule

- For work inside `SparkyFitnessMobile/`, this file is the package guide.
- If a task also changes another package, combine this with that package guide instead of stretching this file to cover the whole monorepo.
- Timed sets without a planned length (#99): the start payload marks every set of a duration exercise `timed: true`. With a `targetDurationSec` the watch counts down (`HoldCountdown`); with none it shows a stopwatch (`HoldStopwatch`, `WorkoutSessionStore.startStopwatch`) that starts on tap, freezes when the set is ticked and logs the elapsed seconds.
- Timer sync for timed sets: both sides share one start time (epoch ms). The phone's newest running `setTimerStartedAt` is sent in every `setTargets` update as `setTimers` (one entry at most, for a set not yet logged, because the wrist holds one timer); the watch (`WorkoutSessionStore.applyPhoneTimers`) starts its countdown or stopwatch from that time, never replacing one it started itself, and stops a copied one if the phone drops it without logging the set. A timer started on the wrist is sent as `setTimerStarted` (`onSetTimerStarted` → `applyWatchSetTimerStart`, ignored for another session, a logged set or a start over 3h old) and starts the phone's stopwatch from the same time; a countdown that already ran out does not buzz. A set logged on the wrist clears the phone's stopwatch after its reported duration is written. Last session's time for a timed set rides along as `previousDurationSec` (in the plan and in every `setTargets` update) and the idle watch stopwatch shows it in gray as "Last m:ss"; it is never a target. A timer started on the wrist is kept until it is logged even when the phone times a different set; logging another set leaves a synchronized timer for a different set running; the running timer is stored in the workout snapshot so a relaunch resumes it; a `setTargets` update that arrives before its plan keeps its `setTimers`; and `setTimerStarted` carries `armedAt` so the phone ignores a queued start from an earlier arm of the same session.

- Timed-set sync both ways: the wrist's Stop button sends `setTimerStopped` (`seconds` and that run's `startedAt`); the phone drops its stopwatch and writes the seconds only when `startedAt` is the timer it has (`applyWatchSetTimerStop`), so a stop queued across a later run is ignored. Start and stop ignores are logged to the app log. Only a duration typed on the phone makes the watch count down; the gray "last time" value never does, so a timed set starts at 0 like the phone.
- Watch RPE (#87): Settings → Apple Watch → "Ask for effort (RPE) after each set" (`watchRpeEnabled`, default off) rides the watch context as `rpeEnabled`. When on, logging a set on the watch holds it behind an effort screen laid out like Hevy's: the exercise and set (`Set 1/3: 65.0lbs × 12`) over one large value box that the Digital Crown changes through 1 to 6 in whole numbers, then 6.5, 7, 7.5, 8, 8.5, 9, 9.5 and 10, an "RPE" label, a line saying how many more reps were left in the tank for that value, and Skip and Save buttons. Those labels and descriptions use `watch.rpe.*` keys in `targets/watch/en.lproj/Localizable.strings`, with the English text as the `defaultValue`. The crown stays quiet when the phone's haptics switch is off. Save sends `rpe` with `setCompleted`; Skip sends the set without one. The screen is an overlay on the workout page and not a sheet, because a sheet's system close button covered the exercise name on a 40 mm watch; the set waits there until Save or Skip. The held completion (set, logged values, the tick time, a timed set's seconds at that tick, and the Save or Skip choice) is written into the workout snapshot in `completeCurrentSet(holdForEffort:)` and cleared only after an activated Watch Connectivity session accepts the send. A choice made before activation is kept and sent when the session activates, including after a relaunch. The phone writes `rpe` through `updateSetField` only when it is within 1–10. The overlay state lives in `ActiveWorkoutView`, not `CurrentSetView`, because logging a set starts the rest and swaps the set view out.
- Exercise History layout: `ExerciseHistoryList` shows one card per session — the workout's name (or the date for a single entry) with the date and start time beneath, and a chevron that opens the workout when `ExerciseDetailScreen` passes `onOpenSession` — over a table of SET, a value column headed for the exercise's modality, RPE and a record marker. Working sets are numbered among themselves; warm-up, drop and failure sets show W, D and F (`setTypeLetter`). A set's RPE is a pill in the effort tones, a record is a filled trophy and a tie of the best set an outlined one.
- Effort (RPE) in the app: the exercise History tab opens with `ExerciseEffortCard` (built by `buildExerciseEffortSummary` in `utils/exerciseEffort.ts` from the loaded history sessions): the last session's average RPE, bars for up to the last ten, and for plain weight × reps exercises the best estimated 1RM with and without counting reps left (`effortAdjustedOneRepMaxKg` in `@workspace/shared`: reps + (10 − RPE) for an RPE of 6 to 10, otherwise plain Epley). Warm-ups never count. Personal records and best sets still use reps alone. In adaptive coaching (`decideAdaptiveAdjustment`) a logged average RPE of 7 or lower, or 3+ reps in reserve, with no difficulty answer adds one step (`low_effort`), the mirror of the existing hold at RPE 9.5+.
